RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
January 9, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne,
Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer,
and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
ELECTION OF CITY COUNCIL PRESIDENT FOR 2012.  Mayor Kaysen noted for the
record that, pursuant to City Code, he would be conducting the elections for City Council President
and Vice-President but would not be voting.   Mayor Kaysen stated nominations for election of the
office of Council President was in order.  Mr. Valdez moved to nominate and elect Mr. Collins as
City Council President for 2012, s econded by Mr. Pierson. Mr. B rown moved tha t nominations
cease, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion to cease nominations carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.  Mr. Collins advised
he is looking forward to serving as Council President for 2012 and noted there are many projects to
address during the upcoming year.  Motion to nominate and elect Mr. Collins carried.  Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.  
ELECTION OF CITY COUNCIL VICE-PRESIDENT FOR 2012.  Mayor Kaysen stated
nominations for election of the office of Council Vice-President was in order.  Mr. Collins moved
to n ominate a nd e lect Mr . Pi erson a s Ci ty Cou ncil V ice-President f or 20 12, s econded b y Ms.
Broyles.  Mr. Collins moved that nominations cease, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion to cease
nominations carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor
Kaysen who did not vote.  Mr. Pierson advised he considered it an honor to serve as a part of Council
leadership.  Motion to nominate and elect Mr. Pierson carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.
On behalf of the governing body, Mayor Kaysen recognized outgoing 2011 Council President
Dr. Rinne, and presented him with an engraved gavel plaque.  He also expressed appreciation to Mr.
Brown for serving as Council Vice-President for 2011.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Collins and Mr. Brown, agenda items #15 and #18 were removed from the Consent Agenda.
Upon request by Mr. Pierson, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Collins  and Ms. Ash, agenda
item #22(a) was re moved from the Consent Ag enda.  Mr . Allen moved to appr ove remaining
Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on December 27, 2011.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez each declared a conflict of interest (business interest in vouchers
payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services and Radio Station KYOY F M respectively) and vacated
Council Chambers.  Mr. Spiker moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by
Mr. Allen.  Upon inquiry regarding a voucher payable to Ayres Associates for Lake Minnehaha
remediation, Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, provided an update on the project, which
involves DEQ grant funding.  Upon inquiry, Lois Huff, City Treasurer, reviewed a voucher payable
to A shley F urniture ($ 1,175.00) f or fu rniture fo r Y outh A lternatives Sp ecial F riends p rogram.
Comments followed whether this was an allowable expenditure under the CCLC Cohort grant used
to purchase the furniture.  Ms. Ash moved to divide the question to allow for a separate vote on the
voucher payable to Ashley Furniture, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Huff advised the
furniture ha s mo st l ikely a lready be en d elivered s ince the  vo ucher w as o n th e pa yables li st.
Comments followed regarding grant stipulations.  Dr. Rinne raised a point of order with regard to
the motion to divi de the que stion being non- debatable with Mayor Kaysen concurring that the
motion was non-debatable.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
present in Council Chambers with the exception of Ms. Broyles and Mr. Pierson voting “no”.  Dr.
Rinne moved to approve payment of the voucher to Ashley Furniture, seconded by Mr. Collins.
Discussion followed regarding payment of the voucher and whether another funding source could
be utilized if it were ascertained that the furniture purchase did not qualify under grant requirements.
Ms. Huff clarified another funding source could be used to pay for the furniture and she could adjust
the payment source through a fund transfer.  Further comments followed regarding grant compliance.
Motion to approve payment of the voucher to Ashley Furniture carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Upon inquiry regarding a voucher payable to
Americoach of Cheyenne Charters LLC for chartering 2 buses for a Youth Alternatives activity,
which also utilized Cohort grant funding, Ms. Ash advised she ove rsees this grant as part of  her
employment d uties and stated it is an a llowable expense unde r the g rant.  Motion to approve
remaining vouchers for payment carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present
in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez returned
to Council Chambers.
Ordinance annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being situated in
a portion of the Northeast Quarter (NE1/4) of Section 1, Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the
6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (generally located north of and adjacent to West Lincolnway
and east of and adja cent to the B NSF Railroad).  Reporting for Public Service s Committee, Mr.
Collins moved to approve  on third and final r eading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carr ied.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#3941)
Ordinance amending Chapter 5.08, Business License Fees, Section 5.08.010, Amusements,
of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Cheyenne City Code pertaining to the license
fee for conducting a theater business.  Re porting for Finance Committee, Mr. Valdez moved to
approve on third and final  reading, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body. (#3942)
Ordinance creating Section 10.52.240, Parking of Oversized Vehicles, Trailers, and Semi-
trailers in Residential and Mixed Use Districts, Prohibited, of Chapter 10.52, Stopping, Standing and
Parking generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.
Reporting for Committee of the Whole, Dr. Rinne stated there was no recommendation on the item
from the Committee.  As sponsor of the ordinance, Ms. Broyles expressed appreciation to the public,
council and staff who had provided input on the proposed ordinance, indicating she plans to continue
working on the topic to bring forward proposed changes, and requested the ordinance be withdrawn
from the agenda.  Upon query by Mayor Kaysen and with no objections being raised by City Council
members, Mayor Kaysen announced the item was being withdrawn from the agenda.
Ordinance re pealing Ordinanc e No. 3408, as ame nded, which a dopted the “Che yenne-
Laramie County Subdivision/Development Regulations 2000,” and repealing Ordinance No. 3720,
as amended, which a dopted the “2006 Roa d, Street, and Site Planning  Design Standards,” a nd
repealing Title 17, Zo ning, of the Cheyenne City Code 2002, in its entirety , and adopting  the
“Cheyenne Un ified D evelopment Co de,” wh ich is  a ttached h ereto a s E xhibit A  a nd fu lly
incorporated herein by reference.  Donn Edmunds commented on the availability of the document
proposed for approval.  Paul Fr auendienst, member of the committee involved in re viewing and
providing input on the proposed Unified Development Code, spoke in support of the revised code.
He stated the code will provide a new framework to build Cheyenne and that modifications may be
required as plans move forward.  He provided information on an annual review process that will be
in place, stating the Critical Path Committee has some recommendations for future consideration,
and noted committee members who helped review and draft the document.  Mr. Collins moved to
approve on second reading with UDC Amendments A1 through A6, UDC proof- read corrections,
and an ordinance effective date of April 1, 2012, seconded by Ms. Broyles. Comments followed
regarding interpretation of the new code and the desire to be more business friendly, size of signs
posted on property sites advising the public of pending development or redevelopment plans, and
the annual code review process.  Upon inquiry, Brandon Cammarata, Planning and Development
Director, provided information on sign notification requirements posted for public awareness and
stated staff  is resea rching and eva luating the public communication proc ess to provide more
information through electronic means (on-line a ccessibility) on deve lopment projects.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Resolution designating local banks as depositories for municipal funds.  Mr. Collins and Mr.
Spiker ea ch dec lared a c onflict of intere st (board me mber a ffiliation with cer tain financ ial
institutions) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Brown.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.
Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Collins and Mr. Spiker returned to Council Chambers.
(#5343)
Resolution designating the Sundays during 2012 that annual l iquor licensees may extend
dispensing room hours of operation for sales until 2:00 A.M. the following day.  Bryan Grzegorczyk,
President, Laramie County Liquor Association, spoke in support of the  resolution and provided
information on the Association’s close communication with local law enforcement on drinking and
driving preve ntion effor ts, noting the Assoc iation’s taxi  ride for  patrons prog ram a nd the
Association’s various fund raisers and charity work benefitting the community.  Mr. Valdez stated
there was no recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Collins moved to adopt,
seconded by Mr. Brown.  Mr. Grzegorczyk reviewed the dates of the four Sundays in 2012 being
requested, which are Feb. 5 (Super Bowl Sunday), July 22 (first Sunday of Frontier Days), Sept. 2
(Sunday of L abor D ay wee kend) a nd Dec . 16 (holiday  parties)  and stat ed the Assoc iation’s
membership voted on the proposed dates.  Upon inquiry whether there was any data reflecting DUI
offenses for the Sunday hours approved for 2011, Mr. Grzeg orczyk advised he did not have any
specific statistics but t hat overall DUI’s are down by about 33% a nd Brian Kozak, Police Chief,
advised he would provide DUI information for the past year to members of the governing body.
Comment f ollowed in o pposition to t he le gislation a llowing fo r e xtension o f S unday ho urs.
Appreciation was expressed to Mr. Grzegorczyk for his assistance involving a recent tragedy in the
community.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception
of Mr. Pierson and Mr. Spiker voting “no”. (#5344)
 
Resolution authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to execute the a ttached Business Ready
Community Grant and Loan Program Grant Agreement between the Wyoming Business Council and
the City of Cheyenne to assist in making pedestrian and streetscape improvements to  W est
Lincolnway, West 17  Street, Thomes Avenue and Pioneer Avenue adjacent to Block 392, Originalth
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. V aldez moved to adopt, sec onded by Mr. Allen.  Discussion
followed regarding potential funding and grant availability to complete improvements on 17  Street.th
Upon inquiry , Matt Ashby , P lanning Ser vices Dire ctor, a dvised staff a nd the Downtown
Development Authority  (DDA ) ar e wor king tog ether to identify potential source s to fund the
remaining area.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5345)
[CA] Resolution authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to execute a State Historic Preservation
Office Certified Local Government Grant Agreement for a ttendance at the 2012 Saving Places
Conference ( State H istoric Pr eservation O ffice g rant).  M otion ( per Co nsent A genda) c arried.
(#5346)
 
[CA] Resolution authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to execute a State H istoric Preservation
Office Certified Local Government Grant Agreement for historic building analysis and case studies
of historic buildin gs (State Historic Pre servation Office grant).  Motion (pe r Consent Ag enda)
carried. (#5347)
Resolution authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to execute a State Historic Preservation
Office Certified Local Government Grant Agreement for an International Code Council Class on
building codes and historic buildings (State Historic Preservation Office grant).  Mr. Valdez moved
to adopt, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Discussion followed on comments that had been received from
some property owners expressing difficulty with current building code requirements relative to
redevelopment efforts involving historic downtown buildings, and that the codes sometimes are not
conducive to historic building re development projects.  Upon inquiry , Matt Ashby , Planning
Services Director, stated the training, which will be available to the building inspectors and property
owners of historic buildings, can assist in creating a better environment of understanding between
the parties on code pr ovisions to help facilitate redevelopment of historic buildings.  F ollowing
further comment regarding conflicts with codes and redevelopment of historic building efforts,
particularly in the downtown area, motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body. (#5348)
Resolution determining and prioritizing City of Cheyenne projects to be proposed to the 1%
Specific Purpose Sales Tax Committee for potential inclusion on t he 1% Specific Purpose ballot
proposition to be presente d to qualified ele ctors of Lara mie County during the August 21, 2012
primary e lection.  Do nn Ed munds, s peaking on behalf of  a  g roup of  e astern L aramie Cou nty
residents, commented on the proposed Christensen Road Overpass project, noting a petition had been
submitted to WYDOT and expressing concerns with the project specific to the Pershing Blvd. area.
Lynn Simons, speaking on behalf of the Friends of the Botanic Gardens Foundation, provided a brief
review of the age of the current facility and the need for improvements and past history of 6  pennyth
Botanic Gardens funding requests, and noted private donations have assisted toward many site
improvements.  John Palmer questioned the pending projects list, expressing concerns with lack of
the landfill expansion project being included; commented on “needs vs. wants” and stated a concrete
plan nee ds to be deter mined prior to submitti ng the ballot question to voters.  May or Ka ysen
confirmed proposed projects being considered would be addressed pursuant to the report from the
Committee of the Whole.  Reporting for Committee of the Whole, Dr. Rinne moved to  adopt,
seconded by Mr. Collins.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  It was
noted the proposed substitute resolution does not provide for prioritization of any project (i.e.  no
items assigne d a priority  number), a nd cont ains the following  proposed pr ojects and f unding
amounts: L ower C apitol Basin L ife-Safety Pub lic I mprovements ($ 4,800,000.00); L andfill
equipment ( $3,000,000.00); Pu blic Sa fety B uilding ($ 25,000,000.00); Ch ristensen O verpass
($1,500,000.00); Ae rial F ire Tr uck Gr ant Match ($600,000.00) ; Municipal Pool R epair and
Renovation ($5,000,000.00); Gr eenway Construction ($2,800,000.0 0); Bota nic Gar dens
Construction (new conservatory) ($16,000,000.00); Recreation Center ($33,000,000.00), and new
Airport Terminal ($4,831,157.00), for a total amount of $96,531,157.00.  Speaking on the proposed
substitute were: Jo hn Palmer, expressing concern with a new recreation center and supporting
inclusion of a landfill expansion project on the projects listing; Kevin Paintner, referencing past
information that had been provided on a proposed rec center and commenting in support of a rec
center, submitting information (on file with City Clerk’s office) and citing cost recovery comparables
for other communities that have built rec  centers; Donn Edmunds, encour aging consideration of
other projects and total dollar amounts being requested by the county and outlying towns and stating
his belief the resolution is premature at this time; John Volk, speaking in support of the 6  pennyth
ballot process and the importance of compromising and working together with the other county
governmental entities on proposed proje cts; Matt Pope, Rec Center  projec t gr oup, ex pressing
appreciation for the  opportunity to communicate ne eds and w ants to better the c ommunity and
commenting on the cost recovery issue, noting a lot of parks and recreation areas are subsidized, and
Morgan Burke, speaking in support of a rec center, noting the lack of available facilities to practice
youth basketball and benefits overall of a facility for youth activities.  During comments, Mayor
Kaysen and Mr. Collins reviewed the Specific Purpose Committee proce ss and the Committ ee’s
upcoming meeting schedule respectively.  Mayor Kaysen confirmed that another resolution would
be proposed at a future meeting with regard to projects and funding amounts for all of the county
governmental entities involved (i.e. L aramie County, Cheyenne, Albin, Burns, Pine B luffs), and
explained the necessary approval process by the entities for a ballot question(s) to be presented to
voters.  Mr. Brown moved to amend the proposed substitute by removing $1,000,000.00 from the
funding amount for the Botanic Gardens project (reducing from $16,000,000.00 to $15,000,000.00)
and adding the 17  Street Pedestrian Lighting and Beautification project in the funding amount ofth
$1,000,000.00, seconded by Mr. Collins.  M r. Spiker dec lared a conf lict of interest (ow ner of
property located on 17  Street) and vacated Council Chambers.  Pursuant to comment by Barbarath
Stafford, Board member, Friends of the Botanic Gardens, it was verified that the proposed funding
amount, p er the pending amendme nt, for the B otanic Ga rdens Conser vatory projec t would be
$15,000,000.00.  John Sundahl, Board President, Downtown Development Authority, referenced
previous information that had been pr ovided in support  of the 17  Street project, noting he w asth
available to answer any questions.  Comments followed with regard to clarification of the proposed
amendment and lack of prioritization of projects as contained in the proposed substitute.  Mr. Pierson
raised a point of order that comments regarding prioritization were not applicable to the motion on
the floor with Mayor Kaysen ruling comments should be germane to the motion on the floor, which
is the motion to amend as moved by Mr. Brown and seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion to amend the
substitute carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.
Following announ cement of the vote, Mr . Spiker retur ned to Council Chambers.  Discussion
followed and included the following topics: Support as well as non-support for prioritizing City
projects; Specific Purpose Committee meeting schedule; the Committee’s preferred sales tax revenue
collection time frame (approximately 5 years) and total amount to present to voters, including the
estimated amount of $55 to $57 million dollars for City projects; providing an opportunity for voters
to decide which projects should be funded; importance of communication of City supported projects
to Specific Purpose Committee members, and verification that the proposed substitute to be voted
upon was the one recommended at the Committee of the Whole meeting, as amended at the current
meeting.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend the substitute, as previously amended, by removing the term
“non-” befor e the word “pr ioritized” a s ref erenced in the r esolution, seconded by  Mr. Allen.
Speaking on the proposed amendment were: Matt Pope, commenting on the Rec Center committee’s
work over the past months and supporting input from the public on a new rec center, and questioning
how the order of listing of projects in the resolution were developed; Brian Tyrrell, commenting that
every project is a priority  and one should not take  precedence over another; Dave Cook, stating
concern that the issue of prioritization had not previously been fully discussed, and requesting the
governing body not prioritize projects and proposing a menu-style ballot question be submitted to
voters; Christina Mayle, questioning what the priority list would be, and Tom Toft, commenting in
support of the rec center not being culled from the list and affording people the chance to vote on
projects.  Mayor Kaysen advised, if the motion to amend carried, the projects would be prioritized
as follows (re: as listed in the previously approved substitute resolution as amended at the current
meeting): Lower Capitol Basin; Landfill Equipment; Public Safety Building; Christensen Overpass
Planning; Aerial Fir e Truc k Gra nt Match; Municipal Pool Repair and Renova tion; Greenw ay
Construction; Botani c Gardens construc tion of new c onservatory; Recre ation Center; Airpor t
Terminal and 17  Street Pedestrian Lighting and Beautification.  F ollowing a 10 minute recess,th
Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:15 p.m.  Motion to amend by removing the term “non-”
before the word “prioritized” failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body.  Motion to
amend by substitute resolution, as previously amended, carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms.
Broyles, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Ms. Ash, Mr.
Brown, Mr. Collins and Dr. Rinne.  Main motion as amended by substitute, as amended, carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5349)
[CA] Resolution authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for A & M
Subdivision, 2  Filing, a replat of Lot 1, Block One, A & M Subdivision, Laramie C ounty,nd
Wyoming (located east of and adjacent to S. Greeley Highway, south of Prosser Road) -- referred

[CA] Preliminary Plat for Gateway South, 3  Filing, a replat of Lots 1, 2, 4 and 5, Block One,rd

Cooperative Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming Department of
Transportation for a railroad quiet zone project at the 24  St./BNSF Crossing (2007 - 2010 1% Salesth
Tax).  Doug Vetter, City Engineer, clarified this is a cooperative agreement for grant assistance from
the State through WYDOT and the City’s share would be approximately $20,000, funded through
the 1%  street renovations budget line item.  He explained the project would reconfigure the railroad
crossing by placing raised medians in the center of the street as a safety improvement, eliminating
a current potential vehicular crossing hazard.  Mr. V aldez moved to approve , seconded by Mr.
Spiker.  Concern was raised regarding utilization of 1% street renovation money relative to other
necessary road improvements.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
with the exception of Ms. Ash, Mr. Pierson and Dr. Rinne voting “no”. (#5785)
[CA] Contract betw een the City of Cheyenne and the United States Department of Homeland

[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Parks and Recreation Department, and Coca-Cola

[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between  the City of Chey enne and the 37  Helicopterth

referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 5726 between the City of Cheyenne, Parks and

additional fill, crushed rock base and other changes for the East Cheyenne Greenway Phase IIIb
(UPRR Underpass) Project (Greenway 1% Specific Purpose Sales & Use Tax funds).  Motion (per

[CA] Amendment No. 1 to Professional Services Contract No. 5624 between the City of Cheyenne
on behalf of the Cheyenne Metropolitan Planning Organization and AVI Professional Corporation
for the Fox Farm Road Corridor Plan, Phase II (MPO Federal Planning funds).  Motion (per Consent

[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-8-12 for a contract for the Pioneer Park Baseball Lighting project
between the Cit y of Chey enne, Par ks and Recr eation De partment, a nd Superior Elec tric &

[CA] Application for a Microbrewery Permit filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Freedoms Edge

Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Application for a  Microbrewery Permit app lication filed with the City  Clerk's Office for
Freedoms Edg e B rewing 2, L LC d/b/a Fre edoms Edg e B rewing Co., 1509 Pioneer  Avenue ,

Re-Appointment of Paul M. Fr auendienst to the B uilding Code B oard of Appeals.  Mr.
Collins moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --  all members
of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointment).
Announcement was made that due to the Martin Luther King, J r. Holiday on Monday,
January 16, 2012, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held in the
Municipal Building, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Room 122, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance
Committee will meet Tuesday, January 17, 2012 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Services Committee will

John Palmer questioned the 1% (6  penney) Specific Purpose Sales Tax Committee meetingth
place and time to be held on January  16  and whe ther this would be a public me eting.  May orth
Kaysen advised the meeting is scheduled for 5:30 p.m. at the Align Building located on the Airport

approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Ms. Ash moved to amend the effective
date of the Unified Development Code (UDC) from April 3, 2012 to April 30, 2012, seconded by
Mr. Allen.  B randon Cammarata, Planning and De velopment Director, advised the a mendment
addresses questions and concerns raised at the Public Services Committee and explained reasons
why an effective date of April 30, 2012 was being proposed relative to timing issues and procedures
for certain applications (i.e. preliminary and final plats; site plans, etc.) involving current  vs. new
UDC regulations.  Upon inquiry  regarding availability of the UD C document to the public, Mr.
Cammarata stated the document has been available electronically through the City’s website and on
CDs throughout the Council process, and indicated that when the document is finalized incorporating
the amendments (approved at the January 9, 2012 City Council meeting), the final version will be
available electronically on the City’s website and through the Planning and Development Office, and
that hard copies will be available for purchase.  Question was raised regarding the motion on the
floor, wh ich is  a n a mendment t o c hange the  UD C e ffective da te to  April 30 , 2 012, a nd no t a
proposed substitute as reviewed by Mr. Cammarata during his remarks, which includes an effective
date of April 30, 2012.  Mr. Pierso n offered a friendly amendment to the motion on the floor by
clarifying the motion is to amend by substitute dated Jan. 23, 2012, with Ms. Ash, as maker of the
initial motion to amend, accepting the friendly amendment.  No objection to the friendly amendment
was raised by other membe rs of the g overning body.  Upon inquiry , Mr. Cammara ta reviewed
proposed language in the substitute ordinance contained in Section 5 which reads as follows: “This
ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication, but no earlier than April
30, 2012, provided however, that an application for approval of a final plat, if filed after April 30,
2012, shall not be subject to this ordinance if (1) the application is filed within eighteen (18) months
following acknowledgment, by the governing body, of receipt of the related preliminary plat, and (2)
the application requesting that the governing body acknowledge receipt of the related preliminary
plat was filed prior to April 30, 2012.”  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body.  Following announcement of the vote, Mayor Kaysen thanked
members of the community who served on the committee and staff for their time and efforts in
preparing and finalizing the UDC, and noted the UDC has an annual review provision that will
provide for continuing improvements. (#3943)

Wyoming (located east of and adjacent to S. Greeley Highway, south of Prosser Road).  Motion (per

(#5791)
Memorandum of Understanding between the City  of Chey enne and the 37  Helicopterth
Squadron of the United States Air Force for use of the Belvoir Ranch for helicopter training.  James

Mr. Brown moved to a pprove, seconded by Mr. Valde z.  Mr. Brown, se conded by Mr. Pierson,

which may be occurring on the Belvoir Ranch.  The Owner agrees to provide the Government with
maps, grid coordinates or legal descriptions indicating landing areas being impacted by on going

Announcement was made of Appointments of Anne Picot, JoAnne Weigand and Clayton W.
Long to the Mayor’s Council for People with Disabilities, to be confirmed February 13, 2012.
Under Other Business:
City Clerk Carol I ntlekofer advised ther e will no t be a City  Council Publ ic Service s
Committee meeting on Tuesday, February 7, 2012 at Noon, Room 122, Municipal Building, as there
are no pending or referred items for the agenda requiring a meeting.
Major Dorr informed those present that a public meeting would be held Tuesday, Jan. 31,
2012 at 6:30 p.m. at the First Baptist Church regarding the topic of school bullying.
Larry Proietti, local business owner, expressed concerns with local fire inspections and code
requirements involving restaurants, and the resulting cost to businesses to meet current hood and
electrical system upgrade requirements.  Comments followed regarding costs to the businesses which
could make them lose their business.  Upon inquiry , Jon Narva, Division Chief, Fire and Rescue
Department, provided infor mation on the chang e to national fire p rotection rules reg arding
acceptable hood fire extinguishing agents for systems; explained problems being encountered with
older systems relative to the unavailability of contractors who can provide maintenance service for
the systems as manufacturers have moved away from the older system types due to f ire safety
concerns; clarified he is not aware of any national association funding assistance to assist business
owners with replace ment sy stem expenses, and re viewed the  inspection proce ss, including
inspections involving vacant buildings and those owned by non-local persons, and the requirement
of having viable fire extinguishing agents in hood systems.  He stated that most restaurants in town
are up to code and that the department works with affected businesses on time frames in which to
implement an upgraded system.  He discussed jurisdictional enforcement of the International Fire
Code 2006 edition currently in effect, which was adopted by the City of Cheyenne, and noted the
International family of codes are developed to be model codes to meet community needs throughout
the country.  James Perrott Macneil commented that old buildings should be “grandfathered” under
previous codes and replacement of systems shouldn’t be required if it meant putting people out of
business.
Upon inquiry, Jim Elias, Public Works Director, provided information on research staff is
conducting relative to funding, including the possibility of bonding, the landfill expansion project;
explained the different design and construction phases that will be involved, and responded to
inquiry regarding sanitation rates, noting a possible effect on customer rates if funding would be
through issuance of bonds.  He explained staff needs time to explore funding options and evaluate
costs and projections prior to s ubmitting to C ouncil, and stated a future work session with City
Council would be held to present funding options.  He discussed the amount of the City’s current
bonding capacity and advised since the proposed funding of the project through the 6  penny ballotth
process was “off the table”, they need to explore different funding sources.  Upon inquiry regarding
costs currently being incurred to transport solid waste to Alt, CO, Mr. Elias advised the ultimate goal
is to use that funding to help offset costs.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, Mayor Kaysen adjourned
the meeting at 6:47  p.m.
 Submitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
February 6, 2012

MEETING: Special Meeting of the Governing Body | DATE: 2012-02-02 | CLIP_ID: 274 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 274

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
February 2, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in a special meeting on this date beginning
at 8:30 p.m. in Council Chambers for the purpose of acting upon a Resolution repealing Resolution
No. 5349 and de termining and prioritizing City  of Chey enne projects to be propose d to the 1%
Specific Purpose Sales Tax Committee for potential inclusion on the 1% Specific Purpose ballot
proposition to be presented to qualified electors of Laramie County during the August 21, 2012
primary election.  Those present were:  MAYOR: Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS - Mr.
Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez.  Also present were: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White,
City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
A Resolution repe aling Resolution No. 5349 and deter mining and pr ioritizing City  of
Cheyenne projects to be proposed to the 1% Spec ific Purpose Sales Tax Committee for potential
inclusion on the 1% Spec ific Purpose ba llot proposition to be pre sented to qualified e lectors of
Laramie County during the August 21, 2012 primary election – referred to Committee of the Whole
(sponsor - Mr. Collins).  Reporting for Committee of the Whole, Mr. Collins stated the item was
reviewed at a special meeting of the Committee of the Whole on Feb. 2, 2012 beginning at 6:00 p.m.,
and provisions of Title 2 of  City Code had been met.  Mr. Collins moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.
Brown.  Jam es Perrott Macneil s poke in o pposition to t he resolution and ballot proposition in
general; Bebe Molway spoke in favor of allowing voters to decide what projects they wished to fund;
Barbara Laybourn spoke in support of funding for the Greenway being included as a ballot question,
and Lynn Simons, Chair, Cheyenne Botanic Gardens Foundation, thanked members of the governing
body for inclusion of the Botanic Gardens conservatory in the project list.  Rick Parish, Parks and
Recreation Director, encouraged consideration of continuing the funding investment in the Greenway
through inclusion on the City’s project list.  Mr. Collins, seconded by Mr. Pierson, moved to amend
by adding an additional paragraph immediately after the words “Total $57,000,000” as follows:  “It
is further resolved that the 1% Specific Purpose Sales Tax Committee add the Recreation Center to
the 1% Specific Purpose Sales tax ballot proposition as a stand-alone County-wide project, in the
amount of $33,000,000.00, subject to the c ondition that no revenues collected from the 2012 1%
Specific Purpose Sales tax shall be allocated to the Recreation Center until all approved 2012 1%
Specific Purpose projects have been fully funded.”  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of  the g overning body.  For  the re cord, Dr. Rinne stated his conce rns re garding the
Christensen Overpass Planning pr oject, noting the overpass location is partially  in the City  and
partially in the County; potential “ballooning” project costs, and what entity would be ultimately
responsible for the overpass (City vs. County) following completion.  Di scussion included cost
sharing of the Christensen project with the County; and possible alternative funding sources for the
Greenway.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Purpose of the special meeting having been met, Mayor Kaysen adjourned the meeting at
8:50 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 February 9, 2012

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY

Office of City Clerk
February 13, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne,
Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer,
and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Ms. Ash, and pursuant to support received by Dr.
Rinne and Mr. Collins, ag enda item #14 wa s removed from the Consent Ag enda.  Mr . Collins
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.

Consent Agenda) carried.

Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to R adio
Station KYOY) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Allen moved to approve payment of vouchers
as presented, sec onded by Mr. B rown.  Upon inquiry  regarding a vouche r payable to Wy oming
Network, website hosting fee (Belvoir Ranch website), Jim Elias, Public Works Director, responded
the site was implemented by the Planning Department and that de partment pays the hosting f ee.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.
Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Valdez returned to Council Chambers.
Public Hearing to receive input and comments regarding the intent of the City of Cheyenne
to submit  an application for funding  to the Wy oming B usiness Council’s Business Rea dy
Community, Co mmunity En hancement G rant p rogram, t o a ssist i n th e c ompletion o f t he Pol k
Avenue Greenway segment.  Cindy Schneider, Grants Manager, reviewed the 1/4 mile proposed
Greenway path to be located along Polk Ave. between Highway 30 and Pershing Blvd. that will
connect t wo sec tions of the pathway  sy stem for c ontinuity and explained provisions of g rant
application requirements.  She advised the project’s cost is estimated at $500,000 and the grant
request will be for $250,000 with a re quired City match of $250,000, which will be funded from
current 6  penny sales tax.  Teresa Moore, Planning Manager, Parks and Recreation Department,th
displayed a map depicting the proposed pathway segment and, upon inquiry, provided information
regarding potential future pathway extension east of the area.  There being no further comments or
input and purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.

classification from P Public District to HR-2 High Density Residential-Developing District for Lot
1, Block 1, The Pointe, Sixth Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located north of and adjacent to Dorothy
Lane, west of Powderhouse Road) -- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor - Ms. Ash).

Maintenance Facility Addition Project, Phase I.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5353)

Investment Board on behalf of the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne for Mineral Royalty
Capital Construction Account (Chapter 3) funds.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5354)
[CA] Resolution approving an Outside Use r Sanitary Sewer Services Agreement with Ty son
Garrett, owner of Lot 6, Block 1, Lindrom Subdivision, Laramie County, Wyoming.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried. (#5355)
 Resolution declaring as surplus the City-owned property located at 2100 Pioneer Avenue,
Cheyenne, Wyoming, commonly referred to as the City  of Chey enne Board of Public Utili ties
Administration building, together with all improvements thereon, and authorizing the Director of the
Board of Public Utilities and the City’s Purchasing Director to proceed with proposed sale of said
property pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §15-1-112.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved
to adopt, seconde d by Mr. Spiker.  Discussion followed r egarding whether Cheyenne Housing
Authority had expressed an interest in the building; if the property could be transferred to another
City entity, and Board of Public Utilities’ (BOPU) intended use of property sales revenue toward
debt incurred for the new a dministration building being constructed.  Dur ing discussion, Mayor
Kaysen clarified for the record that Tim Wilson, BOPU Executive Director, acknowledged from the
audience that no recent feedback or inquiry from the Housing Authority had been received.  Upon
inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, advised it appears the City does have the authority to make a
donation to the Cheyenne Housing Authority if the governing body so desired, and that the governing
body would have the option of rejecting all bids if it were decided to donate the land after declaring
the property as surplus and a ccepting bids.  F urther comments f ollowed on the intere st in the
property expressed by Mike Stanfield, Cheyenne Housing Authority, relative to construction of a
new Senior Center involving the site as it is near the current Center.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Wilson,
clarified the BOPU has been planning on selling the property and using the funds received to help
pay down the debt on the ne w administration building, and a dvised there has not been a  recent
appraisal of the prope rty, commenting on the estimated va lue of the building several years ago.
Upon further inquiry whether loss of property sales revenue to BOPU could affect water and sewer
users’ rates, Mr. Wilson responded the impact on water rates would not be as significant as an effect
on the sewer enterprise fund.  Following further discussion, Dr. Rinne moved to postpone for  2
weeks (to Fe b. 27, 2012), se conded by  Ms. Ash.  Comments followed reg arding support for
protecting citizens’ water and sewer user rates, and whe ther Cheyenne Housing Authority had
funding available to remodel t he building if it was donated to them.  Upon inquiry , Mr. Wils on
provided information on his past conversation with Mr. Stanfield, noting it had been quite awhile
ago.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.

Consideration of Bid #M-6-12 for a contract for the Lions Park Remediation project between
the City of Cheyenne, Parks and Recreation Department, and Scotch Boy, Inc., Cheyenne, WY.  Mr.
Brown moved to re ject all bids, seconde d by Mr. Spiker.  I t was cla rified that all bids  received
exceeded the engineer’s estimate of $150,000 for the project.  Upon inquiry, Rick Parish, Parks and
Recreation Director, advised available funding is in the amount of $123,000, explaining so me
funding has alre ady been use d for de signers and landscape architects.  May or Kaysen advised
community partners who had expressed an intent in providing funding assistance are still committed
to securing  $40,000 tow ard the project and that $10,000 has be en provide d to the City  from
Cheyenne Light, Fuel and Power. Comments followed regarding the decision to cut down trees in
Lions Park for airport flight safety reasons and the need for the City to remediate the area as the
public expects.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Parish provided information on the scope of work for the project
and elements involving redesign of the scope of work due to consultants’ estimate of rock quantities
needed and the high bids received, resulting in new bid specifications being prepared to advertise
for re-bidding to meet the funding amount available.  He stated the redesign removes rock walkways
and reduces the number of trees to be planted, and reviewed meetings held for public comments
involving the original design, advising they did not go back out for public comment on the redesign
as it had bee n planned to timely  plant the new tr ees dur ing the  upcoming spring  season.  He
expressed support for  moving f orward with re- bidding f or the re designed proje ct base d upon
available funding, stating more trees and amenities could be added in the future when funding may
be available, and noted that often times  initial project designs have to be scaled back due to costs.
Upon quer y, he conf irmed th e lowest bid rec eived wa s over the e ngineer’s estimate by  about
$170,000.  Discussion included the following topics:  Project funding availability; expectation of the
public regarding sc ope of the  orig inal projec t plans; finding money  in the budg et to provide
additional project funding; redesign still including the planting of more trees than the number that
had been removed; removal of rock walkways in the redesign; whether a commitment had been made
to the public regarding the original design; whether revenue from the property sale to Menard’s could
be utilized toward the project, and airport runway flight area and safety concerns.  During discussion
Mr. Parish provided further information on the project, including an explanation that revenue from
the sale of property to Menard’s is planned to be used toward other projects, referencing the girls
softball complex project, Pioneer Par k improvements, re quired AD A compliance needs, a nd
unknown outcome of the pending 6  penny ballot projects.  Mayor Kaysen provided informationth
on the intent of the original funding proposal brought forward by the Cheyenne Airport Board and
community partners, which was from a  remediation standpoint to replace  trees.  He a dvised the
proposal was a 3 year phasing project tied with the dollars that would be provided from the Airport
Board and community partners in a total amount of $175,000 that included work that would be done
for land architectural services and tree and shrub plantings.  He stated the project budget amount of
$175,000 was shared during public meetings and that the focus was to revegetate the area with trees,
which, upon inquiry, he advised would not grow to a height that would affect airport landing safety
standards, and the possibility of an irrigation system. He shared that he had attended a meeting earlier
in the day with the community partners who advised they are working toward securing the $40,000
as previously committed, and that this amount was included in the budgeted amount of $175,000,
and confirmed $123,000 is the re maining amount of pro ject funds. Mayor Kaysen provided a n
explanation of why there has been few amenities in this portion of Lions Parks due to safety reasons
and regulations involving aircraft traveling over the area.  Further discussion followed regarding
project funding; support for as well as against the pending motion to reject all bids; removal of rock
formations/walkways in the redesign; funding shortages affecting needs in other parks; request for
Parks and Recreation prioritization list of department projects; public input received on the original
design vs. redesign of the scope of work for the project and what may constitute a commitment to
the public, and project phasing.  M otion carried.  Vo ting “yes” -- Mr.  Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms.
Broyles, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Ms. Ash, Mr. Collins
and Mr. Pierson.
[CA] Consideration of B id #M-9-12 for  a contr act for the Powers Field Storag e Shed proje ct
between the City of Cheyenne, Parks and Recreation Department, and Construction Specialists, Inc.,
Cheyenne, WY.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $39,950.00). (#5800)
   
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-22-12 for furnishing 3 SUV's for the Fire and Rescue and Planning
Departments from Sheridan Motor, Sheridan, WY (2007 - 2010 1% Sales Tax and General Fund).
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $89,575.75).
[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-19-12 for a contract for the Prairie View Golf Course and Okie
Blanchard Sanitary Sewer Main project be tween the City of Cheyenne, Parks a nd Recre ation
Department, and Merrill, Inc. of Cheyenne, WY -- referred to Finance Committee.
Appointments of Anne Picot, JoAnne Weigand and Clayton W. Long to the Mayor’s Council
for People w ith Disabilities.  Mr. Collin s  moved to approve , seconded by Mr. B rown.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” --  all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen
who did not vote (Mayoral appointments).
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 27, 2012 at 6:00 p.m. in
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for renewal of existing City liquor
licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, ba r and grill licenses, microbr ewery and winer y
permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.  
            Announcement was made that due to the Presidents’ Holiday on Monday, February 20, 2012,
regularly schedule d City  Council Comm ittee meeting s will inst ead be  held in the Municipal
Building, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Room 122, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance Committee
will meet Tuesday , February 21, 2012 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Service s Committee will meet
Wednesday, February 22, 2012 at Noon. 
Following query under other business by Mayor Kaysen to the public and City Coun cil
members and there being no other business, City Attorney Dan White requested the governing body
meet in executive session for the purpose of providing governing body members with an update
regarding the status of an eminent domain action involving Hollywood Videos property; an appeal
to the Wyoming Supreme Court involving the Roundup Heights litigation, and potential acquisition
of real property under circumstances where there is a reasonable likelihood that publicity regarding
proposed acquisition might affect the purchasing price.  Mr. Collins m oved to meet in executive
session, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body.  Mayor Kaysen announced members would meet in executive session in Council Chambers,
not open to the public; requested the City Attorney, City Clerk, and other staff as requested by the
City Attorney, be present at the executive session, and stated there would be no action taken by the
governing body as a result of the executive session, and that the regular City Council meeting would
officially conclude at the close of the executive session.  (Clerk’s Note: Executive session convened
at 7:03: p.m. following a brief recess.)
The meeting was officially adjourned at 8:00 p.m. following closure of the executive session.
      
 Submitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2012-02-27 | CLIP_ID: 279 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 279

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
February 27, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne,
Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer,
and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Collins, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Pierson and Ms. Ash, agenda items #9 and 11 were removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr.
Collins moved to approve  remaining Consent Ag enda items, sec onded by Mr. B rown.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 13, 2012. Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez each declared a conflict of interest (business interest in vouchers
payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services and Radio Station KYOY F M respectively) and vacated
Council Chambers.  Mr. Allen moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by
Mr. Pierson.  Upon inquiry regarding a voucher payable to the Clerk of District Court, City Attorney
Dan White advised that in conjunction with the conde mnation filing for  the Holly wood Video
property, the court filing process requires the amount of the Ci ty’s last offer for the property be
submitted with filing  of the c omplaint.  Moti on carri ed.  Voting “yes” - - all member s of the
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown and
Mr. Valdez returned to Council Chambers.
Public Hearing for renewal of existing City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail,
resort, bar and grill licenses, microbrewery and winery permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, advi sed of 3 re staurant liquor lice nsees who did not file r enewal
applications; noted those licenses proposed for renewal that are currently in a “parked” status, and
explained there are renewal applications for 2 retail licensees who were initially approved, with
conditions, for licenses at the July 25, 2011 Council meeting, stating the licenses have not yet been
issued.  She reviewed the conditions for each of the 2 retail licensees (re: One East Sixteenth, LLC
and Let ‘Er Buck Investment Group, LLC d/b/a Steamboat’s); time frame for the conditions to be
met (i.e. no later than July 25, 2012), and offered a suggestion that consideration be given to extend
the time frame for conditions to be met to coincide with the end of the upcoming annual licensing
term as approval of renewal applications will be for the term of April 1, 2012 through March 31,
2013.  In response to inquiry, James Yates, speaking on behalf of Let ‘er Buck Investment Group,
LLC, advised they are pursuing renewal of the liquor license for The Commons area, but due to
property acquisition issues, they  may be applying at a future  date to cha nge the location for the
restaurant operation.  Upon inquiry and during discussion, Officer Steve Neisen, Cheyenne Police
Dept., reviewed information he had compiled for the department’s 2011 annual c ompliance and
licensing inspection report involving undercover observer operations, TIPS compliance by licensees,
number of ca lls for servic e, and c ompliance c hecks involving  sales to under age per sons.  He
explained the depar tment is supporting a community alcohol educ ation program involving such
issues as “over service” and underage drinking.  Discussion included concerns with sales to and
consumption by underage persons and increase in police calls for service for certain establishments.
There being no further comments or input and purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor
Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the applications to Finance Committee.
Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Z oning Districts Established, and
Section 17.08.040, Cheyenne City Map, of the Cheyenne City Code, by changing t he z oning
classification from P Public District to HR-2 High Density Residential-Developing District for Lot
1, Block 1, The Pointe, Sixth Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located north of and adjacent to Dorothy
Lane, west of Powderhouse Road).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash moved to
approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body.
 Resolution declaring as surplus the City-owned property located at 2100 Pioneer Avenue,
Cheyenne, Wyoming, commonly referred to as the City  of Chey enne Board of Public Utili ties
Administration building, together with all improvements thereon, and authorizing the Director of the
Board of Public Utilities and the City’s Purchasing Director to proceed with proposed sale of said
property pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §15-1-112.  John Ridge, Assistant City Attorney, advised pursuant
to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-112(b) the City can sell the building directly to a political subdivision, of which
Cheyenne Housing Authority is considered, and explained a proposed substitute resolution includes
provisions involving sale of the property to the Housing Authority.  He advised if the Housing
Authority does not  meet the time frame in which to submit a qualified proposa l to purchase the
property, the substitute resolution then allows the City  to offer the property for s ale through the
public bidding process, noting proceeds from the sale will be utilized to offset the Board of Public
Utilities’ debt as required by statute.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to adopt,
seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mr. Brown moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon
inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, advised it is the belief of the City’s legal staff that the City has
a statutory obligation to sell the property at the appraised value and that Cheyenne Housing Authority
will be given the “first shot” to purchase the property at the appraised value.  He further advised if
the Housing Authority chooses to not exercise the option, then the property will be offered for sale
pursuant to a publicly sealed bid process and Cheyenne Housing Authority could choose to submit
a sealed bid in a lesser amount, the same as any other interested bidder.  He reiterated proceeds from
the sale will be used to pay down BOPU’s debt as provided by statute.  Upon query, Mr. White
clarified the 30 day time frame for the Housing Authority to submit their proposal begins from the
date the property appraisal information is rec eived by the Housing Authority.  Motion to amend
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended by substitute
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5356)
Resolution increasing the level of funding placed into the Solid Waste Management fund for
the specific purpose of providing funding to pay for increased professional services costs and solid
waste tipping fees, increased recycling costs, and increased costs for the lease/purchase of solid
waste equipment.  M r. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Mr. Brown moved to
amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  It was advised the substitute allows for a clerical
correction to amounts listed for the Vehicle and Equipment Replacement fund.  Jim Elias, Public
Works Director, e xplained fur ther staf f re view of th e amount reque sted for the  Vehicle  and
Equipment Replacement fund was conducted following the Finance Committee meeting, resulting
in another proposed substitute resolution being prepared, and requested approval of that substitute,
which reduces the requested amount for that fund from $133,500.00 ($1,602,000.00 annually) to
$116,833.00 ($1,401,996.00 annually).  He indicated the lesser amount proposed is due to delaying
the schedule for acquisition of vehicle and equipment leases/purchases for 6 months.  Motion to
amend failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body.  Mr. Brown moved to amend by
substitute (attorney stamp date of February 24, 2012) to incorporate the changes as presented by Mr.
Elias, seconded by Mr. Pierson. Upon inquiry, Mr. Elias provided further information on the reduced
amount being proposed and estimated savings, and noted that delaying the leasing of some of the
vehicles and equipment until calendar year 2013 will defer payments until fiscal year 2014.  Upon
inquiry, Lois Huff, City Treasurer, advised that in fiscal year 2014 two other lease payments will
expire.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Upon inquiry
during discussion, Mr. Elias reviewed recycling costs associated with operating the various recycling
programs (curbside, e-waste, composting, household hazardous waste); cost of tipping fees relative
to not having a recycling program; challenges involved with collecting recyclables on a weekly basis
and effect on solid waste pickup schedules; expansion of recycling programs to multi-family units
as well as commercial establishments, and volume of household solid waste and effect of overall
community g rowth.  Discussion topics included sug gestion of a solid waste e nterprise fund;
recycling effects; recycling collection time frames, and recycling opportunities for multi-family
dwellings and commercial establishments.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5357)
Resolution re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2012 budget of the City of Cheyenne according
to the provisions of W.S . 16-4-112 &  16-4-113.  Mr. B rown moved to adopt, seconde d by Mr.
Pierson. Following brief co mments, Mr. Collins m oved to amend by  removing the amount of
$350,000.00 proposed f or a ne w phone system, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Discussion followed
regarding the need for a new phone system due to the system’s age and limited maintenance service
and e quipment r eplacement a vailability; ti me fr ame to a cquire a nd imp lement a  ne w s ystem;
unknown status of fiscal year 2013 budget revenues affiliated with anticipated state funding to be
received; concern with use of reserves for this type of purchase; suggestion of funding review for
inclusion in next year’s budget, and number of City-issued cell phones.  During discussion and upon
inquiry, Bob Bradshaw, Special Projects Director, provided information on the status of the current
phone system, explaining the limited serviceability of the system, which is approximately 20 years
old; outdated version of the system’s software, and the inability to replace failed individual user
phones as only refurbished phones (limited amount) are available.  He advised the requested funding
amount was for the first phase  of the re placement system involving the Municipa l Building and
perhaps another outside area, and would not cover  replacement of all phones in the sy stem.  He
clarified there is no backup to the phone system if it were to fail, noting funding for a new system
has been requested for quite a few years. During comment on City-issued cell phones, Mayor Kaysen
advised some employees utilize their own personal cell phones and not City ones.  Motion to amend
by removing the amount of $350,000.00 for a replacement phone system carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Brown and Mayor Kaysen voting “no”.
Upon inquiry, Jim Elias, Public Works Director, reviewed fuel cost increases affecting the Fleet
Maintenance budget; inventoried and non-inventoried parts; Fleet Maintenance staffing level, and
outsourcing work for repairs that can not be done in-house.  Discussion followed on funding in the
amount of $65,000.00 for an employee salary and staffing analysis study, and timing of the study
affiliated with curre nt economic c oncerns and unknown fisc al year 2013 budget situation, and
potential effect on level of staffing.  Mr. Pierson moved to amend by removing the amount of
$65,000.00 proposed for the employee salary and staffing analysis study, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Rich Wiederspahn, Human Resources Director, provided information on the benefits of the proposed
study, which has been contemplated since 2009, and expressed concerns with retaining and recruiting
qualified employees due to competitive salarie s offered by other e mployers.  Upon inquiry  he
clarified components of the study would be conducted as a package over an estimated five month
period, and indicated the study needs to be  a comprehensive regional study to be ef fective.  Mr.
Wiederspahn advised a salary survey that was recently conducted which focused on law enforcement
personnel resulted in salaries being increased to attract qualified police officers. Comments followed
regarding a similar study, referred to as the Anderson Study, that was conducted 20 years ago and
the inability to implement increases per that study due to l ack of funding.  Cl aus Johnson, City
employee and former Cheyenne Public Employees Association (CPEA) President, spoke in support
of the study and the difficulty the City has in retaining quality employees.  He stated the study would
include review of e mployee benefits and a  determination of appropriate job classifica tions and
whether the City is properly staffed.  He noted increasing costs to employees due to “frozen salaries”
and additional employee financial participation toward health and retirement expenses over the years.
Discussion included the following topics: Different levels of service between similar cities; job
classifications; employ ee c ompensation adjust ments and funding  conce rns; benef its of an
independent s tudy, a nd he alth ins urance sa vings (due  to less c laims su bmitted b y e mployees)
covering the cost of the study.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Pierson and Mr. Spiker.
Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez
and Mayor Kaysen. Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen provided a brief overview of his understanding of
the biennial budget status involving capital construction and direct distribution revenue amounts
affecting municipalities that is currently  before the state  legislature.  Comment of conc ern was
expressed relative to balancing the budget.  Mr. Spiker moved to postpone for two weeks (to March
12, 2012), seconded by Mr. Collins.  Discussion included state legislative budget decisions affecting
the upcoming  budg et y ear and not the cur rent budg et y ear; suggestion that re- appropriation
resolutions be submitted on the ag enda on a monthly instead of qua rterly basis; current status of
reserves; administrative Blue Form Committee process utilized for hiring employees or adjusting
salaries; two positions included for funding in the proposed re-appropriation, and support for as well
as ag ainst postponement.  During  discussion L ois Huff , City Treasurer, advised if the r e-
appropriation is approved the reserve level will be approximately $5.8 million, and Mayor Kaysen
provided information on the two employee salaries contained in the re-appropriation and explained
the review and approval process for hiring of employees through the Blue Form Committee.  Rick
Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, and Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, reviewed their
justification for the two e mployee positions (one in each department) included in the r e-
appropriation.  Mayor Kaysen reviewed the status of the amount in reserves, explaining that due to
efforts of employees this amount was grown to $7.1 million at the close of June 30, 2011.  Motion
to postpone failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez. Voting “no” -- Mr.
Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.  Upon inquiry,
Ms. Huff reviewed the amount contained in the re-appropriation relating to Depot Plaza events and
explained this is a cumulative negative balance that has been carried forward for a number of years
that she is try ing to ba lance out so they  can sta rt fr esh for 2012, and Mr. B radshaw stated a n
accounting change is being made regarding the tracking of expenditures and reviewed Depot events
funding partnerships and provisions involving vendors at events.  Comments followed regarding the
Blue Form Committee, its formation a number of years ago, individuals comprising the committee,
including the Chair of Finance Committee, and possibility of monthly re-appropriation requests
being placed on the agenda.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes”-- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash,
Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen. Voting “no” -- Mr.
Pierson and Mr. Spiker. (#5358)
Resolution authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for Country
Homes Subdivision, 3rd Filing, a replat of Lot 4, Block 5, Country Homes Subdivision, Laramie
County, Wyoming (located east of Cedar Avenue, between Grape and Peach Streets).  Brett Walker,
Development office, referenced a memo (on file with City Clerk’s office) dated February 24, 2012
issued to City Council members indicating continuing staff support for recommendation #1, which
would provide for a 26 ft. dedicated right-of-way to provide a pedestrian and vehicular connection
and not a road reservation, as suggested by the developer.  Bruce Perryman, AVI Engineering, spoke
in opposition to the staff recommendation and in support of a road reservation due to public access
concerns involving a right-of-way.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, advised if a right-of-
way is p latted and recorded, it b ecomes right-of-way dedicated to p ublic use, and expressed his
hesitancy in offering an opinion at the meeting on the locking/un-locking of a gate, affiliated with
the topic of public access, without having the opportunity to research.  Mr. Perryman submitted maps
(on file with City Clerk’s office) of the property area and discussed concerns with proximity of the
proposed right-of-way area to a n existing structure on the lot , public access to area relative to
residences and a gated area, and setback requirements.  He stated the developer’s preference is for
a road reservation, instead of the 26 f t. dedicated right-of-way, which would reserve the strip for
future road access and keep the property ownership private, and offered the option of a private access
easement.  He noted past development examples involving road reservations.  Comments followed
regarding  connectivity and right-of-way for future use, and staff continuing to discuss the issue with
the developer to hopefully reach an agreement.  Mr. Collins raised a procedural point of order with
Mayor Kaysen advising there was no motion on the floor.   John Volk discussed a past subdivision
development (re: Mustang Ridge) relative to road reservation vs. right-of-way access issues.  Mr.
Walker advised the  2nd plat filing f or Country  Homes Subdivision was approved w ith two
hammerheads in the layout in such a way that if certain lots did sell at a future date it would allow
connectivity and fulfill the patterns of the blocks.  James Perrott Macneil commented on provisions
he was aware of for property located on the east coast that allowed access to remain private.  Ms.
Ash moved to adopt, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Comments followed regarding future connectivity,
public vs. private access and effect on landowners, road reservation vs. dedicated right-of-way, and
possibility of a r oad re servation affiliated with a me morandum o f understanding with the
landowner(s).  Upon inquiry, Mr. White advised, pursuant to h is research, he could not find a ny
understanding or explanation of a road reservation as a term utilized in Wyoming Statutes.  Mr .
Collins moved to postpone for two weeks (to March 12, 2012), seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Comments
followed in support of postponement to allow staff and landowner(s) an opportunity to discuss the
matter further to protect the interests of both parties.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body. 
Following a brief recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 9:07 p.m.
Resolution authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a  Final Plat for WYODI GS
Properties Subdivision, 2nd Filing, a r eplat of Lots 2 and 4, Block 4, Campstool Addition, Third
Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located between Trucker Trail and Industry Court, west of the KOA
Campground).  Ms. Ash moved to adopt with staff recommendation #1, seconded by Ms. Broyles.
Comments followed reg arding conce rns of the de veloper wit h John Volk, Campstool L and
Company, advising that drainage issues will be approved by the City Engineer prior to the plat being
signed.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5359)
[CA] Resolution authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for Prairie
Dog Alley Subdivision, a replat of the north half of Tract 38, Dell Range Addition, Third Filing,
Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to Summit Drive, east of Braehill Road).
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5360)
Resolution a uthorizing the  Ma yor a nd the  City  Cle rk to s ign a  F inal Pla t f or Riv ett
Subdivision, a portion of the NE1/4 of Section 1, T.13N., R.67W., 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming
(located north of and adjacent to West Lincolnway, east of Westland Road).  Ms. Ash moved to
adopt, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Ms. Ash moved to ame nd to inclu de staff recommendation #1,
seconded by  M r. Collins.  Upon inqui ry, Br ett Walker, De velopment offic e, advise d staff
recommendation #1 is to record required easements on the lot adjacent to the east before signatures
are affixed to the map.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  Discussion topics included: Accessibility concerns to
certain portions of the land; lack of right-of-way being included for roads for future access; unknown
factors involving future area development; MPO corridor study; area property owners’ involvement
in future g rowth relative to roads an d City criteria, and the pr oposed hotel deve lopment on the
property.  Upon inquiry , Br andon Cammara ta, Planning a nd Deve lopment Direc tor, provided
information on the preliminary plat review process, conceptual land use plan for the area affiliated
with the Missile Drive corridor plan, and the need for a road plan.  He clarified the MPO did delve
into what was the best approach for a road network for the area, and advised marketability factors
involving development or non-development of the area may affect road development.  John Steil,
Steil Surveying Services, agent for applicant, stated if they put streets in this would set the pattern
for any further development and they are not sure what will be developed at this time.  Upon inquiry,
Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, advised of the general purpose of subdivision regulations
and that placing a condition for right-of-way may be premature.  Main motion as amended carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Ash and Dr . Rinne
voting “no”. (#5361)
Resolution granting with conditions an exception for deve lopment of a small waste water
system pursuant t o th e “ 201 A greement” fo r p roperty de scribed a s a  tr act of  la nd in
N1/2N1/2NE1/4NE1/4 of Section 11, Township 13 North, Range 67 West, of the 6th P.M. (a/k/a
4121 West 5th Street), Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming.  Ray Martin, attorney representing
Profile Properties, LLC, applicant, questioned the conditions affiliated with the exception request.
Jim Flesher, Planning and Development office, reviewed the conditions which includes annexation
of the property.  Mr. Martin spoke in opposition to the annexation condition, advising the applicant
does not desire to annex all of the property at this time due to potential acquisition of the property
by WYDOT for future re-design and reconstruction of the I-25/I-80 interchange, and referenced a
lease that had bee n entered into with Dy na Nobel.  He sugges ted a condition be placed on the
property that if the owner sells the property or develops any of the property in the future, at that time
it should be annexed.  Discussion followed on the 201 exception and conditions recommended by
staff and City-County Health Department, Environmental Health Division.  During comments, Mr.
Flesher p rovided r easons fo r s taff’s re commendations a nd the  wa stewater ho lding ta nk tha t is
proposed as a compromise, and Gary Hickman, Environmental Health, clarified the holding tank
could not be held in perpetuity as septic systems are not allowed within City limits.  James Perrott
Macneil spoke in opposition to the annexation condition.  Ms. Ash moved to adopt.  Motion died
due to lack of a second to the motion.
[CA] Resolution granting an exception for development of a small wastewater system pursuant to
the “201 Agreement” for property described as a portion of Tract 1, Lincoln Valley Tracts, Section
36, Township 14 North, Range 66 West, of the 6th P.M. (a/k/a 6509 Tate Road), Cheyenne, Laramie

Alternatives, Special Friends, and Wyoming Department of Family Services, Protective Services
Division, to provide Outreach Mentoring Program services.  Ms. Ash declared a conflict of interest
(employment conflict) and vacated Council Chambers.  Dr. Dick Berry, Youth Alternatives Director,
advised of a potential update to the contract and expressed support for a two week postponement.
Mr. Brown moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Brown move d to postpone for two
weeks (to Marc h 12, 2012), sec onded by Mr. Valde z.  Upon inquiry, Dr. B erry explained the
postponement will not affe ct the g rant, and Dan White, City  Attorney, clarified the purpose  of
postponement is to address concerns with contract language.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement
of the vote, Ms. Ash returned to Council Chambers.

Finance Committee.
