Works Department, and E/S3 Consultants, Inc. for Energy Project Consulting Services (Energy
Development fund).  Motion (per  Consent Ag enda) carried (amount not to ex ceed $25,000.00).
(#5776)
[CA] Contract Modification No. 2 to Contract No. 5377 between the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation
Division, and Alpine Waste and Recycling for sorting, processing and marketing of single stream
(recyclable) materials.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5377)
Amendment No. 1 to Contract No. 5775, Professional Services Agreement between the City
of Cheyenne, Police Department, and DLR Group, Inc., for the Cheyenne Public Safety Center site
analysis -- referred to Finance Committee.  Mr. Brown reported the item was reviewed under other
items for the agenda at Finance Committee and provisions of Title 2, City Code, had been met.  Mr.
Brown moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $34,770.00, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Chief
Brian Kozak, Police Dep artment, provided infor mation on service s previously provided by  the
company regarding a space needs analysis, which assisted with the determination that a new public
safety center was needed, and recommended site locations for the new facility.  He stated the scope
of services to be provided through the amendment will allow DLR Group to provide an architectural
design concept and analysis, which will include square  footage and f loor plans; detailed budg et
information, and an exterior view of the building.  He noted the information will be provided to the
public for their  knowledge relative to the upcoming  6  penny ballot proposition.  J ohn Palmerth
expressed concern that the building project was being placed on the ballot without previously having
acquired the infor mation that is requested throug h the amendme nt, and s uggested, if the ba llot
question passes, waiting and using 6  penny funds to pay for the services to receive the information.th
Upon inquiry on whether money that may be expended now f or the additional services could be
reimbursed at a later date from 6th penny funds if the ballot question is approved, Dan White, City
Attorney, expressed his hesitancy  in offer ing an opi nion at the meeting  without having the
opportunity to research state statutes.  Discussion topics included: How the estimated cost for the
new Public Safety Center was determined; services under the initial contract; need for specific
information to share with voters; suggestion the contractor be approached to inquire whether some
services might be donated; concern with future potential benefit to the contractor relative to other
professional services for the project, and comments in support as well as non-support of expending
money for the a dditional services prior to the ballot question being approved by voters.  Motion
failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr.
Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Spiker.
[CA] Contract Modifica tion No. 2 to Cont ract No. 5496 bet ween the City  of Chey enne,
Engineering Department, and Ayres Associates for additional design services for the East Pershing
Boulevard reconstruction project (Phase IV) (2003-2006, 2007-2010 1% Sales Tax, Board of Public
Utilities, Federal Highway Administration funds) -- referred to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-19-12 for a contract for the Prairie View Golf Course and Okie
Blanchard S anitary Sewer  Main projec t betwee n the City  of Chey enne, Par ks and Recr eation
Department, and Merrill, Inc. of Chey enne, Wyoming.  Motion (per  Consent Ag enda) carried.
(#5805)
[CA] Consideration of B id #R-25-12 for f urnishing one new r oll-off truck for the Sanitation
Division from Peterbilt of Wyoming, Cheyenne, Wyoming -- referred to Finance Committee.
Announcement was made  of the a ppointment of J ames W. Hea rne, Tuc ker Fagan and
William C. Beaman, to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming-Laramie County, State of Wyoming Joint
Powers Board for Frontier Park improvements to be confirmed March 12, 2012.
Under other business, Rick Parish, Director of Parks and Recreation, reminded those present
of a  City Cou ncil w ork s ession on  AD A c ompliance ma tters inv olving t he de partment t hat is
scheduled for February 29, 2012 at noon.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, Mayor Kaysen adjourned
the meeting at 10:07 p.m.
      
 Submitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk

Central Ave.  City Clerk Carol I ntlekofer offered a suggestion that in lieu of rea ding the list of

recorded in its entirety in the official minutes of the meeting together with any motions and votes.

before the governing body has been conducted.  She further stated that the City Attorney has advised
that this statement o n the record provides sufficient doc umentation to identify  those individual
renewal applications that may be acted upon at the meeting.  U pon query by Mayor Kaysen to
members of the governing body, there was no objection voiced to recognize the renewal applicants
as listed on the ag enda in lieu of  the Clerk re ading the individual applicants, and Ma yor Kaysen

owner, Two Bar Bowl, advised he was available to answer any questions.  Mr. Brown moved to

continuation of the conditions for these applicants.  Mr. Brown moved to divide the question to allow
for separate action on the liquor renewal application filed by One East Sixteenth, LLC, seconded by
Mr. Allen.  Motion to divide the question carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body present.  Mr. Brown moved to approve the renewal application filed by One East Sixteenth,
LLC  with the condition that a lease agreement with a restaurant establishment to operate within the
building must be signed no later than July 25, 2012 or the license will revert to the City, seconded
by Mr. Allen.  It was noted the license was initially approved July 25, 2011 with the same condition
and the motion on the floor confirms the condition remains in p lace.  M s. Intlekofer clarified if
renewal of the license is approved, the license term will be the same as other annual licenses, which
term is from April 1  through March 31  each year, and if the licensee does not comply with thest st
condition by the July 25  date, the City may need to pursue legal procedures available to revoke theth
license.  Motion ca rried.  V oting “yes” -- all members of the g overning body present with the
exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  Mr. Brown moved to divide the question to allow for separate
action o n th e liq uor r enewal a pplication f iled b y L et ‘ Er Bu ck Investment G roup, L LC d/ b/a
Steamboat’s, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion to divide the question carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present.  Mr. Brown moved to approve the renewal application filed
by Let ‘Er Buck Investment Group, LLC d/b/a Steamboat’s with the condition that construction of
the establishment must begin no later than July 25, 2012 or the license will revert to the City,
seconded by Mr. Spiker. James Yates, Let’Er Buck Investment Group, advised he was present to
answer any questions and stated they are planning on fulfilling the July deadline condition.  Pursuant
to inquiry conce rning use of the te rm “c onstruction” vs. “r emodeling”, Matt Ashby , Planning
Services Director, expressed his belief that if a condition is for a building to be “under construction”
or “remodeled” that there is no difference in terminology as far as both would require a building
permit.  Discussion included the following topics:  Concerns with regard to the initial issuance of
the license, which wa s for a  specific restaurant theme pla n within a newly developed area (The
Commons) in n orth Cheyenne; parking and property acquisition issues involving the i nitial site
within The Commons development; possible future change in location (to Dell Range Blvd.) for the
proposed restaurant affiliated with us e of the  license, a nd utilizing retail liquor licenses f or the
purposes of economic development.  Mr. Yates provided information on Steamboat’s project status,
advising they are close to entering into a lease agreement for a different site; reviewed his history
as a business/restaurant owner in Wyoming, specifically Cheyenne, and being fiscally responsible
to shareholders involved in the projec t, and monetar y investment by  shareholders to date, and
explained the business plan has re mained the sa me.  S helly Clements, Let ‘Er Buck Investment
Group shareholder, provided information relative to parking issues at the initially proposed business
lots located at The Commons, which has resulted in the group looking for an alternative location.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of
Ms. Ash and Dr. Rinne voting “no.”  Ms. Broyles moved to divide the question to allow for separate
action on the liquor renewal application filed by Two Bar Bowl, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Motion
to divide the question carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the
exception of Ms. Ash and Mr. Va ldez voting “no”.  Ms. Broy les moved to approve the renewal
application for Two Bar Bowl, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Co mment of concern was expressed
regarding underage drinking at t he establishment.  Robert L anghoff, Two Bar Bowl, provided
information re lating to thei r efforts to reduce  and eliminate potential unde rage drinking
opportunities, advising they have diligently worked with the Police Department and have passed all
compliance checks and TIPS certifications, and have taken responsible steps to enforce policies.  Ms.
Ash stated that due to child issues she would be excusing herself from attendance at the remainder
of the City Council meeting (City Clerk note: Ms Ash vacated Council Chambers at 6:55 p.m.).
Upon inquiry, Officer Steve Neisen, Cheyenne Police Department, explained he has worked closely
with staff at Two Bar Bowl on ways to reduce and eliminate drinking under age (DUA) availability
within the establishment’s open concourse area, and provided information on enforcement strategies
and the number and types of police calls for service.  Mr. Langhoff explained his concerns with
enforcement and communication, and sug gested more timely communication to all licensee s be
provided by the Police Department regarding the number of  calls for  service affiliated with the
department’s annual report submitted during the renewal process, and particularly prior to the annual
public hear ing.  D iscussion followed a nd included informa tion from Offic er N eisen re garding
security, enforcement, the department’s new community alcohol educational program, and the unique
environment involving the bowling  alley as b oth an adult and y outh recreational area.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Ms. Broyles moved to divide
the question to allow for separate action on the liquor renewal application filed by Stillwater, Inc.
d/b/a Mingles, seconded Mr. Pierson.  Motion to divide the question carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr.
Allen, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Spiker.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown, Mr. Valdez
and Mayor Kaysen.  Ms. Broyles moved to approve the renewal application for Stillwater, Inc. d/b/a
Mingles, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Owen Groshart, Mingles representative, stated he was available
to answer any questions.  Upon inquiry, Officer Neisen reviewed the number and types of incidents
that had occurred based on c alls for ser vice, per the de partment’s annual report, and e xplained
operational changes that have or will be occurring and training that will be provided to servers by
the Police Department regarding “over service” situations.  M otion carried.  Vo ting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present.  Ms. Broyles moved to divide the question to allow for
separate action on the liquor renewal application filed by W.W. Enterprises, Inc. d/b/a Les’ Lamp
Lounge, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Motion to divide the question failed (due to a tie vote).  Voting
“yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson and Mr. Spiker. Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown, Dr. Rinne,
Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Discussion followed on the main motion, excluding those renewal
licenses already acted upon.  Officer Neisen responded to inquiries regarding underage drinking
concerns at various locations within the community, specifically a recent incident at Romero Park;
confirmed the Lamp Lounge has a very positive system for tracking of incidents; addressed training
and changed practices and policie s for many liquor licensee s (i.e. Red L obster, Pizza Hut/Wing
Street (east location) and Crown Bar) based upon the department’s report information and inquiry
by City Council members during this year’s renewal process.  Dave McGee, provider of security
services for Lamp Lounge, reported on incident tracking and security operations at the Lamp, and
stated they are working closely with the Police Department.  Comments followed regarding DUAs,
possible legislation to require a “must appear” status when someone is cited into municipal court for
an underage drinking offense, and self-responsibility of youth and young adults to know right from
wrong.  Main motion to approve re newal of remaining liquor lice nses (re: those not acted upon
pursuant to separate motions) carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
  
Appointment of James W. Hearne, Tucker Fagan and William C. Beama n to the City  of
Cheyenne, Wyoming-Laramie County, State of Wy oming Joint Powers B oard for Frontier Park
improvements.  Ms. Broyles moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not
vote (Mayoral appointments).
Announcement was made of the Appointment of Catherine Nicholas and Maida Wedell to
the Arts In Public Places Committee to be confirmed March 26, 2012.
Under other business, Deputy City Attorney Erik Rasmussen requested the governing body
meet in executive sessi on to discuss matters involving pe nding potential litigation.  Mr. Pierson
moved to meet in executive session, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of  the  g overning bo dy pr esent.  Ma yor Ka ysen a nnounced me mbers would me et in
executive session in Council Chambers, not open to the public; requested the Deputy City Attorney,
City Clerk, and other staff as requested by the Deputy City Attorney, be present at the executive
session, and stated there would be no action taken by the governing body as a result of the executive
session, and that the regular City Council meeting would officially conclude at the close of the
executive session.  (Clerk’s Note: Executive session convened at 7:55 p.m. following a brief recess.)
There being no further business to come before the governing body prior to the executive
session, the meeting was officially adjourned at 8:21p.m. following closure of the executive session.
      
 Submitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
March 22, 2012

Contract Modifica tion No. 2 to Cont ract No. 5496 betwee n th e City of Chey enne,
Engineering Department, and Ayres Associates for additional design services for the East Pershing
Boulevard reconstruction project (Phase IV) (2003-2006, 2007-2010 1% Sales Tax, Board of Public
Utilities, Federal Highway Administration funds) (postponed from March 12, 2012).  Mr. Brown
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Brown moved to amend by substitute, seconded
by Mr. Valdez.  It was clarified the substitute provides for an increase in the modification amount
to $41,645.00 for additional design work.  Doug Vetter, City Engineer, advised the modification was
originally intended to have the consultant reconfigure the East Pershing project in two separate
corridors, and with the amendment an additional segment is being included to extend the projec t
west by seven blocks due to additional federal funds that are available.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Vetter
estimated construction cost for the additional segment will be about $400,000.00.  Motion to amend
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Comments followed regarding
funding and splitting the project into phases, additional segment and related design cost ($21,175.00)
per the amendment, and  previous demolition of two houses in the projec t area.  Main motion as
amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the
exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”. (#5496)
Amendment No. 1 to Professional Services Agreement/Contract No. 5613 between the City
of Cheyenne on behalf of the Cheyenne Metropolitan Planning Organization and Felsburg, Holt and
Ullevig for South Greeley Highway Corridor Plan - Supplemental B udget Request.  Pat Crank,
attorney repr esenting local busi ness owner Maury  Br own, spoke in opposition to the plan as
currently drafted.  He e xplained his client is co ncerned about highway traffic control struc tures
envisioned in  the  pla n th rough im plementation o f r aised me dians; effect of  lim ited a ccess t o
businesses in the area, and proposed future side road accesses along the “backside” of businesses.
Mr. Crank provided information on stakeholders’ meetings that had been held to receive public
comments in c onjunction w ith fo rmulation o f t he pla n, no ting tha t Mr . Brown  a nd/or his
representative had voiced opposition to the raised median concept at the meetings, and encouraged
consideration of other alter natives.  H e spoke in opposit ion to the proposed ame ndment to the
agreement, stating the additional services to be provided, which includes more time to receive  public
comments, was not a serious commitment to listen to residents and business owners in the ar ea
pursuant to their previous input.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Crank reviewed stakeholders’ meetings held so
far and made  statements in support of the c onsultant and MPO staff listening to those who ha d
voiced opposition to the concept of raised medians.  Mark Shubert, business owner in South Greeley
Highway area, spoke in opposition to the plan and the proposed amendment.  He commented on lack
of communication with business owners; raised median concerns; out of state consultants; MPO
authority; state highway department involvement and timing r egarding reconstruction plans, and
suggested o ther t raffic fl ow options in  lie u o f r aised me dians.  Joh n Pa lmer e xpressed h is
concurrence with most of Mr. Shubert’s comments and spoke in opposition to the plan and cost of
the study.  Tom Mason, MPO Director, reviewed the project generally, stating the need for a corridor
plan had been identified by MPO committ ee members, consisting of representatives of City and
County governments and State WYDOT, as a  planning project.  He explained this is a long ter m
project, which will not be implemented in the nea r future, to pro-ac tively address the corr idor’s
traffic problems.  He  stated a w ide variety of solution options  are being reviewed and that the
amendment will ena ble the consultant to continue w ork on the plan, w hich includes ad ditional
meetings with the pu blic and property owners in the area, and the goal is to address, t hrough
planning, the best way to deal with traffic issues and anticipated future volume increases on South
Greeley highway.  He a dvised the consultant, a Colora do firm, is a wel l known transpor tation
planning firm and was selected to provide initial service s through the usual review and approval
process.  Up on inquiry, Mr. Mas on described the purpose of raised center medians as a safety
solution and current problems associated with numerous points of acc ess to properties along the
corridor; stated the draft plan and initial maps are not y et finalized as a policy statement and are
subject t o re vision(s), an d p rovided i nformation o n planning fo r f uture ar ea gro wth t hrough
additional north-south roads, tra ffic safety solutions and curre nt volume of traffi c.  Mr. Mason
advised efforts of the study will also help with beautification along the corridor by including a plan
for sidewalks and bike ways, etc.  Mr. Mason provided clarification on additional expenses related
with the amendment; information on the consultant’s work achieved under the original proposal and
additional work pursuant to the amendment, and noted the final plan will be presented to the public
and property owners and also to the MPO committee and City  and County governing bodies for
approval.  Upon inquiry as to status of the plan if the  amendment was not approved, Mr. Mason
indicated MPO staff will take what information they have and try to finalize, but the plan may not
be complete.  Discussion included the following topics: Raised center medians; service roads and
traffic circulation; access issues that may affect property owners; traffic volume along the corridor;
initial scope of the pr oject; rights-of-way acquisition conce rns af filiated with future  are a
developments or redevelopments; stakeholders’ meetings; purpose of the amendment, which will
include additional stakeholders’ meetings, and provisions of the orig inal agreement and cost of
consultant’s services to date.  During discussion, Mr. Mason provided additional information upon
request and noted the City’s governing body serves as the fiscal agent for the MPO.  Mr. Shubert
expressed his concerns with the effect of raised medians/barriers on businesses; communication with
stakeholders, and traf fic flow issues a nd alterna tives.  Dino Moncec chi, resident in the a rea,
suggested waiting to do a final plan based upon an updated traffic analysis when WYDOT is closer
to acquiring funding to do reconstruction of the highway.  Bob McCue, stating he does work for Mr.
Brown, spoke in opposition to the amendment, commenting  on the cost involved to complete the
plan and questioning whether provisions of the initial contract required the consultant to produce a
deliverable product.  Mr. Brown moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $20,050.00.  Motion
died due to lack of a second to the motion.

Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, April 9, 2012 at 6:00 p.m.
in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for Fiscal Year 2012-2013
Annual Action Plan, City of Cheyenne Community Development Block Grant (CDBG) entitlement.
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, April 9, 2012 at 6:00 p.m.
in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a Bar and Grill liquor
license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Blazin Wings, Inc. d/b/a Buffalo Wi ld
Wings Grill & Bar, 1740 Prairie Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.
There being  no further  business to come bef ore the  g overning body, the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 7:25 p.m.
      
 Submitted by,
 
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
April 5, 2012

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
April 9, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil Chambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker
and Mr. Valdez. Absent:  Mr. Allen. Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney. The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Ms. Ash, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Collins and Dr. Rinne, agenda item #24(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Collins
moved to approve  remaining Consent Agenda items, sec onded by Mr. B rown.  Motion car ried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gover ning Body on Marc h 26, 2012. Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown
returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for Fiscal Year 2012-
2013 Annual Ac tion Pl an, City of Chey enne Community  Deve lopment Block G rant (CDB G)
entitlement.  Deanne Widauf, Housing and Community Development Program Manager, provided
an overview of the CDBG entitlement program; reviewed the annual action plan, which is consistent
with the 5 year Comprehensive Consolidated Plan, and the City=s estimated entitlement allocation
amount of $375,173.00, which is 21% le ss than last year, and anticipated program income in the
amount of $5,000.00.  She noted funds have been utilized for programs that address decent housing,
homeless prevention, senior and youth needs, special population needs, neighborhood revitalization,
and expanding economic opportunities.  Ms. Widauf explained the grant application process; public
notifications and opportunities for input, and review and recommendations of eligible applications
by the Advisory Council.  She reviewed the Advisory Council=s proposed recommended public
service projects ($55,000.00) and general service projects ($109,400.00) f or funding, including
program administration ($76,034.00), and estimated funding allocation amount for each project.
Upon inquiry regarding an application that was previously received relative to funding for a tennis
court at the Martin Luther King, Jr., Park, Ms. Widauf provided an explanation of why the project
was ineligible under HUD criteria.  She clarified Parks and Recreation Dept. has since submitted
another application for funding to assist with irrigation, electrical work and sod at the park, which
should meet funding eligibility requirements.  There being no further comments and purpose of the
hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a Bar and Grill
liquor license application filed with the City  Clerk=s Office for Blazin Wings, Inc. d/b/a Buffalo
Wild Wings Grill & B ar, 1740  Prairie Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  B rian Proffitt, attorney
representing the applicant, introduc ed Jack Van B rocklin, regional manager, and Pam Cannon,
general manager of the Thornton, CO re staurant and regional alcohol trainer. They reviewed the
history and concept of Buffalo Wild Wings restaurants, utilization of the comprehensive alcohol
server BARS (Be A Responsible Server) training program and plans for the Cheyenne restaurant
(anticipated opening date scheduled for mid-November).  Comments followed regarding the local
safe rides program and the TIPS (Training for Intervention ProcedureS) alcohol server program.
Upon inquiry, Mr. Proffitt advis ed he ha s reviewed Wyoming laws and code  requirements and
restrictions regarding alcohol services and training.  There being no further comments and purpose
of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the application to
Finance Committee.
Ordinance amending Title 17, Z oning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established, and

Resolution designating the Mayor and Councilman Jim Brown to meet and confer with the
International Association of Fire Fighters, Local Union No. 279, to develop a proposed 2012 - 2013
Collective Labor Agreement for the Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Department -- referred to Finance
Committee (sponsors - Mr. Brown and Mr. Collins).  Mr. Brown advised the item had been reviewed
under other items for the agenda at the April 2, 2012 Finance Committee meeting and provisions
of Title 2 of City Code had been met.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon
inquiry, City Attorney Dan White advised the resolution was requested by the Union and its attorney
and will authorize the Mayor and Mr. Brown to negotiate provisions of the proposed contract, which
is subject to final approva l by the g overning body.  He indica ted the resolution is  a Agood faith
effort@ by the City and provides for continuing negotiations, advising that the arbitration process has
been initiated due to state statute time frame requirements.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen provided
information on members of the City=s negotiating team as named in the ground rules.  Discussion
topics included: Prior approval of ground rules by the Union with regard to recognition of the City=s
named negotiators; purpose of the  resolu tion and whether it was nee ded; firefighter collective
bargaining statutory requirements; procedures u tilized by other Wy oming municipal g overning
bodies to au thorize negotiators; designating all members of  the g overning body vs. May or and
Councilman Brown and a ffiliated negotiation meetings if a  quorum of the governing body was
present; timing of the Union=s request for the resolution; arbitration process and current status of
selecting arbitrators; continuing negotiations, even though arbitration has begun, as allowed by state
law, and comments in support as well as non-support of the resolution.  During discussion and upon
inquiry, Mr. White provided information on topics raised and explained the Union requested the
resolution as it believes there is a technicality in the ground rules regarding who is authorized on
behalf of the City to negotiate; advised the resolution will provide for continuing negotiations even
though the arbitration process has begun, and re viewed the effect on c urrent negotiations if the
resolution is not adopted.  He discussed designating all members of the governing body vs. Mayor
and Councilman B rown and meeting procedures involving a quorum; provided info rmation on
negotiations vs. the arbitration process; the City=s next fiscal year budget time frame relative to
negotiations and arbitra tion, and reviewed c onducting neg otiations in closed vs. public venue
sessions.  Motion carrie d.  Voting “ yes” -- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne and
Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Ms. Ash, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez. (#5375)

Committee meeting on Tuesday, May 8, 2012 at Noon in Room 122, Municipal Building, as there
were no pending or referred items for the agenda requiring a meeting.

under other business:  “2012-2013 Collective Labor Agreement between the City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming, a nd Chey enne F irefighters L ocal Union No. 279, I nternational Association of
Firefighters” with Mayor Kaysen referring the item to Finance Committee.  It was noted the next
Finance Committee meeting will be held on Monday , May 7, 2012 at 11:00 a.m. in Room 122,
Municipal Building.

volunteers, Cheyenne Light, Fuel and Power, Rocky Mountain Landscaping and City staff who
contributed toward and assisted with the recent planting of 35 trees in David R. R omero South
Cheyenne Community Park.
There being  no further  business to come before the g overning body , May or Ka ysen
adjourned the meeting at 6:16  p.m.

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
May 14, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: MAYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and
Mr. Valdez.  A bsent: Mr. Brown.  A lso present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, Ci ty
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Collins  moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Ms Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gove rning Body on April 23, 2012. Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez declared a conflict of interest (business interest in Radio Station KYOY FM) and
vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Collins moved to appro ve payment of vouche rs as pre sented,
seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Motion carr ied.  Voting “ yes” -- all member s of the g overning body
present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Valdez returned to Council
Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a Publ ic Hearing to determine w hether compliance of anne xation
conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land being situated in a portion of
the N½ of the N½ of the NE¼ of the NE¼ of Section 11, T.13N., R.67W. of the 6th P.M., Laramie
County, Wyoming (generally located at the end of West 5th Street, west of Southwest Drive, a/k/a
4121 W. 5th Street).  Jim Flesher, Urban Planning Office, reviewed requirements and compliance
of conditions c ontained in Wy o. Stat. §15- 1-402, including specific information i ndicating
compliance with Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 (a)(i) through (vi).  Upon inquiry, Mr. Flesher advised there
is a building currently located on the property and stated annexation of the parcel was pursuant to
an exception to the 201 agreement recently approved by the governing body.  There being no further
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public
hearing.
An Ordinance amending the City of Cheyenne’s Sanitation Fee Schedule.  Jim Elias, Public
Works Director, advised City ordinance requires an annual review of the fee schedule with rate
increases based upon the Southeast Wyoming Consumer Price Index (CPI), which is currently 4.1%,
and that the proposed fee increases are in the amount of 4.1% for commercial and residential solid
waste collection and dis posal services.  Mr. Elias repor ted that a 1%  increase was approved for
Fiscal Year 2011 (C PI was 3.4%) and no incr ease was approved for Fiscal Year 2012 (CPI was
3.2%).  He  noted increased costs in City operations involving expanded areas of service due to
population and commercial growth, and expansion of services in such areas as curbside recycling,
lawn waste, electronic waste and household hazardous waste.  He advised the proposed rates will
increase the monthly fee to a ty pical residential customer by $0.85 and the a verage commercial
account (one pick-up a week) by $3.00 a month.  J oe McCord expressed concerns regarding the
proposed increase, costs for roll-off containers, and Sanitation truck routes, and requested Sanitation
policies be reviewed to determine efficiency of pick-up routes.  Reporting for Finance Committee,
Mr. Spiker moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Elias
stated his belief that the City is competitive cost-wise in pricing for roll-off containers compared to
private haulers, and explained route patterns that are utilized involving alleys and on-street pick-ups
involving the automated container pick-up process.  He further advised that the increase in funding
from the proposed rate increases would assist to off-set costs, such as transportation costs affiliated
with fuel, maintenance, repairs, parts and tires.  Concern was expressed regarding the method of
billing for multiple business tenants located in one building, and recycling fees assessed involving
multiple tenants within residential apartment-type buildings.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present.
An Ordinance approving and establishing revised water rates, sewer rates, tap fees, system
development fees, administrative fees, service charges and pe nalties and septic a nd commercial
dump rates, a s recommended by the B oard of Public Utili ties -- re ferred to F inance Committee
(sponsors - Mr. Brown, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Valdez).

Agreement betwee n the City  of Chey enne a nd Merc er G roup, I nc., for a  Personnel
Organizational, Classification and Compensation Study and Analysis.  Rich Wiederspahn, Human
Resources Director, reviewed the Request for Proposal (RFP) process that was utilized resulting in
a recommendation from the City’s Employment Strategy Group (ESG) committee to enter into the
agreement with the Mercer Group.  He provided information on criteria used to evaluate and rank
proposals received; scope of services that will be provided under the agreement, and benefits to the
City involving an independent study and analysis.  Phillip Robertson, representative of the Mercer
Group, reviewed components that will be included in the study and analysis with regard to the areas
of compensation and classification, stating the classification portion will include review of positions
through use of employee questionnaires and interviews, overall point score based on 10 factors,  and
qualifications and skills needed to fulfill job descriptions, with the goal of providing information on
equal pay for equal work scenarios.  He noted that the compensation element will include a regional
salary survey and comparison with other similar market pay plans.  He advised that if the study and
analysis results reflect that certain salary areas should be adjusted, implementation of any
adjustments could be structured to occur over whatever time frame the City decides.  Upon inquiry,
Mr. Robertson stated the c lassification study would be conduc ted prior to the or ganizational
(staffing) study .  Commenting in support of the a greement wer e:  Vicki Ne mecek, Assistant
Director, Public W orks Department; Officer John Gay , representative for the Chey enne Police
Protective Association; Sara Vasquez, President, Cheyenne Public Employees Association (CPEA);
Todd Feezer, Assistant Director, Parks and Recreation Department, and member of the ESG; Claus
Johnson, City employee, former CPEA president and member of the ESG, and Robin L ockman,
Deputy City Treasurer and member of the ESG.  Comments included the following topics: Benefits
of the  stu dy a ssisting wi th e stablishing the worth of pa rticular j obs r elative to p ay sc ales;
information from the study helping the City with future planning as well as having a long term plan
in place for employees; having an employee master plan as a mechanism to provide administrative
guidance on staffing  and compe nsation levels;  study and analysis as a g ood investment when
compared to the percentage of budget attributed to employee costs (i.e. 74% of general fund budget
for Fiscal Year 2013 involves personnel costs); benefits of having a thorough analysis of salaries
to ascertain any minimum/maximum salary caps per classification; concerns with the City’s lack of
current competitiveness r elating to retention of curre nt employ ees a nd rec ruitment of new
employees affiliated with salary levels, and importance of an independent staffing level study by a
professional firm vs. City administrative staff.  Mr. Spiker reported there was no recommendation
on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Collins moved to approve, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Upon
inquiry, Lois Huff, City Treasurer, clarified funding for the study is off-set from previous savings
on employee health insurance coverage costs.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Wiederspahn reviewed various
reasons that employees have left employment with the City, explaining his belief that it is critical
to focus on maintaining employees, and noted the importance of having a market pay plan that is
comparable with neighboring states and the need to review current pay plan inequities.  Discussion
included the following topics: Reasons to approve the study; employees expressing support for the
study; concern with lack of f unding to timely  implement any  salary adjustments that may  be
recommended; an analysis being helpful in determining staffing levels, and employee morale.  Upon
inquiry, Mr. Robertson clarified the scope of services for compensation areas to be studied does not
include consideration of the employee benefits package, noting that the factoring in of benefits into
a salary study is hard to measure as different types of benefits have different importance levels to
individual employees.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present
with the exception of Mr. Pierson and Mr. Spiker voting “no”. (#5838)

Community College, City/County Health Department, and an area of Laramie County adjacent to

[CA] Consideration o f B id # R-32-12 f or fu rnishing thr ee ne w 2012 cab a nd c hassis w ith

liquor license a pplication filed with the City  Clerk's Office for Judi Bufkin d/b/a Judi’s Cajun
Kitchen, 1719 Central Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming.

the regularly scheduled meeting of the Governing Body will instead be held on Tuesday, May 29,
2012 beginning at 6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers, Municipal Building, 2101 O’Neil Avenue,
Cheyenne, Wyoming.
Under other business:
Announcement was made that a calcutta, benefitting the Cheyenne Boys and Girls Club, will
be held Thursday, June 7, 2012 beginning at 7:00 p.m. at Uncle Charlie’s Lounge, 6001 Yellowstone
Road, Che yenne, Wy oming, in  c onjunction w ith the  a nnual Uncle Cha rlie’s “ DONE” g olf
tournament (15% of proceeds to the Cheyenne Boys and Girls Club).
Upon inquiry, Rick Parish, Director, Parks and Recreation Department, provided information
concerning a  pa st Wy o. De pt. of  Tr ansportation ( WYDOT) beautification p roject on  Wes t
Lincolnway involving trees and landscaping areas that has resulted in future maintenance by the City
after completion of the project.  He noted department staff has worked on irrigation system repairs
and replacement of trees, which may be covered by warranty, and discussed the current status of the
trees and watering system.  Doug Vetter, City Engineer, advised when the project was accepted from
WYDOT last fall, it was his understa nding that the war ranty WYDOT had with the landsc ape
contractor for  the tree s would be extended to the City .  May or Ka ysen provided a dditional
information on staff contact with the landscape contractor involved.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Parish also
provided information on Forestry staff watering efforts for City tree areas along Pershing Blvd. and
staffing levels and olde r equipment a ffecting service s.  I t was noted that the D owntown
Development Authority also assists with watering trees downtown.
Upon inquiry regarding the status of replacing non-working lights on the Depot Plaza, Mayor
Kaysen advised the  initial lighting fixtures and ba llasts that were  installed have f ailed to work
properly; that a solution has been found and lighting replacements are in progress.
There being  no further  business to come bef ore the  governing body , May or Ka ysen
adjourned the meeting at 7:17 p.m.

 Carol Intlekofer, MMC

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2012-05-29 | CLIP_ID: 297 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 297

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
May 29, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.
Absent: Mr. Collins and Mr. Pierson.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance, led by members of the Mayor’s
Youth Council, was recited.
Expression of Appreciation to Mayor’s Youth Council and State of the Youth report.  Mayor
Kaysen and Jan Spires, May or’s Youth Council Coordinator, commended member s of the 2011-
2012 Mayor’s Youth Council and prese nted those members present with a cer tificate and gift of
appreciation. Those recognized were: Dean Jackson - Chair, Ian Dome - Vice-Chair, John Matsen -
Co-Secretary, Tyler Bedwell, Juliette Givhan, Joey Marchand, Robert McGarry, McKinzea Olsen,
Jacob Peterson and Gary Sayler.  Those members unable to attend were: Kadi Davis - Co-Secretary,
Addie Fleming and Margarita Medrano.  Mr. J ackson provided a State of  the Youth re port and
described various activities and issues the g roup was involved in during the  past year.  He noted
members lea rned va luable lessons with regar d to leade rship and educ ational experienc es that
included involvement with local government staff, tours of several City facilities and operations, and
participation in community efforts.  Mr. Jackson was presented with a plaque recognizing his service
during the past school year as chair of the Youth Council.  Mayor Kaysen expressed appreciation
to members for their time, commitment and dedication, and also to members’ families for their
support.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [C A] are considered to be

other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by

moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carr ied.

Resolution approving the budget of the Downtown Development Authority for the Fiscal
Year beginning July 1, 2012 and ending June 30, 2013.  John Sundahl, DDA Board President, noted
supplemental budget information that had been provided to governing body members prior to the
beginning of the meeting (information on file with office of City Clerk), and discussed proposed
DDA administrative staff compensation increases included in the budget.  He requested support for
approval of the proposed budget and provided information on the number of DDA employees (2
full-time; 3 part-time)and their duties, and noted staff cost-saving efforts in such areas as in-house
marketing.  Mr. Sundahl also discussed anticipated additional staff duties during the next budget
year due to planned membership in the Main Street program; District property tax mill levy monies
to be received within the next year; estimated Fiscal Year (FY) 2013 revenue amount ($481,220.00)
to be received from TI F (Tax Increment Financing) sales tax funding a nd point of sales issues
currently being determined; past administrative staff services provided under c ontract vs. direct
employee relationship, which has been in effect since hiring of the current executive director, and
amount of TIF funds (estimated $280,000.00) which will be provided back to the City by the DDA
for c ertain identified prog rams/projects.  Upon inquiry , Ch ristie DePoorter , DDA Executive
Director, provided infor mation on the amount of DDA  budget reserves, explaining the level of
current reserves and carry-over amounts, and anticipated parking revenue.  During comments and
upon inquiry, Lois Huff, City Treasurer, expressed her belief that the amount of anticipated revenue
from FY 2013 TIF funding should be in the estimated amount of $473,280.00 based upon
information received from the state and her calculations.  Inquiries included the following topics:
Level of DDA budg et reserves; amount of projec ted TIF revenue, and staff in-house ma rketing
efforts vs. profe ssional media assistance .  Ms. DePoorter and Mr. Sundahl provided a dditional
information in response to questions with Mr. Sundahl noting that DDA has scheduled a meeting
on J une 6  with gove rning body  members to review DD A ac tivities/programs and pr ovide ath
measurement briefing.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by
Mr. Valdez.  Discussion topics included: Approval of the proposed DDA FY budget pursuant to
Wyoming Statut es and City  Code; proposed pe rcentage of sa lary incre ases f or DDA  staff;
anticipated cost of the Main Street program and assistance through the Wyoming Business Council;
adjusting the proposed budget amount for projected TIF revenue; TIF funds submitted by the City
to DDA and the portion DDA then returns to the City; concern with approving DDA staff salary
increases relative to the City’s proposed FY 2013 budget which does not provide for City employee
increases, and suggestion that the resolution be postponed to further review the proposed budget and
supplement information re ceived just prior to the meeting .  During discussion information was
provided by Mr. Sundahl and Ms. DePoorter relative to the percentage of proposed salary increase
per positi on; possible Main Street g rant funds tha t may  be ava ilable; potential adjustment of
estimated TIF revenue amount due to  d iscussions being held, and past DDA staff arrangements
relative to compensation and benefits.  Ms. Ash moved to postpone to June 11, 2012, seconded by
Mr. Allen.  I t was noted a  postponement would allow for  additional information t o be re ceived
pursuant to the previously noted June 6  meeting.  Request was made that the DDA Board considerth
adjusting the budget amount for projected TIF revenue.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Valdez voting “no.”

Resolution authoriz ing the  I ssuance and Sale by  the City  of Chey enne, Wy oming, of
Refunding Revenue Bonds, Series 2012, dated Jun e 5, 2012 in a principal amount not to ex ceed
$9,325,000, for the pur pose of a dvance ref unding a nd defe asing the City ’s Refunding  and
Improvement R evenue B onds, Se ries 2 003; p rescribing the  fo rm of  sa id S eries 2 012 B onds;
providing for the pa yment of the principal a nd interest ther on; and ra tifying, a pproving and
confirming previous ac tions of office rs and membe rs of the G overning Body of the City  and
employees thereof.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Mr. Brown moved to
amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  It was noted the substitute provides for corrections
and inclusion of interest rates pursuant to sale of the bonds.  Entitlement of the substitute Resolution
is as follows: “A Resolution authorizing the Issuance and Sale by the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming,
of Refunding Revenue Bonds, Series 2012, dated June 12, 2012 in a principal amount not to exceed
$9,455,000 for the purpose of advance refunding and defeasing the City’s Outstanding Refunding
and Improvement Revenue Bonds, Series 2003; prescribing the form of said Series 2012 Bonds;
providing f or the pa yment of the princ ipal and intere st thereon; and r atifying, a pproving and
confirming previous ac tions of of ficers and members of  the Gover ning B ody of the City  and
employees thereof.”  Dan Baxter, Kaiser and Company,  advised all of the bonds have been sold
with a number of local banks involved; reviewed the different interest rates per maturity year, and
stated the re funding of bonds has re sulted in a 12% saving s to the City .  He f urther provided
information on ave rage intere st rate and yield, and saving s rec ognized.  Moti on to amend by
substitute carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Upon inquiry, Mr.
Baxter reviewed the bond payment date schedule, which is in Ap ril and October, noting the due
dates are the same as the initial (Series 2003) bond payment schedule.  Main motion, as amended,
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. (#5389)

Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [C A] are considered to be

Ordinance appr opriating money s for the Cit y of Chey enne, Wy oming, f or the va rious
purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing the amount of general and
special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year

of the proposed budget; reviewed the budget process, and noted no changes have been made to
department  b udgets p ursuant t o re cent n otification t hat em ployee h ealth i nsurance co verage
amounts will not change for the next fiscal year.  She stated that, if necessary, reallocation of monies

Vetter, City Engineer, provided information relative to current and upcoming engineering projects
and staff ing le vels, and stated it is difficult to be c ompetitive in the ar ea of  eng ineer salary
compensation  compared with the general market.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Huff clarified, pursuant to her
earlier comments, that $280,000.00 is the total amount that was budgeted for an anticipated health
insurance increase and the amounts are still designated in departmental budgets.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.

Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Symphony Orchestra  for use
of the Cheyenne Civic Center  for Youth Educ ation Concerts.  Kim L ovett, Executive Director,
Cheyenne Symphony Orchestra (CSO), introduced members of the board present in the audience,
and explained the agreement is for a community project that involves all 5th and 6th grade students
within the City.  She stated the purpose of the concerts are to p rovide cultural li teracy, music
appreciation and general musical knowledge to students.  Anna Marie Hales, Cheyenne Symphony
Board member , expressed appr eciation for past Ci ty funding for the pr oject and e ncouraged
continued funding.  Mr. Brown moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $6,453.00, seconded
by Mr. Valdez.  Comments followed on funding participation level for the concerts by the City,
Cheyenne Symphony and the sc hool district with concern expressed relative to whether funding
assistance for this purpose is part of the City's mission as City funds are not expended for other types
of school district educational programs, such as math or science education.  Ms. Hales discussed the
contract arrangement the Civic Cente r has with the  school district.  During comments, M ayor
Kaysen clarified the previous agenda item involving CSO use of the Civic Center provides revenue
to the City and is not a City expenditure amount.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Lovett provided information
on concert costs and funding assistance received, which includes in-kind donations, to help cover
the cost.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the
exception of Mr. Collins and Dr. Rinne voting "no". (#5860)

MEETING: Budget Hearing/Special Session of the Governing Body | DATE: 2012-06-19 | CLIP_ID: 306 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 306

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
June 19, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne convened in special session at 5:30 p.m. in
Council Chambers for the purpose of acting upon the Fiscal Year 2013 budget appropriation ordinance
on 3  and final reading.  Those present were:  MAYOR: Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -rd
Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.
Absent: Mr. Allen.  Also present were: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer; Randy
Hays, Administrative Manager, Board of Public Utilities, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge
of allegiance was recited.
An Ordina nce a ppropriating money s for the City  of Cheyenne, Wy oming f or the va rious
purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing the amount of general and
special taxes as part of the re venue required to meet the said a ppropriation, all for  the fisca l year
beginning July 1, 2012, and ending June 30, 2013.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. B rown
moved to approve the ordinance on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Ms. Ash moved
to amend by removing the following amounts contained within each applicable departmental budget,
which had be en included a s an anticipa ted increase in employee health insurance costs during  the
upcoming fiscal year, and placing the amount, which totals $279,413.00, in a special projects fund line
item to be identified for economic development, seconded by Ms. Broyles:  $6,460.00 - City Council;
$11,392.00 - May or; $12,347.00 - City  Clerk; $66,951.00 - Public Works; $69,215.00 - Po lice;
$61,359.00 - F ire; $31,703.00 - Par ks and Recr eation; $2,253.00 - City  T reasurer; $4,274.00 -
Engineering, and $13,459.00 - Planning Services.  Upon inquiry, Lois Huff, City Treasurer, advised the
funding amounts were included during preliminary preparation of the proposed budget and since the
time the budget was submitted to the governing body the City had received information that there will
be no increase in health insurance costs during the next budget year.  She explained budgeted amounts
for health insurance costs for the current fiscal year, noting an increase in premiums was not as high as
estimated during preparation of the Fiscal Year 2012 budget, and stated the special project line item that
would be created if the motion to amend were approved would be added to the budget book.  Motion
to a mend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Main motion as
amended carried.  Voting  “yes” -- all member s of the g overning body present.  Appre ciation was
expressed to staff for their efforts on preparing the budget.  (#3950)
There being  no further  business previously  noted for the  specia l session, May or Ka ysen
adjourned the meeting at 5:38 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 June 25, 2012

approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --  all members
of the governing body.
Ordinance granting a Franchise by the City of Cheyenne to H igh West Energy, Inc., its
successors and assigns, the right to furnish, sell and distribute electricity to the City and all persons,

Resolution approving the budget of the Downtown Development Authority for the Fiscal
Year beginning July 1, 2012 and ending June 30, 2013 (postponed from 6/11/12).  John Sundahl,
President, Downtown Development Authority (DDA) Board, advised he was present to answer any
questions, and requested approval of a substitute resolution/budget.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt,

Resolution prioritizing the City of Cheyenne’s 2012 applications for Mineral Royalty Capital

Avenue).  Ms. Ash  moved to adopt with staff recommendation #1, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Upon
inquiry, Brett Walker, Development Office, stated the staff recommendation requires the applicant
to submit a revised traffic impact study, which has already been done and is under review by the

Resolution re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2012 budget of the City of Cheyenne according
to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 and 16-4-113.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.
Spiker.  Mr. B rown moved to amend by  substitute, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  I t was advised the
amendment increases the employ ee health i nsurance share program total pay ment amount from
$165,000.00 to $181,000.00.  Upon inquiry, Lois Huff, City Treasurer, advised the substitute was
prepared based upon comments made a t Finance Committee and explained the prog ram which
involves full-time employees during calendar year 2011, but not elected officials; health insurance
savings during calendar year 2011 due to employee health wellness efforts to keep down premium
costs, and elig ibility provi sions to receive a portion of the sa vings.  Ms. Huf f also provide d
information on employ ee health insurance savings realized during the budg et process last y ear,
noting $65,000.00 of the amount saved is going toward the Employee Investment Study, and stated,
if the substitute is approved, there will be approximately $19,000 in savings remaining in the line
item.  It was clarified elected officials will not receive any payment, and that representatives for the
Police Protective Association, Cheyenne Public Employees Association and the Firefighters local
union were all contacted and in agreement with the proposal.   Motion to amend carried.  Voting
"yes" -- all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2012-07-09 | CLIP_ID: 314 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 314

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
July 9, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson,  Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Allen
and Mr. Collins, ag enda items #16 a nd #17 wer e removed from the Consent Ag enda.  Mr . Collins
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on June 25, 2012.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins and Mr. Valdez each declared a conflict of interest (business interest
in vouchers payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services, Bicycle Station, Inc. and Radio Station KYOY
FM respectively) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Pierson moved to approve payment of vouchers
as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Upon inquiry, Doug Vetter, City Engineer, provided information
pertaining to a  voucher payable to A VI Engineering for professional services involving the Norris
Viaduct Detention Pond Project, noting design services are to assist with alleviation of excess drainage
and that anticipated project construction will be later this year or next year.  He advised awareness of
the problem was during reconstruction of Norris Viaduct.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Vetter and Matt Ashby,
Planning Services Director, provided information on a vouc her pa yable to Chase Par k Grants for
strategic grants planning services involving the Lower Capital Basin Project.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement
of the vote, Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins and Mr. Valdez returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a restaurant liquor
license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for 2 Doors Down 2, Inc. d/b/a 2 Doors Down 2,
1802 Dell Range Boulevard, Suite 2, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  There being no comments or input and
purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the application
to Finance Committee.
Ordinance pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts
Established and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map (Cheyenne City Map), of the Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from HR-2 High Density Residential-Developing District to
MR-2 Medium-Density Residential-Developing District for Lot 21, Block 14, Saddle Ridge, Cheyenne,
Wyoming, to be known as a portion of Saddle Ridge, Fourth Filing (located at the southeast corner of
the intersection of Whitney Road and Countryside Avenue).  Reporting for Public Services Committee,
Ms. Ash moved to approve  on third and final r eading, seconded by Ms. Broy les.  Motion carr ied.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#3953)
Ordinance pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts
Established and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map (Cheyenne City Map), of the Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business District to LI Light Industrial
District for Lots 2, 3, and 4, Block 397, Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southeast
corner of the intersection of Dillon Avenue and W. 17  Street).  Ms. Ash moved to approve on thirdth
and final reading, seconded by Ms. Broy les.  Motion carr ied.  Voting  “yes” -- all members of the
governing body. (#3954)
Ordinance annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being situated in a
portion of the North Half of the North Half of the Northeast Quarter of the Northeast Quarter of Section
11, Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (generally located
at the I-25/I-80 Interchange, a.k.a. 4121 W. 5th Street).  Ms. Ash moved to approve on third and final
reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carr ied.  Voting “yes” --  all member s of the g overning
body. (#3955)
Ordinance g ranting a  F ranchise b y t he C ity o f C heyenne t o H igh W est E nergy, I nc., its
successors and assigns, the right to furnish, sell and distribute electricity to the City and all persons,
businesses, and industry within a portion of the City High West’s certificated territory and the right to
acquire, construct, install, locate, maintain, operate and extend into, within and through said City all
facilities reasonably necessary to furnish, sell and distribute electricity within the City and the right to
make reasonable use of all streets and other public places and public easements as herein defined as may
be necessary; and fixing the terms and conditions thereof.  Randy Reed, representative for High West
Energy (HWE), advised communications are being held between HWE and Cheyenne Light, Fuel and
Power (CLFP) and requested a postponement of the item for a month to continue discussions to possibly
propose a modification of the proposed ordinance.  It was confirmed the postponement date requested
was August 13, 2012.  John Sundahl, attorney for CLFP, concurred with Mr. Reed’s comments and
spoke in support of the requested postponement to August 13 .  James Perrott Macneil expressed histh
opinion that those present who w ished to speak on the item should be a llowed to do so and that the
mayor should not cast a vote on the item, and commented in favor of competition. Savannah Birkle,
Miss Teen Laramie County, commented in support of CLFP, noting its support to numerous community
organizations and activities.  Upon inquiry regarding whether the governing body would be involved
in determining which utility company would provide service to future properties annexed into the City,
if the franchise ordinance for HWE is approved, Mr. Reed and Mr. Sundahl provided their opinions of
the Public Service Commission’s vs. the governing body’s authority.  Thurman King commented on
the issue of fairness vs. dollars and spoke in support of quality services he has received over the years
from HWE.  R eporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve on second reading,
seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Mr. Brown moved to postpone to August 13, 2012, seconded by Mr. Spiker.
Recommendation was made that the City also needs to be “in the loop” with regard to subjects being
discussed by HWE and CLFP that may result in modifications to the proposed ordinance as well as the
existing franchise ordinance with CL FP.  Upon inquiry , Carol I ntlekofer, City Clerk, reviewed the
following Council meeting  schedule for the a genda item if the motion to post pone was approved:
Ordinance would return on the August 13, 2012 City Council meeting agenda for referral back to
Finance Committee for review and recommendation on second reading (no discussion or action will be
taken at the August 13  meeting); Finance Committee would meet Monday, August 20, 2012 at 11:00th
a.m. (Room 122, Municipal Building) to review and vote on a recommendation on the ordinance, and
the ordinance would be placed on the August 27, 2012 City Council meeting agenda for anticipated
action on second reading.  Ms. Intlekofer advised when an agenda item is postponed for a time frame
that is more than the next regularly scheduled City Council meeting, the item is referred back to the
respective Council committee for review/recommendation.  Motion to post pone to August 13, 2012
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Mayor Kaysen called for a brief recess to allow those in attendance for the previous agenda item
to vacate Chambers if they wished to do so, and reconvened the meeting at 6:34 p.m.
Ordinance pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts
Established and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map (Cheyenne City Map), of the Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from MUR Mixed Use with Residential Emphasis District to
MUB Mixed Use with Business Emphasis District for the South ½ of Lot 1, the West 5.72 feet of the
North 66 feet of Lot 1, all of Lots 2, 3 and 4, Block 201, Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located
at the southeast corner of the intersection of Warren Avenue and E. 23rd Street) -- referred to Public
Services Committee (sponsor - Ms. Ash). 
[CA] Resolution author izing the submittal of an application to the  Wyoming State L oan and
Investment Board for a loan from the Wyoming Clean Water State Revolving fund for an amount not
to exceed $5 million for the Crow Creek Reclamation Facility Improvements Project and authorizing
the Mayor, City Clerk and the Director of the Board of Public Utilities to execute the application and
all related loan documents.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5407)
Resolution authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Saddle Ridge,
Fourth Filing, a replat of Lots 1-19, 21 and 23, Block 14 and Lots 1-27, Block 15, Saddle Ridge,
Cheyenne, Wyoming (located east of and a djacent to W hitney Road, betwe en Laramie Street and
Countryside Avenue).  Ms.  Ash moved to adopt with staff recommendation #2, seconded by Ms.
Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (5408)
[CA] Resolution authorizing the submittal of an application to the Wyoming Water Development
Commission for additional grant funding for the Southern Water Transmission Main - Phase II Project
in an amount not to exceed $4,261,200 and authorizing the Mayor, City Clerk and the Director of the
Board of Public Utilities to execute the application and all related documents.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried. (#5409)
Resolution authorizing the Mayor to enter into negotiations for the purchase of Lot 1, Block 1,
Marriott Subdivision, located near the intersection of West Lincolnway and Capitol Avenue, Cheyenne,
Wyoming.  L ea B ain re quested the r esolution not be adopted a nd offered sug gestions reg arding
development of a strategic plan for the site, through use of a portion of funds from the economic
development budget line item, or consider ation of ente ring into a perpe tual lease with the adjac ent
private property owner to discuss development options and lease provisions.  She expressed concern
with the expense to potentially purchase the property at this time.  Jack Whyatt concurred with Ms.
Bain’s comments; expressed concern with purchasing the property and whether the private property
owner involved would be willing to sell for the appraised value amount, and commented that the City’s
involvement in private enterprise is not a wise use of dollars.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by
Mr. Collins.  D iscussion i ncluded t he f ollowing t opics: P urpose o f t he r esolution ( i.e. a uthorize
negotiation discussions only); need for short and long term plans for the property if the City was to
acquire ownership of all of the area; property appraisal; difficulty in garnering interested developers due
to private ownership involved in the area; estimated time frame for negotiations; erosion of sidewalk
and adjacent building foundation concerns; City’s legal condemnation authority; public sentiment;
creating an envir onment to attrac t a private  develope r; conc ern w ith establ ishing a purc hasing
precedence involving property blight in the downtown area; monetary concerns; public vs. private
development role and collaborative efforts; leveraging of resources, and topics that may be presented
during negotiation discussions.  During discussion Mayor Kaysen provided information in response to
inquiries from Council members, including the following areas:  Discussions and conversations that
have occurred over the years since “the hole” was created (due to fire) with potential developers and
interested individuals; lack of interest by developers; short term plan to install a fence/facade to screen
property visually if the City acquires the property; potential long term/future property use, including
partnering efforts; current evaluation and work to be done to stabilize the sidewalk; plans to update a
previous property appraisal, and possible negotiation discussion topics.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Allen voting “no.”  (#5410)
Resolution authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to enter into cooperative and other agreements
as necessary to enable the City of Cheyenne to join with other participating Wyoming Municipalities
in an applica tion for State S mall Business Credit I nitiative funds available  from the United States
Department of Treasury pursuant to the Small Business Jobs Act of 2010, and repealing Resolution No.
5317.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Inquiry was made with regard to the
difference between the proposed resolution involving a coalition of Wyoming municipalities and the
previously adopted r esolution (Resolution #5317), and compensa tion formula for se rvices to be
provided by Wyoming Smart Capital Network (WSCN).  Ra ndy Bruins, CEO, Cheyenne LEADS,
explained details affiliated with the initial resolution (#5317), stating that since the time an application
was submitted pursuant to that resolution the U.S. Treasury made substantive modifications to i ts
program rules that resulted in LEADS and the Cheyenne-Laramie County Economic Joint Powers Board
determining that the entities would no longer be able to assist as initially proposed.  Diane Wolverton,
Executive D irector, Wy oming Smart Capital Ne twork, discussed the amount of trea sury funding
available to municipalities and percen tage of distribution payments that can be allocated for service
fees.  She stated the amount of fees will be set out in the affiliated coalition cooperative agreement and
WSCN services agreement.  M otion carried.  V oting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
(#5411)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing a street name change from Archer Service Road to Hutchins Drive
for the portion of the former I-80 Service Road located East of Industry Drive and South of Campstool
Addition, 1  and 3  filings, to the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming -- referred to Publicst rd

[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Worldwide

Ash).
[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Campstool

Walk and Dart Tournament (15% of proceeds to benefit the Association).
Under other business:
Matt Ashby , Planning Ser vices Dire ctor, noted the  attendanc e of the Historic Downtown
Cheyenne Placemaking Project consultants, Elaine Carmichael and Tom Gallaher, at the meeting and
provided a brief synopsis of the project, which was to identify strategies for invigorating the downtown
area.  He noted the final draft project report would be presented to City Council members at a work
session the following day with other public meetings to present the report later in the week  Ms.
Carmichael and Mr. Gallaher eac h stated they  enjoy ed wor king on the  projec t and expressed
appreciation to those who helped with the project.
Rick Pa rish, D irector of  Pa rks a nd Recreation, a dvised th e a nnual Wy oming Op en G olf
Tournament begins July 11 ; noted the success of the recent annual Super Day event and referencedth
several softball tournaments that have been held.
Police Chief Brian Kozak reminded those present that the 3  annual Neighborhood Night Outrd
event would be  held Tuesda y evening , J uly 10  and , upon inquiry, advised he  would provideth
information on each neighborhood event location to members of the governing body.
Ms. Ash commented on recent City Council agenda items involving the Unified Development
Code and expressed concern with UDC interpretation by staff and developers.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, Mayor Kaysen adjourned
the meeting at 7:36 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
