MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2012-07-23 | CLIP_ID: 321 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 321

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 July 23, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson,  Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the a genda.)  M r. Collins moved to approve  Consent Ag enda items, sec onded by Mr.
Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on July 9, 2012.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.
Mr. Collins declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a  voucher payable to Bicycle
Station, Inc. and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of vouchers as
presented, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Up on inquiry regarding a voucher payable to A ll Traffic Data
Services Inc., Matt Ashby , Planning Ser vices Director, advised se rvices provided by  the company
involved a study  initiated throug h the Metropolitan Planning  Org anization (MPO) for timing
information on traffic signals.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, provided information affiliated
with a voucher payable to the Associated Legal Group, LLC, pertaining to legal costs for litigation of
the eminent domain matter involving the Hollywood Video property.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Ashby advised
a voucher payment for a Dale Carnegie Course was an MPO expenditure for e mployee training on
communication and public relations/interaction skills.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Collins
returned to Council Chambers.
Ordinance pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts
Established and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map (Cheyenne City Map), of the Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from MUR Mixed Use with Residential Emphasis District to
MUB Mixed Use with Business Emphasis District for the South ½ of Lot 1, the West 5.72 feet of the
North 66 feet of Lot 1, all of Lots 2, 3 and 4, Block 201, Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located
at the southeast corner of the intersection of Warren Avenue and E. 23rd Street).  Jan Nelson Schroll
expressed concerns regarding the proposed zone change and encroachment of commercial activities in
residential neighborhoods, particularly historic neig hborhoods.  Randy  Byers, The De sign Studio,
architectural firm representing the applicant, Meridian Trust Federal Credit Union, clarified the zone
change request will more closely reflect the business environment use already present on the block.  He
briefly reviewed the credit union’s business history at the location and current zoning in the vicinity,
planned site enhancements involving improved access for safety to the drive-up facility and ownership
of lots within the block by the credit union.  He clarified the property involved in the proposed zone
change was not within a historic district.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash moved
to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” - all members
of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Allen voting “no”.
[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing a street name change from Archer Service Road to Hutchins Drive
for the portion of the former I-80 Service Road located East of Industry Drive and South of Campstool
Addition, 1  and 3  filings, to the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming.  Motion (per Consentst rd
Agenda) carried. (#5412)
RESOLUTION - A doption of the Chey enne Metropolitan Area O n Street B icycle Plan and
Greenway Plan update.  Sreyoshi Chakraborty, MPO Office, provided information on compilation of
the proposed plan which involved an 18 month planning and data driven process; vision for the health,
safety and welfare needs of the community involving safer bike routes and connectivity; communication
and coordination of the plan’s development through an advisory committee and local, county, state and
city agencies and staff; public meetings that had been held and surveys conducted, and plan correlation
with the 2005 Cheyenne Transportation Master Plan.  Jeff Wiggins, Parks and Recreation Department,
discussed the safe routes to school program and commented on the plan being a guide for short and long
range planning representing a unified vi sion for the c ommunity.  He pr ovided information on the
Greenway Plan update; advised the draft plan as well as the final draft has been available for public
review on the City ’s website throug hout the proce ss, and commented on the  plan pr oviding
identification of safer bike routes.  Dick Krahenbuhl, Greenway Advisory Committee member, spoke
in favor of the On Street Bicycle Plan and its attributes in reflecting foresight, planning and commitment
to the future of Cheyenne, noting the plan is not a construction document.  Frank Cole commented on
his concerns with the plan as follows:  County Planning Commission has not yet acted on the plan,
accessibility to t he Greenway by all persons in all conditions, need to review City laws relative to
motorized assistive vehicles on the Greenway (i.e. motorized bicycles and wheelchairs), and  path
connectivity involving the area near Converse Avenue and Ogden Road.  Jeff Moench spoke in support
of the plan a s a valua ble resource for the c ommunity.  Todd Fe ezer, Assistant Direct or, Parks and
Recreation Department, reviewed recent successes in acquiring funding through grants and donations
for a ssistance with pre -planning and construc tion of projects, noting  the cur rent Holliday  Park
Greenway Connector Project and Paul Smith’s Children’s Village in Lions Park.  He explained an on-
street bicycle plan is the safest way to convey  bicyclists into areas whe re a Greenway path is not
available, and commented that acquiring property for Greenway path extensions is becoming harder to
achieve.  Ms. Ash reported there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee.
Ms. Ash  moved to adopt, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Discussion followed on safety concerns involving
vehicular drivers and bicycle operators and level of know ledge and e xperience in sha ring roadway
environments; shared lane markings; bicycle parking requirements for new or re-development projects,
and having a conceptual document in place to assist with opportunities to seek potential grant funding
to help with future implementation of the plan.  Staff and bicycle advisory committee participants were
commended for their efforts in developing the plan.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the governing body. (#5413)
RESOLUTION - Au thorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a  Final Plat for Worldwide
Subdivision, a portion of the NE¼ NE¼ of Section 11, T.13N., R.67W., of the 6th P.M., Cheyenne,
Wyoming (located at the I-25/I-80 interchange).  Ms. Ash moved to adopt with staff recommendations
#2 and #3, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion carried.  Voting "yes" -- all members of the governing body.
(#5414)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a  Final Plat for Ca mpstool
Addition, Fourth Filing, situate in portions of the NE¼ of Sec tion 31 and the NW¼ of Section 32,
T.14N., R.65W., 6th P.M., and a replat of a portion of Campstool Addition, First Filing, Cheyenne,
Wyoming (located north of and a djacent to I -80 Service Road, eas t of Industry Road and south of
Venture Drive).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5415)
 RESOLUTION - Authorizing a special use approval for a creative sign program for Block 392,
Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (The Dinneen Block located on Lincolnway between Pioneer and
Thomes).  Randy  Byers, The Design Studio, architectura l firm working  with the applicant ( Dinco
Development, L LC), provided infor mation on proposed conditions off ered by the deve loper (i.e .
applicant), instead of recommended staff conditions, during discussion on the item at Public Services
Committee.  I t was conf irmed that members of  the g overning body  had re ceived a copy  of the
developer's proposed conditions.  Ms. Ash moved to adopt, seconded by  Ms. Broyles.  Ms. Ash moved
to amend by using Dinco Development, LLC conditions instead of City staff conditions #1 through #4,
seconded by Ms. Broy les.  Mr. By ers clarified the r evisions offered by the deve loper included 5
recommendations.  C omments fo llowed regarding the Dinneen Block p roject an d t he d eveloper's
financial commitment to the downtown throug h projec t are a improveme nts; concern with staff
conditions that had been proposed; support for the developer to be able to proceed with their proposed
signage and landscaping plans; developer's request for unique signage, and clarification of the pending
motion and the number of developer conditions proposed.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen verified the
motion on the floor wa s to amend by  using the  develope r's conditions as proposed.  B randon
Cammarata, Planning and Development Director, clarified this special use would allow for  creative
signage affiliated with a redevelopment project that would not otherwise be allowed, and stated staff
has r eviewed th e d eveloper's c onditions a nd s upports th e s ame.  M r. Byers p rovided in formation
involving the unique aspects of one of the signs, which will be used as a screening and support structure
for the dow ntown 17th Street Pedestrian L ighting and Bea utification Project.  He stated e lectrical
equipment to be installed will provide sufficient power for anticipated downtown events and activities.
Motion to amend carried.  Voting "yes" -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms.
Ash voting "no".  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting "yes" -- all members of the governing body.
(#5416)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Adopting a Schedule of Application Fees, Initial License Fees and Renewal
License Fees for certain new classifications of City of Cheyenne Contractor Licenses and Building
Trade Licenses -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsor - Mr. Brown).
[CA] RESOLUTION - Certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne has agreed on
County Projects to be submitted to the State Loan and Investment Board for the 2013-2014 Countywide
Consensus Block Grant Funding -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsor - Mr. Collins). 
[CA] RESOLUTION - Certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne is in agreement
on a Garage Project for the Town of Burns to be submitted to the State Loan and Investment Board for
the 2013-2014 Countywide Consensus Block Grant Funding -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsor
- Mr. Collins).
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department Victim Witness Program, and the
State of Wy oming, Division of Victim Service s, for services to victims of cr ime (Victim Service s
grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5889)
[CA] Contract Employment Ag reement betwee n the City  of Chey enne, Police D epartment, and
William Linden, for crime victim assistance services (Victim Services grant).  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried. (#5890)
[CA] Contract Employment Ag reement betwee n the City  of Chey enne, Police D epartment, and
Brenda Johnson, for crime victim assistance services (Victim Services grant).  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried. (#5891)
[CA] Contract Employment Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and Leslie
Burch, for crime victim assistance services (Victim Services grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda)
carried. (#5892)
[CA] Contract Modification No. 2 to Con tract No. 5414 between the City of Cheyenne and Clean
Harbors Environmental Services, Inc., for Hazardous Waste Disposal Services, Cheyenne Household
Hazardous Waste Facility -- referred to Finance Committee.
 Consideration of Bid #M-23-12 for contracts between the City of Cheyenne and Inman Roofing,
Inc., Chey enne, Wy oming, and Weather craft Company , Scottsbluff-Ger ing, N ebraska, for  Roof
Replacement at Municipal Building and Civic Center  (SLIB-12 funding).  Reporting  for F inance
Committee, Mr. Brown moved to accept the bid submitted by Inman Roofing, Inc. in the amount of
$138,229.00, and the bid from Weathercraft Company in the amount of $27,720.00, for a total bid
amount of $165,949.00, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Mr. Brown moved to amend by substitute contracts
to accept the bid from Inman Roofing, Inc. in the amount of $192,035.00, and the bid from Weathercraft
Company in the amount of $80,975.00, for a total bid amount of $273,010.00 with additional funding
of $107,010.00 to be transferred from general fund reserves, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  It was explained
that the initial total bid amount would onl y be enough funding, as received from the State Land and
Investment Board program, to replace certain segments of the roof, and that the proposed amendment,
utilizing reserves, would enable all of the roof work to be completed at the same time.  It was noted
there are several roof problem areas that need to be fixed.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting "yes" --
all members of the governing body.  Upon inquiry, Jim Elias, Public Works Director, stated that with
staff oversight they will be able to identif y specific areas of the roof tha t each company works on
relative to any warranty issues.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting "yes" -- all members of the
governing body. (#5893/#5894)
[CA] Consideration of B id #M-24-12 for  a contr act between the City of Chey enne, Engineering
Department, and Modern Electric Co., Casper, Wyoming, for Ridge Road Traffic Signal Replacement
Project.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5895)
[CA] Consideration of B id #M-1-13 f or a contract between the City  of Chey enne, Engineering
Department, and Kolbe Striping, Inc., Castle Rock, Colorado, for 2012 Epoxy Striping Project (2007-
2010, 2011-2014 1% Sales Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee.
Restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for 2 Doors Down 2, Inc.
d/b/a 2 Doors Down 2, 1802 Dell Range Boulevard, Suite 2, Chey enne, Wyoming.  To a void any
perception of conf lict i nvolving a business relationship with the applica nt, Mr. Spiker dec lared a
conflict of interest and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Brown moved to approve, seconded by Mr.
Collins.  Mo tion car ried.  Vo ting “yes” -- all m embers of the governing body present in Council
Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Spiker returned to Council Chambers.
Re-Appointment of Jeannine R. Stallings to the Cheyenne Public Transit Advisory Board.  Mr.
Pierson moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointment).
[CA] Annual Report to the City of Cheyenne regarding capital improvements made at the Airport Golf
Course as required by Lease Agreement -- referred to Finance Committee.
Announcement of Appointments of Scott French and Amiee Reese to the Visit Cheyenne Board
to be confirmed August 13, 2012. 
Under other business, William Overling expressed concerns regarding the Cadillac Ranch Bar
and its businss effect on his neighborhood, citing problems with noise, parking, debris, vandalism and
pedestrian safety, and the process for approving noise permits when dealing with excessive noise.  He
suggested City Council consider utilizing EPA guidelines for establishing an excessive noise program.
Mr. O verling f urther c ommented o n th e fo llowing top ics:  L imiting no ise pe rmits un der c ertain
conditions and situations that involve events and activities to be held near residential neighborhoods,
suggested applications for permits be reviewed and approved through the City Council meeting process;
concern with City Council approval of certain types of liquor licenses for business locations that are in
close proximity to residential areas, and providing special parking areas (with permits) designated for
residents in mixed use commercial/residential areas.  He thanked the Police Department and elected
officials for their assistance in responding to his neighborhood concerns.  It was advised there are some
existing special residential parking areas, pursuant to City Code, within the City.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, Mayor Kaysen adjourned
the meeting at 7:18 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk

Allen and Mr. Collins. Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan
White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the a genda.)  Mr. Spiker moved to appr ove Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr.
Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on July 23, 2012.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown  declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a  voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Servic es and vac ated Council Chamber s.  Mr. Valde z moved to approve pa yment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present in Council C hambers.  Following  announcement of the vote, Mr. Br own
returned to Council Chambers.
Ordinance pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts
Established and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map (Cheyenne City Map), of the Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from MUR Mixed Use with Residential Emphasis District to
MUB Mixed Use with Business Emphasis District for the South ½ of Lot 1, the West 5.72 feet of the
North 66 feet of Lot 1, all of Lots 2, 3 and 4, Block 201, Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located
at the southeast corner of the intersection of Warren Avenue and E. 23rd Street).  Jan Nelson-Schroll
referenced comments of concern regarding the proposed zone change that she expressed at the August
8  Public Services Committ ee me eting as we ll as documents (lett er and photo packe t) she hadth
submitted at the Committee meeting (on file with City Clerk’s office). She reiterated and commented
on se veral of  he r c oncerns in volving commercial/retail b usiness a ctivities a nd e ncroachments in
residential neighborhoods; MUB vs. MUR landscaping requirements; demolition of the house within
the block that she believes is a heritage structure and asbestos removal from same; need for alternative
ideas to kee p and re store the integ rity of re sidential neighborhoods and he ritage homes (example:
structure relocation efforts), and the importance of historical-based tourism involving structures, and
historic building revitalization and preservation efforts.  Randy Byers, The Design Studio, agent for
applicant, Meridian Trust Federal Credit Union, expressed his understanding of certain concerns stated
by Ms. Nelson-Schroll and verified the property to be re-zoned does not involve the house that will be
demolished.  He a dvised the house is not l ocated within an historic district and that any  asbestos
removal will be handled in ac cordance with required specifications, and commented on the natural
evolution of city growth generally and the economic feasibility difficulties of re-locating structures.
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash moved to approve on third and final reading,
seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Comments followed on the proposed re-zoning area involving the applicant’s
drive-through and parking areas and not affecting the outcome of the house that may be demolished,
and encroachment concerns balanced with business needs and requests.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body present. (#3956)
Ordinance granting a Franchise by the City of Cheyenne to High West Energy, Inc., its
successors and assigns, the right to furnish, sell and distribute electricity to the City and all persons,
businesses, and industry within a portion of the City High West’s certificated territory and the right to
acquire, construct, install, locate, maintain, operate and extend into, within and through said City all
facilities reasonably necessary to furnish, sell and distribute electricity within the City and the right to
make reasonable use of all streets and other public places and public easements as herein defined as may
be necessary; and fixing the terms and conditions thereof.  Due to postponement of the item from  July
9, 2012, Mayor Kaysen referred the item back to Finance Committee and stated that it had come to his
attention that High West Energy has requested the ordinance be withdrawn from the a genda.   Mr .
Brown, one of the ordinance sponsors, advised he was aware a letter had been submitted to the City
Clerk from the attorney representing High West Energy requesting the ordinance be withdrawn from
the Council’s meeting  agenda, and inquired whe ther a representative from Hig h West E nergy was
present at the meeting to provide information regarding the request.  Randy Reed, attorney for High
West Energy, confirmed High West has requested this item be withdrawn explaining High West would
like more time to continue communications with Cheyenne Light, Fuel and Power and the City, noting
High West plans to come back at a later date to request a franchise and withdrawing the item now will
alleviate the need to ask for additional postponements due to continuing discussions.  Upon query by
Mayor Kaysen to the other City Council ordinance sponsors, as well as other Council members present,
no objection was voic ed by Council members re garding the re quest to withd raw the item.  May or
Kaysen then announced the item was being withdrawn from the agenda.
[CA] RESOLUTION - Adopting a Schedule of Application Fees, Initial License Fees and Renewal
License Fees for certain new classifications of City of Cheyenne Contractor Licenses and Building
Trade Licenses.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5417)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne has agreed on
County Projects to be submitted to the State Loan and Investment Board for the 2013-2014 Countywide
Consensus Block Grant Funding.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5418)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne is in agreement
on a Garage Project for the Town of Burns to be submitted to the State Loan and Investment Board for
the 2013-2014 Countywide Consensus Block Grant Funding.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
(#5419)
RESOLUTION - S upporting the  City  of  Che yenne’s Ap plication f or Re creational T rails
Program (RTP) Funds to c reate the North Cheyenne Community Park Trail System – r eferred to
Finance Committee (sponsors - Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez).  Ma yor Kaysen referred the item to
Finance Committee.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown advised the item wa s reviewed
under other items for the agenda at the August 6  Finance Committee meeting and provisions of Titleth
2 of City Code have been met.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Jeff Wiggins,
Parks & Recreation Department, stated the grant is administered through the Wyoming State Parks and
Cultural Resources Department and the grant request is to implement approximately 11,000 lineal feet
of trails within North Cheyenne Community Park that would serve hikers, trail joggers and mountain
bicyclists.  He stated the estimated project cost is $50,000 and the grant request will be $35,000 with
the City’s grant match amount of $15,000 being provided (donated) by area businesses.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. (#5419)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Grenville
Subdivision, First Filing, a replat of the West 44 feet of Lot 3, Lots 4-7 and the East 10 feet of Lot 8,
Block 1 , Lake M innehaha A ddition a nd Lots 2 1-30, B lock 1 , Re subdivision o f Lake M innehaha
Addition, and the alley between said lots, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to E.
Lincolnway, west of Hot Springs Avenue) – referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor -- Ms.
Ash).
[CA] RESOLUTION - Re- appropriating the  Fisca l Year  2012 budg et of the Cit y of Chey enne
according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 and 16-4-113 – referred to Finance Committee (sponsor
– Mr. Brown). 
[CA] RESOLUTION - Re- appropriating the  Fisca l Year  2013 budg et of the City  of Chey enne
according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 and 16-4-113 – referred to Finance Committee (sponsor
-- Mr. Brown).
[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to Approve and Accept the Fiscal Year 2011
Staffing f or Adequate Fire  and Emer gency Response (SAF ER) Gra nt from the United States
Department of Homeland Security in the amount of $846,090.00 (Award No. EMW-2011-FH-00743)
– referred to Finance Committee (sponsor – Mr. Brown).
[CA] Juvenile Housing Agreement between Laramie County, Wyoming, and the City of Cheyenne
– referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] User Agre ement between the City  of Chey enne, Par ks and Recr eation De partment, a nd
Cheyenne Stampede Junior “A” Hockey, LLC, to host the 2012-2013 season at the City-owned Ice and
Events Center – referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Media Sales Ag ency Ag reement betwee n the C ity of C heyenne, Par ks and Recr eation
Department, Cheyenne Stampede Junior “A” Hockey, LLC and SeeHear Media, LLC, for Static Visual
Advertising at the City-owned Ice and Events Center – referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Wind Energy Lease Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Wind, LLC, for
Windpower Facilities on the Belvoir Ranch – referred to Finance Committee.
 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 2 to Contract No. 5414 between the City of Cheyenne and Clean
Harbors Environmental Services, Inc., for Hazardous Waste Disposal Services, Cheyenne Household
Hazardous Waste Facility.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $100,000.00)
(#5414)
[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-1-13 for a contract between the C ity o f C heyenne, Engineering
Department, and Kolbe Striping, Inc., Castle Rock, Colorado, for 2012 Epoxy Striping Project (2007-
2010, 2011-2014 1% Sales Tax).  Motion to accept bid (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to
exceed $129,025.00). (#5897)
[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-2- 13 for a  contra ct betwe en the City  of Chey enne, Par ks and
Recreation Administration, and Pioneer Construction Co., I nc., Cheyenne, Wyoming,  for the new
concessions and toilet facilities for Pioneer Park Project (State and Local Grants funding) – referred to
Finance Committee.
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-4-13 for furnishing bulk Ice Control Product to the City of Cheyenne,
Public Works Department (2007-2010, 2011-2014 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee.
Appointments of Scott Fre nch and A miee Reese to the Visit Cheyenne Board.  Mr . Spiker
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. B rown.  Motion car ried.  Voting  “yes” -- all member s of the
governing body present with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointments).
[CA] Annual Report to the City of Cheyenne regarding capital improvements made at the Airport Golf
Course as required by Lease Agreement.  Motion to acknowledge receipt (per Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Request by retail liquor licensee One East Sixteenth, LLC, 1601 Central Avenue, for a 90 day
extension of the time frame condition placed on the license pertaining to a lease agreement with a
restaurant establishment to operate within the building – referred to Finance Committee.
Announcement w as made that the Laramie County Republican Party will be  sponsoring a
Calcutta to be held Saturday, September 22, 2012 beginning at 6:00 p.m. at the Kiwanis Community
House, 4603 Lions Park Drive, Cheyenne, Wyoming, in conjunction with the Red State Rendezvous
(Proceeds to benefit Laramie County Republican Party).
Announcement was made of a Public Meeting to be held Monday August 27, 2012 at 6:00 p.m.
in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’N eil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the purpose of receiving
public comment on the submission of a 5307 Federal Grant to fund transportation services by Cheyenne
Transit for Fiscal Year 2013.
Announcement was made of the Appointment of Michael A. Metzler to the Contractor Licensing
Board to be confirmed August 27, 2012.
Under other business:
James Perrott Macneil made statements in opposition to any wind turbine farm development on
the Belvoir Ranch; expressed Belvoir Ranch ownership concerns, and reviewed his family lineage.
In response to Council member inquiry regarding the removal of geese from Holliday Park due
to a construction project at Lincolnway and Dunn Ave., Mayor Kaysen advised upon completion of the
reconstruction of the intersection and the Greenway connector, the geese will be relocated back to the
park.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, Mayor Kaysen adjourned
the meeting at 6:30 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 August 20, 2012

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2012-08-27 | CLIP_ID: 326 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 326

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 August 27, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson,  Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez. Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
PRESENTATION FROM THE  CITY OF CHEYENNE TO CHEYENNE FRONTIER DAYS
FOR THEIR WORK DURING CFD 2012.  Police Chief Brian Kozak commended Cheyenne Frontier
Days leadership and vo lunteers for their responsible liquor mana gement procedures and policies at
Frontier Park during the recent annual CFD event and presented the organization, through Rod Hottle,
this year’s General Chairman, with the annual Police Chief’s award.  Chief Kozak also presented Dawn
Thompson w ith a commemorative Ca lvary Ho nor G uard sw ord in  re cognition of  he r e fforts i n
spearheading the CFD’s liquor management program.  Participating in the presentation were members
of the Police Department’s Calvary Honor Guard.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desi gnation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Collins, and pursuant to support received by Dr. Rinne
and Ms. Ash, agenda items #13 and #21(d) were removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Brown moved
to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gover ning Body on Aug ust 13, 2012.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown  declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a  voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Services and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Spiker moved to approve payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following  announcement of the vote, Mr . Brown
returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public meeting f or the purpose of receiving public comment on the
submission of a 5307 Federal Grant to fund transportation services by Cheyenne Transit for Fiscal Year
2013.  J oe Doug herty, Transit Dire ctor, c larified the y wer e re questing $784,911.00 in Fede ral
assistance, and City  Gene ral F und Revenue  in the amount of $40 9,130.00.  He a dvised it takes
$1,666,553 to run the bus program, and reviewed various services that are provided.  There being no
further comments and purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
ORDINANCE - Amending, repealing, and creating certain sections of Chapter  10.52, Stopping,
Standing, and Parking Generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming, relating to the Parking of Recreational Vehicles and Trailers – referred to Public Services
Committee (sponsors – Ms. Ash, Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez). 
RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Grenville
Subdivision, First Filing, a replat of the West 44 feet of Lot 3, Lots 4-7 and the East 10 feet of Lot 8,
Block 1, Lake Minnehaha Addition a nd Lots 21- 30, Block 1, Resubdivision of Lake M innehaha
Addition, and the alley between said lots, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to E.
Lincolnway, west of Hot Springs Avenue).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash advised
there was no recommendation on the item from the Committee.  Ms. Ash moved to adopt, seconded by
Ms. Broyles.  It was noted that questions raised at the Committee meeting had been answered by the
City Engineer.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  (#5421)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Re- appropriating the  Fisca l Year  2012 budg et of the City  of Chey enne
according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 and 16-4-113.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
(#5422)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Re- appropriating the  Fisca l Year  2013 budg et of the City  of Chey enne
according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 and 16-4-113.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
(#5423)
RESOLUTION - Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to Approve and Accept the Fiscal Year 2011
Staffing for Ade quate F ire a nd Emerg ency Response (SAF ER) Grant from the United States
Department of Homeland Security in the amount of $846,090.00 (Award No. EMW-2011-FH-00743).
Chief James Martin, Fire and Rescue Department, clarified the grant will provide funding for salaries
and benefits to hire 5 additional firefighters for a 2 year term, noting the additional staff will assist in
providing coverage for increased areas to the City .  Reporting  for F inance Committee, Mr. B rown
moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Chief Martin explained the current Collective
Labor Agreement states there will be a minimum of 88 firefighters and unless the number changes over
the next 2 y ears it will  remain the same.  He  explained the City  will not be obligated to reta in the
additional firefighters at the end of the 2 year term.  Discussion included concerns with expiration of
the grant funding and maintaining the required number of firefighters; Labor Agreement provisions
relative to staffing requirements and future negotiations, and overtime costs.  Upon inquiry, Jon Narva,
Operations Chief, verified the grant allows for the hiring of up to 5 additional firefighters and if less
than 5 were hired the grant funding amount would be reduced accordingly.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5424)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Au thorizing the filing of an a pplication w ith t he F ederal Transit
Administration, an operating administration of the United States Depa rtment of Transportation, for
Federal Transportation Assistance authorized by Title 49, United States Code, Section 5307 (Cheyenne
Transit Program) – referred to Finance Committee (sponsor - Mr. Brown).
[CA] RESOLUTION - Amending Resolution No. 5335 Authorizing the City of Cheyenne, through
the City  Attorney ’s Off ice, to commenc e conde mnation proce edings to obtain certa in temporary
construction easements for the use of certain land generally located at 1900 Converse Avenue necessary
for the c ompletion of the East Pershing  Boule vard Rec onstruction Project – re ferred to F inance
Committee (sponsor – Mr. Collins).
[CA] RESOLUTION - Repe aling Resolution No. 4919;  authori zing the Dire ctor of the  City of
Cheyenne Par ks and Recr eation De partment to  establish fees f or the use of  fac ilities and f or
participation in programs administered by the Recreation, Golf and Leisure Services Divisions; and
directing that certain discounts be made available to City employees, City retirees and their immediate
family members – referred to Finance Committee (sponsor – Ms. Broyles).
RESOLUTION - Repealing Resolution No. 5417 and adopting application, initial and renewal
fees for certain new classifications of City of Cheyenne contractor licenses and building trade licenses
– referred to Finance Committee (sponsor – Mr. Brown).  Mr. Brown advised the item was reviewed
under other items for the agenda at the August 20  Finance Committee meeting and provisions of Titleth
2 of City Code had been met.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Bruce Wilson,
Chief Building Official, explained the purpose of the resolution was to provide for inclusion of a new
Class R  lic ense c lassification, wh ich p ertains to s ingle-family bu ildings, wh ich w as in advertently
omitted from the schedule (Exhibit A) of new licenses and fees submitted with the initial resolution that
was previously adopted.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5425)
[CA]  RESOLUTION - Authorizing submission of a Mineral Royalty Capital Construction Account
Grant (Chapter 3) application to the State Loan and Investment Board for the Street and Alley Road
Maintenance Facility Addition Project, Phase I – referred to Finance Committee (sponsor - Mr. Brown).
[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing submission of a Mineral Royalty Capital Construction Account
Grant (Chapter 3) application to the State Loan and Investment Board on behalf of the Governing Body
of the City of Cheyenne for the Snyder Avenue Sewer Rehabilitation Project from West 24  Street toth
West Pershing Boulevard – referred to Finance Committee (sponsor - Mr. Collins).
 
[CA] RESOLUTION - Prioritizing the City of Cheyenne’s September 2012 applications for Mineral
Royalty Capital Construction Grants – referred to Finance Committee (sponsors - Mr. Brown and Mr.
Collins).
[CA] Juvenile Housing Agreement between Laramie County, Wyoming, and the City of Cheyenne.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5899)
[CA] User Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Parks and Recreation Department, and Cheyenne
Stampede Junior “A” Hockey, LLC, to host the 2012-2013 season at the City-owned Ice and Events
Center.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5900)
[CA] Media Sales Ag ency Ag reement between the City  of Chey enne, Par ks and Recr eation
Department, Cheyenne Stampede Junior “A” Hockey, LLC and SeeHear Media, LLC, for Static Visual
Advertising at the City-owned Ice and Events Center.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5901)
Wind Energy Lease Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Wind, LLC, for
Windpower Facilities on the Belvoir Ranch.  James Perrott Macneil spoke in opposition to any lease,
or sub-lease, agreements involving the Belvoir Ranch property, and expressed Belvoir Ranch ownership
concerns.  Ed Clark expressed c oncerns w ith a esthetic vie ws a nd ph ysical a ppearances r elated to
gateways into the City; restricting the City’s opportunity for growth; initial purposes of acquiring the
ranch, and long  term impact a  wind far m may be on the c ommunity.  Pau l Edner raised que stions
regarding the view being greatly depreciated if there are 150 more wind turbines within sight of the
western part of the City; income to the City during the project’s development time frame; agreement
provisions addressing any default of the lease by the developer and putting the land back to its original
state, and bonding requirements if the company  becomes insolvent.  Mr. Brow n moved to approve ,
seconded by Mr. Collins.  U pon inquiry, Bruce Morley, CEO,  Mo rley Companies (affiliated with
Cheyenne Wind), reviewed terms of the lease relative to revenue to the City once production begins;
sharing of planning  studies information with the City ; bond cover age provision in the lease ,
environmental study impacts, and location and visual impact of the wind turbines.  Discussion topics
included visual impact c oncerns;  ro yalties t o the City  over the  next 30 y ears from wind turbine
production; Ci ty Council work sessions that have  been he ld on the subject, and le ase a greement
provision involving an option to extend the lease time frame.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney,
reviewed terms in the agreement pertaining to the option to extend the term.  Mr. Morley explained that
due to the natur e of de veloping wind power  projects, it t akes a great deal of time to complete  the
planning, studies and permitting processes involved, and spoke in favor of the option to extend the lease
time frame.  Upon inquiry, Mr. White clarified pursuant to the lease agreement the company must
demonstrate they are making reasonable progress during the additional 5 year lease option and will be
required to make annua l payments as outlined in the lease.  He stated his viewpoint that if it is not
shown there is progress on the project then a resolution could be presented to the governing body to
make that determination and to consider terminating the lease.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen provided
information on the interest in a lease agreement expressed by Cheyenne Wind, LLC, and subsequent
discussions resulting in the pr oposed agreement, noting this ty pe of ex clusive agreement does not
require a compe titive bid process.  Mr. White stated his opinion t hat the g overning body has the
discretionary judgment to decide whether or not to enter into the lease agreement with the company.
Mayor Ka ysen re viewed the  proce ss that resulted in pre paration of the lease  ag reement with the
company, which included utilization of outside legal counsel specializing in wind energy development
agreements, and noted similar State of Wyoming  leases were also reviewed in conjunction with drafting
the proposed lease agreement.  Discussion followed on factors involving the ascertainment of a bonding
amount; whether additional bonding could be required, and whether development of the project was
contingent upon any Congressional tax credit action.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Morley addressed questions
regarding the matter of any Federal wind power tax credits, and discussed Wyoming’s capacity to
generate wind energy.  Tom Zachary questioned whether communication had been made with the Corps
of Engineers regarding the proposed area to be leased relative to restoration of the former missile site
on the property.  Mr. Morley responded that they have interfaced extensively with the Cheyenne Board
of Public Utilities, U.S. Army Corps of Engineers and Wyoming DEQ and advised it has been agreed
that one project won’t interfere with the other and if it is determined there is a ground water pollution
area, then the project won’t extend into that area.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body with the exception of Ms. Ash voting “no”. (#5902)
 
[CA] Cooperative Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming Department of Transportation
and To urism Pr omotion Join t Po wers B oard fo r a  ra ilroad q uiet zon e pr oject a t th e S outhwest
Drive/Union Pacific Railroad Crossing (2007-2010 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Use Ag reement betwee n the City  of Chey enne a nd Creative O utdoor Adver tising g iving
permission to install advertising bus shelter benches and pads within and along the City’s Public Rights
of Way – referred to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and AVI to develop the Snyder
Avenue reconstruction design (2007-2010 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 5725 between the City of Cheyenne and Reiman
Corporation, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the East Cheyenne Greenway Phase IV Project (1% Specific
Purpose Sales & Use Tax) – referred to Finance Committee.
 
[CA] Consideration of B id #M-2-13 for  a contr act be tween the City of Chey enne, Par ks and
Recreation Administration, and Pioneer Construction Co., I nc., Ch eyenne, Wyoming, for the ne w
concessions and toilet facilities for Pioneer Park Project (State and Local Grants funding).  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $308,609.00). (#5903)
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-4-13 for furnishing bulk Ice Control Product to the City of Cheyenne,
Public Works Department (2007-2010, 2011-2014 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried
(accept bid from Desert Mountain Corporation, Riverton, WY; amount not to exceed $114,880.00).
[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-5-13 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, 1% Construction,
and CBJ Asphalt and Concrete Contractors, LLC, Phoenix, Arizona, for Fiscal Year 2013 Crack Seal
Project (2007-2010 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee.
Appointment of Michael A. Metzler to the Contractor Licensing Board.  Mr. Collins moved to
approve, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointment).
[CA] Request by retail liquor licensee One East Sixteenth, LLC, 1601 Central Avenue, for a 90 day
extension of the time frame c ondition placed on the lice nse pertaining to a  lease agreement with a
restaurant establishment to operate within the building.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (approved
to extend the time frame to October 23, 2012).
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing  to be held September 10, 2012 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a transfer of ownership and transfer
of location for a retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for Safeway Stores 46,
Inc. d/b/a Safeway Store #2975, 305 Cole Shopping Center, Cheyenne, Wyoming (License currently held
by Lambousis Revocable Trust, 112 W. 17  Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming).th
Announcement was made that due to the L abor Day holiday on Monday, September 3, 2012,
regularly scheduled City Council Committee Meetings will instead be held in the Municipal Building,
Room 122, 2 101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance Committee will meet
Tuesday, S eptember 4 , 2 012 a t 1 1:00 a .m. a nd P ublic S ervices C ommittee w ill m eet W ednesday,
September 5, 2012 at Noon.
Under other business:
Barbie Harrington questioned the amount of money the City has spent to date on attorney fees
regarding the “taking” involving the Hollywood Video property.  Dan White, City Attorney, advised
legal fees incurred are approaching $30,000 and that negotiations are continuing, noting a right-of-entry
hearing is scheduled for Nov. 26, 27 and 28 in district court.  Ms. Ha rrington commented on re cent
governing body approval of a bicycle route plan and questioned if it could be repealed as there appeared
to be a lot of “tentacles” in the plan.  She stated her concerns with public awareness of the lengthy plan
document.  Upon Ms. Harrington’s inquiry to Ms. Ash, Ms. Ash explained the document is simply a plan
for guidance, which may be reviewed/revised in the future, and allows for the capability to apply for
grant funds.  Ms. Ha rrington further commented on r evoking the Uni form Development Code, and
expressed concerns with property rights and public awareness; Belvoir Ranch exclusive lease agreement,
and recently reported $3,000,000 in funds that  was  “found” in the City ’s budget.  May or Kaysen
confirmed the $3,000,000 amount was not “lost”; it was a budgeted amount and is the difference in what
was available funding in last year’s budget vs. what was expended.
Pursuant to comment regarding weed removal on City owned property near the intersection of
Converse and Pershing, Mayor Kaysen advised that maintenance of the property was done earlier this
season and he would address further work regarding the weeds. 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, Mayor Kaysen adjourned
the meeting at 7:23 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 September 10, 2012

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 September 10, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins,  Dr. Rinne (arriving at 6:01 p.m.) and Mr.
Spiker.  Absent: Mr. Pierson and Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff,
City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  Members of Boy Scout Troop 116 present at the meeting
led the pledge of allegiance.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Ms. Ash, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Collins
and Ms. Broyles, agenda item #22(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Collins moved to
approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Governing Body on Aug ust 27, 2012.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown  declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a  voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Services  and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Spiker  moved to approve  payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present in Council C hambers.  Following  announcement o f the vote, Mr . Brown
returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public meeting for the purpose of receiving comments and input on a
transfer of ownership and transfer of location for a retail liquor license application filed with the City
Clerk's Office for Safeway Stores 46, I nc. d/b/a Safeway Store #2975, 305 Cole Shopping Center ,
Cheyenne, Wyoming (License c urrently he ld b y Lambousis R evocable Tr ust, 1 12 W. 1 7  Str eet,th
Cheyenne, Wy oming).  J ohn Sundahl, attorney  repr esenting Safew ay Stores, ref erenced thr ee
documents he  ha d p rovided Co uncil m embers; in troduced staff members of  Sa feway Sto res in
attendance at the meeting, and reviewed the proposed location and size of building where they plan to
transfer the liquor license  for the pur pose of operating a package liquor store.  He noted Safeway’s
support to various org anizations, agencies and a ctivities and commitm ent to the community , and
advised the Board of Adjustment recently approved a conditional use permit for the type of business
proposed at the location.  J eff F ergot, D esign M anager a nd C orporate A rchitect f or S afeway,
commented on the site r eview and clarified they have a n architect engaged to do re novations for 2
buildings that will re main on the site, and a dvised a pha sing plan is in place.  He  reviewed square
footage of the building and exterior improvements planned for the package liquor store, and stated the
store will provide a convenience for their customers and shoppers in the area.   Jay Gomez, Safeway
District Manager, reviewed the store’ s liquor training  for staf f, which inc ludes TIPS training, a nd
enforcement policies, and reviewed security measures that will be in place.  Mr. Sundahl further advised
as part of the Board of Adjustment application process they were required to notify individuals that live
in the area of the time and place of the Board of Adjustment mee ting.  Upon inquiry , Mr. Sundahl
confirmed Safeway has an existing lease in place for the property proposed for the package liquor store
and if the license is approved and Safeway should want to move the license to a different location in
the future, an application requesting a change of location is required to come before the governing body.
Larry Proietti, Foy Jolly, and Major Dorr spoke in opposition to the transfer of location and ownership,
raising the following concerns: Alcohol sales in a g rocery store envir onment; past g overning body
actions denying similar application requests; competitive concerns involving grocery stores vs. liquor
stores; negative alcohol environment relative to youth presence, and concerns with setting a precedent
by issuing a retail liquor license to a grocery store applicant and the proposed location being adjacent
to a residential neighborhood.  Annette Williams requested information on how near-by residents were
notified of the proposed package store relative to the Board of Adjustment process and requested a copy
of the letter that Safeway issued.  John Lambousis spoke in support of the transfer commenting on his
years involved in the liquor business, Safeway’s plans to o perate a small scale site, in a separate
building with its own entrance/exit, and Safeway’s plans to redevelop the area, noting the opportunity
for additional community businesses and sales tax benefits.  Jan Nelson-Schroll spoke in support of the
concept of revitalizing the shopping center area and Safeway’s re-development plans, which will not
intrude into the residential neighborhood.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Sundahl provided further information on
the notifications to residents in the a rea, pursuant to the B oard of Adjustment process, and content
contained in the notices, noting notifications were issued twice due to the matter being continued before
the Board due to the lack of a quor um.  Pursuant to Ms. W illiams’ previous request, he advised he
would provide a copy of the notification that was issued to residents to the Development Office to share
with Ms. Williams.  Com ments made by governing body members included inquiries pertaining to
leasing of the property, potential future re-location of the license, and concerns with youth and domestic
challenges currently observed in the suring residential neighborhood.  There being no further comments
and purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the
application to Finance Committee.
ORDINANCE - Amending, repealing, and creating certain sections of Chapter  10.52, Stopping,
Standing, and Parking Generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming, relating to the Parking of Recreational Vehicles and Trailers.  The following individuals
spoke in reference to the ordinance:  Joe McKee, Walter Barber, Peggy Hehr, Scott Dominick, Glenn
Crock, Connie Cranmore, John Dunlay, Charlie Giroux, Frank Cole, Frank Cross, Tom Zachary, M.
Lee Hasenauer an d Ba rbie H arrington.  T opics p resented an d d iscussed i ncluded:  Al lowing a
reasonable amount of time for RVs (recreational vehicles) or trailers to be parked on streets, especially
in neighborhoods with narrow street widths; being able to park an RV in front of your own residence;
utilization of off-site RV storage facilities; concerns with neighbors parking multiple vehicles, including
but not limited to RVs, on the street which occupy street space in front of other residents’ homes;
current parking restrictions involving a required distance (i.e. 10 ft.) from the street centerline and how
many streets in the City are affected by the distance; safety hazards regarding other visual obstructions
such as trees and bushes blocking drivers’ view at intersections; legally parked vehicles; “eye-sore” vs.
safety issues pertaining to intent of the ordinance; width of RVs vs. various other types of vehicles;
definition of an RV relative to large sized vehicles (ex: Peterbilt) converted for use as an RV; allowing
property owners to determine who parks in front of their home and being courteous neighbors; standards
for street widths outlined in the Unified Development Code (UDC); uniform enforcement concerns;
whether the ordinanc e, if a pproved, is elig ible for a referendum by petition process, a nd g eneral
perception that the proposed or dinance punishes RV/trail er owners.  Dur ing comments and upon
inquiry, Nathan Beauheim, Assistant City Engineer, stated he estimated that any City street built prior
to the WWII time frame probably doesn’t have the required 10 ft. parking distance  width and provided
information generally on where many of these streets are located, noting he would try to compile further
information pursuant to the inquiry for the next Public Services Committee meeting.  He further advised
the standard street width is 36 ft. per  the UDC.  Upon inquiry , Dan White, City Attorney, reviewed
current City Code requirements involving the 10 ft. distance for streets that have centerline pavement
markings vs. changes in the proposed ordinance.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Ms. Ash moved to amend by substitute
(dated 9-5-12), seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon query, Mr. White reviewed the changes contained in
the proposed substitute involving the penalty clause, parking distance from railroad crossings and 30
ft. distance f rom intersec tions, and expl ained the distance scenar io of “bump out” c urb lines at
intersections.  Mr. Giroux questioned the penalty provisions with Police Chief Brian Kozak responding
that any penalty imposed would be through the court’s determination regarding setting of a bond and
penalty amount.  James Perrott Macneil commented on problems with corners and visibility; t hat
owners have a financial investment in their purchase and shouldn’t be required to store their vehicles
off-site, and spoke in opposition to regulated parking.  Ms. Broyles raised a point of order stating her
belief that comments being made were not relevant to the motion on the floor, and noted the ordinance
does allow for people to park RVs in front of their own homes and does not require them to privately
store their vehicles.  Ms.  Ash moved to call for the question, seconded by Mr. Brown.  M r. White
advised the call for the question is a motion to limit debate and requires a 2/3rd’s affirmative vote to
pass.  Motion failed.  Voting  “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. B rown, Ms. Broyles and Dr. Rinne.
Voting “no” -- Mr. Collins, Mr. Spiker and Mayor Kaysen.  Discussion followed on the minimum 10
ft. distance from street centerline, or centerline pavement marking, relative to safety concerns that some
streets are not wide enough for emergency response vehicles if RVs/tra ilers as well as other motor
vehicles are parked on both sides of the street, and concern with neighbor/neighborhood conflicts.
Motion to amend by substitute failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body present.  Ms.
Ash moved to amend by  substitute (dated 9-7-2012), seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon inquiry, Mr.
White advised the substitute corrects punctuation errors; reinserted a provision that states it is illegal
to park where official signs prohibit parking, and added language allowing RVs or trailers that have a
width of 36 or fewer feet to be parked on a street for a period of 72 hours for the purposes of loading
and unloading and provided further that “slide outs” of a RV may not be deployed during loading and
unloading.  Mr. Dominick commented on sa fety regarding emergency response vehicles a nd street
passage width involving an RV parked in the street within the 72 hour time frame.  Comments followed
regarding safety concerns involving street width relative to the 72 hours allowable time frame; safety
vs. aesthetic issue, and 30 ft. parking distance from any point formed by an intersection as outlined in
the pr oposed o rdinance.  U pon inqui ry, Ch ief K ozak r eviewed v isibility lim itations wi thin a  sit e
distance triangle at intersections and advised this provision would help reduce crashes, particularly at
uncontrolled intersections.  Chief Kozak discussed diagrams he had provided showing examples of site
distance triangles and parked vehicles’ effect on driver visibility, and noted, if this provision goes into
effect, it would be actively enforced and the public would need to be educated on the requirement.
Upon inq uiry regarding e nforcement o f t he 72  ho ur pa rking lim itation, Chi ef K ozak a dvised th is
restriction would be hard to be actively enforced and would require dialogue and contact with RV/trailer
owners.  Discussion followed on favorable aspects of the 30 ft. parking distance at intersections; parking
distance from fire hydrants and driveways; suggestion that two separate ordinances could be considered
addressing safety vs. RV parking issues, and whether any existing handicap parking spaces may violate
the 30 ft. re quirement.  Mr. A llen moved to amend Sec tion 10.52.030 by striking the phr ase “for a
period of seventy-two (72) hours” and Section 10.52.200 A. 3. by adding the phrase “excluding Class
B motor homes” at the end of the sentence, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Commenting on the proposed
amendment were:  Mr. Dominick, Gary Freeman, Mr. Hasenauer, Mr. Zachary and Victor Ciccone.
Topics discussed included: Size of Class B motor homes; enforcement of 5 ft. parking distance from
driveways; addressing shrubbery at intersections as a safety hazard; whether an ordinance regarding a
distance restriction is needed vs. curb painting; potential additional law enforcement personnel needs,
and inclusion of motor coaches with 10 wheels relative to the definition of a recreational vehicle.
During comments, Chief Kozak provided information on enforcement of the 5 ft. parking distance from
driveways, stating this portion of the ordinance will not change much with regard to curr ent
enforcement.  Comments followed in opposition to removing the 72 hour time frame and the proposed
amendment generally.  Motion to amend the proposed substitute failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms.
Broyles and Dr . Rinne.  Voting “ no” -- Ms. Ash, Mr. Br own, Mr. Col lins, Mr. Spiker and Ma yor
Kaysen.  Motion to amend by  substitute (dated 9-7-12) carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Collins voti ng “no.”  Main motion to approve on
second reading as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body
present with the exception of Mr. Collins voting “no.”
Following a 10 minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:22 p.m.

[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the filing of  a n a pplication w ith the  F ederal Transit
Administration, an operating administration of the U nited States Department of Transportation, for
Federal Transportation Assistance authorized by Title 49, United States Code, Section 5307 (Cheyenne
Transit Program).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5426)
RESOLUTION - Amending Resolution No. 5335 Authorizing the City of Cheyenne, through
the City  Attorney ’s Off ice, to commenc e conde mnation proce edings to obtain certa in temporary
construction easements for the use of certain land generally located at 1900 Converse Avenue necessary
for the completion of the East Pershing Boulevard Reconstruction Project.  The following individuals
spoke on the proposed resolution:   John Palmer, Eric Miller, Barbie Harrington, Gary Freeman, Annette
Williams and M. Lee Hasenauer.  Topics presented and discussed included: Opposition to the City’s
utilization of the eminent domain process generally; alternative options for roadway and negotiations;
judicial decision-making involvement; suggestion of a different design for the proposed roundabout at
the Pershing/ Converse int ersection (made  by  Mr. Miller who provide d and discussed a  handout
depicting a roundabout design utilized in New Jersey); traffic counts at the intersection of Pershing and
Converse; cost of the roundabout project; conce rn with taking  property and setting  a pre cedent of
concern to property owners; request to reconsider moving forward with the eminent domain process;
notification to citizens and representation of constituents; property needed for the project and the City’s
UDC.  Pursuant to inquiry during comments, Nathan Beauheim, Assistant City Engineer, reviewed
traffic counts for the Pershing/Converse intersection over a period of years; provided information on
estimated costs of the r oundabout project; explained the two phases consisting of the East Pershing
Reconstruction Project and funding sources, which does not involve federal funds for the roundabout
portion.  Upon further inquiry, Mayor Kaysen clarified discussions have been taking place between the
property owner’s legal representative and the City.  During comments regarding the UDC and whether
it is a ffiliated with “Ag enda 21", Mr. Collins raised a point of orde r that the c omments were not
germane with Mayor Kaysen ruling that comments should pertain to the motion on the floor.  In
response to previous comments regarding design of the proposed roundabout, Mr. Beauheim indicated
the needs of fire department vehicles and semi-trucks were taken into consideration during the design.
He e xplained the purp ose of the re solution i s to add temporar y construction ea sements into the
proceeding to allow for “blending” of project improvements, citing curb and gutter as an example, and
noted if the resolution did not pass it would mean improvements would be cut-off at the property line.
Upon inquiry by Mr. Miller regarding the diameter dimension (outside) of the roundabout, Mr .
Beauheim stated he did not have that information with him at the meeting.  Mayor Kaysen requested
Mr. Beauheim follow up with the request.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown stated there
was no recommendation on the item from the Committee.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.
Collins.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Beauheim explained the resolution is to seek permission to make certain
improvements such as curb, gutter and sidewalk that will separate the property from the parking area
and, at the completion of the improvements, access to the property goes away and it is relinquished back
to the property owner, noting the proposed resolution is different from the previous resolution involving
eminent domain to acquire property for the roundabout.  U pon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney,
expressed his impression that the proper ty owner  has no objec tion to the temporar y construction
easements as he has not heard of any objection from the property owner’s attorney, and that, pursuant
to the initial eminent domain resolution and resulting complaint filed involving the Hollywood Video
site, a November 26-28 hearing date has been scheduled.  He explained issues the City must prove at
the hearing (i.e. public convenience and necessity) and judicial determinations involved; reviewed the
amount of the last off er made by the City  to the proper ty owner prior to filing  the conde mnation
complaint, and discussed the additional temporary construction easement involving approximately
1,700 sq. ft. which would be used to “blend” the property.  He noted his understanding that the City
does not need the easements to build the reconstruction project; that other easements have been acquired
along Pershing Blvd. for the project, and that the easements are a benefit to the land owner as it enables
blending of improvements.  Upon query, Mr. Beauheim confirmed after work within the easement areas
are done, the pr operty owner  retai ns ownership of their ar ea, a nd Mr. White verified se ttlement
negotiations with the property owner can still proceed.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body present. (#5427)
RESOLUTION - Repe aling Resolution No. 4919; authoriz ing the  Direc tor of the City  of
Cheyenne Par ks and Recr eation De partment to establish fe es for  the us e of facilities and for
participation in programs administered by the Recreation, Golf and Leisure Services Divisions; and
directing that certain discounts be made available to City employees, City retirees and their immediate
family members.  Mr. B rown mo ved to adopt, sec onded by Mr. Spiker. Upon inquiry  reg arding
potential effect on the budget due to discounts, Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, stated it is
unknown at this time whether discounts will affect budget revenues as it is hard to determine how many
eligible persons would participate.  Comments followed on the potential for additional memberships
the City could gain due to the discounts and affordability for employees; beneficial effects that may be
realized in tandem with the Employee Wellness Program; giving the discount program a “pilot try”;
concern with putting the discount program in place and scenario of possibly having to remove it in the
future; annual reporting requirement outlined in the resolution, and wording that states no fee increase
will become e ffective prior to the City ’s next fiscal y ear and be ba sed upon the Western U rban
Consumer Price Index.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Parish clarified that currently fees, except golf fees, are set
by the department director and commented on fees tied to the consumer price index literally vs. “ing”
of amounts.  It was noted that wording in Section 2. States “ ... the Director may, at his or her discretion,
increase the amount of any such fee ...”.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend Section 2. to  delete the balance
of the sentence following the words “fiscal year,” seconded by Mr. Collins.  I t was verified that the
proposed amendment would result in the sentence reading as follows:  “The Director of the Parks and
Recreation Department shall conduct a n annual review of fees charged by the Recreation, Golf and
Leisure Services Divisions and the Director may, at his or her discretion, increase the amount of any
such fee, provided however, that no fee increase shall become effective prior to the commencement of
the City’s next fiscal year.”  Discussion followed on c urrent department fees, other than golf, being
determined administratively by the Dire ctor; concern with employee discounts; elements involving
employee benefit packages generally and current employee investment study, which is not addressing
the benefits areas; employee turnover, and support for the Employee Wellness Program.  Motion to
amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr.
Allen and Mr. Brown voting “no”.  It was noted that discounts would not apply to team sports and
events nor to the City’s elected officials.  Upon inquiry regarding the April 1  requirement for an annualst
report, Mr. Parish advised adequate numbers for golf for the first report would not be available since
the sea son is winding down, a nd that other a ctivities could be monitored throug hout the y ear.
Comments followed on acquiring historical fee data and purpose of the report.  Mr. Collins moved to
amend by deleting Section 6. (which removes the requirement to submit an annual report by April 1st
each year ), seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Upon inquiry regarding tracking of discount fee areas, Mr. Parish
provided information on what’s in place to track certain, but not all, golf users, adding that swimming
users can be tracked if employees show their City I.D., and explained other types of programs that
would be difficult to track   Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body present with the exception of Mr. Brown voting “no”.  Main motion as twice amended carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Brown and Dr.
Rinne voting “no”. (#5428)

Announcement was made of the Appointment of Dave Ohde  to the Downtown Development
Authority Board, to be confirmed September 24, 2012.
Under other business:
Upon request, City Clerk Carol Intlekofer read an Announcement of a Public Hearing to be held
at 6:00 p.m. on Monday, September 24, 2012 in Cit y Council Chambers, 2101 O’N eil Avenue ,
Cheyenne, Wyoming, to receive public comment regarding the intent of the City of Cheyenne to submit
an application for funding to the Wyoming Business Council’s Business Ready Community, Business
Committed Grant Program, to assist Microsoft in establishing a low-impact, renewable energy project
in Cheyenne.
Barbie Harrington commented on agenda item #14 (Resolution to commence condemnation
proceedings to obtain certain temporary construction easements for use at 1900 Converse Avenue) and
queried governing body members with regard to their vote and discussions regarding the Resolution.
Upon her inquiry, Fire Chief Jim M artin advised the City’s largest fire truck will be able to safely
navigate the proposed roundabout to be built at the Pershing/Converse intersection.
Brad Harrington provided a handout regarding the City’s utilization of federal and state dollars
and intergovernmental money (i.e. sales tax revenues) as part of the budget, and read a paragraph from
a past article in the Wyoming Tribune Eagle regarding the City’s budget. He expressed concerns with
using grant money and effect on the City’s budget if federal funding were reduced.
Eric Miller que stioned how pede strians would cross the r oundabout to be built at
Pershing/Converse int ersection a nd response was p rovided b y  Na than Beauheim, As sistant C ity
Engineer, who advised a pedestrian crosses a roundabout the same way they would cross any other
pedestrian crossing.  Upon further inquiry by Mr. Miller regarding what occurs when an emergency
vehicle approaches the roundabout, Mr. Beauheim explained existing traffic would exit the roundabout
the same way you would exit any intersection when an emergency vehicle is coming, which would be
to exist the roundabout and pull over to the side of the road.
M. L ee H asenauer e choed c omments previously  expressed by  Mr. Har rington reg arding
potential future revenue concern relative to federal funding, and commented on PlanCheyenne, property
rights, the Unified De velopment Code (UDC) and process to repeal it, and questioned w hether the
City’s UDC was related to “Agenda 21".  Mayor Kaysen advised the UDC is not linked to “Agenda
21"; explained the code will undergo annual reviews involving public meetings, and stated on-going
discussions and review of the code with developers and contractors are occurring and the public will
have the opportunity to provide input on any proposed changes to the code.  A request was made to Mr.
Hasenauer that he submit UDC items he found to be objectionable.
John Palmer requested documents being discussed at the City Council meetings be  projected
on a screen so everyone could see them, and c ommented on the public participation format during
meetings.  It was noted that discussions are being held regarding use of visual media at meetings.  
William Be nnett expressed conc erns r egarding a construc tion project af fecting on- street
customer pa rking in front of his and his wife ’s re staurant business near the Per shing/Snyder
intersection.  Upon inquiry regarding the project, Nathan Beauheim, Assistant City Engineer, advised
that construction is suppose to be completed in that area some time this week.  Mr. Beauheim advised
they did let the contra ctor know they were not happy with the defe ctive product and he a dvised he
would follow up on it.
Eric Miller questioned adopting the International Code.  James Perrott Macneil commented on
roundabouts located on the east coast and how they are used and spoke in opposition to the roundabout
proposed for Pershing/Converse intersection and using eminent domain to get the land.  He  further
commented on his lineage and advised he disagreed with the resolution to give employees reduced fees
for city recreational activities. 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, Mayor Kaysen adjourned
the meeting at 10:41  p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

 September 21, 2012
