RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 September 24, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney. 
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the a genda.)  Mr. Collins moved to appr ove Consent Ag enda items, sec onded by Mr.
Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 10, 2012.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez each declared a conflict of interest (business interest in vouchers
payable to Amer i-Tech HVAC Service s and Radio Station KYOY F M respe ctively) and va cated
Council Chambers.  Mr. Spiker  moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr.
Allen.  Mo tion c arried.  Vo ting “ yes” -- all m embers of  the  g overning bo dy pr esent i n Co uncil
Chambers.  F ollowing announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez returned to Council
Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public meeting for the purpose of receiving  public comment regarding
the intent of the City of Cheyenne to s ubmit an application for funding to t he Wyoming Business
Council’s Business Ready Community, Business Committed Grant Program, to assist Microsoft in
establishing a low-impact, renewable energy project in Cheyenne.  Cindy Schneider, Grants Manager,
advised the purpose of the grant request, which is $1.5 million, is to provide infrastructure to facilitate
the Microsoft Data Plant Demonstration Project to be located at the Board of Public Utilities (BOPU)
Dry Creek Water Reclamation Facility.  She stated the project is a public-private partnership and, if the
grant is awarded, it will pay for 20% of the estimated $7.6 million project and that the City is not being
asked to fund any of the proje ct.  Ms. Schneider  referenced an affiliated resolution on the meeting
agenda which will be reviewed at the Oct. 1  Finance Committee meeting with anticipated resolutionst
action taken at the Oct. 8  City Council meeting.  Anja Bendel, Development Director for Cheyenneth
LEADS, clarified that the project is not related to Microsoft’s data center project being constructed west
of the City , and provide d additional projec t information, advising  that Microsoft is exploring
opportunities to reduce their carbon footprint by using renewable resources for its data centers.  She
noted project participants who are entering into a memorandum of understanding related to the project
are:  Microsoft Corp., FuelCell Energy, Inc., BOPU, Cheyenne LEADS, Western Research Institute,
Wyoming Business Council, Cheyenne Light, Fuel and Power and the University of Wyoming.  She
stated the fuel cell that will be installed will use methane produced from the biodigester to generate
electricity to run the data server, and that at the end of the demonstration project time frame (i.e. 18
months), the parties involved will determine the viability of continuing the project.  There being no
further comments and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
ORDINANCE - Amending, repealing, and creating certain sections of Chapter 10.52, Stopping,
Standing, and Parking Generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming, relating to the Parking of Recreational Vehicles and Trailers.  The following individuals
spoke in reference to the ordinance: Mike Rosenthal, Steven Miller, Fr ank Cole, Paige Smith, Mike
Schulte, Ed Melko, John Palmer, Tom Zachary and Jeannine Stallings.  Topics presented and discussed
included: Community health, safety and welfare issues relative to timely emergency vehicle response
access; concerns with allowing an indefinite parking time frame and limiting public access generally
to rights-of-way; parking of recreational vehicles (RVs) and trailers elsewhere than on streets; 10 ft.
street width minimum concerns; reservations that approving the ordinance will lead to future on-street
parking restrictions; whether the City will be painting curbs to designate the required 30 ft. setback from
intersections; authority of City Engineer pertaining to allowable or restricted parking pursuant to other
sections of City Code; parking distance concerns involving the 30 ft. setback and space limitations for
residents to park in front of their own property; pedestrian and driver safety concerns at intersections,
and ordina nce e nforcement.  Dur ing disc ussion, information was provided c oncerning pr ovisions
contained in a proposed substitute ordinance.  Upon inquiry, Nathan Beauheim, Assistant City Engineer,
stated it would be the City ’s intent to paint curbs delineating intersection 30 ft. setback a reas in the
Avenues due to the high volume of vehicles parking in the area during Frontier Days.  Reporting for
Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms.
Broyles.  Ms. Ash moved to amend by substitute ordinance (dated 9-18-12), seconded by Ms. Broyles.
Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, reviewed changes contained in the proposed substitute, stating
the principal change was language regarding the parking of RVs and trailers on the street directly in
front of or adjacent to the property owned or occupied by the owner of the RV or trailer, and confirmed
there were no changes proposed to existing City Code Section 10.52.030 involving the 10 ft. width of
roadway, as measured from the centerline pavement marking.  Mr. Schulte commented on the restricted
parking area if someone lives on a triangle near intersections with regard to the 30 ft. setback.  Motion
to amend by substitute failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body.  Ms. Broyles moved
to amend by substitute ordinance (dated 9-24-12), seconded by Ms. Ash.  Upon inquiry, Mr. White
advised the pr oposed substitute changes the de finition of rec reational vehicle to a mor e simplified
definition, taken from the American National Standards Institute, and would read as follows: “ ... any
vehicular-type unit that is primarily designed as a temporary living quarters for recreational, camping,
or seasonal use; has its own motive power or is mounted on or towed by another vehicle; and is
regulated by the National Highway Traffic Safety Administration as a vehicle or vehicle equipment.”
He stated other sections of the ordi nance were the same a s the substitute version recommended for
approval at the Sept. 18  Public Services Committee meeting.  Comments followed on safety concerns,th
particularly involving emergency vehicle response and accessible street width, and importance of the
“sight triangle”.  Mr. Pierson moved to amend the proposed substitute by adding the following sentence
at the end of Section 10.52.020, Prohibited in specified places, Subsection B. 4., seconded by Ms.
Broyles:  “Exception: driver’s personal private driveway.”.  Upon inquiry regarding the history of the
current City Code restriction that does not allow parking within 5 ft. of either side of a driveway, Brian
Kozak, Chief of Police, e xplained the distance provides safety to drivers’ who are backing out of a
driveway by having more vision of the area, and advised active enforcement of the provision is mostly
the result of complaints received from an affected property owner.  Motion to amend the substitute
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Discussion followed on the purpose of
the  ordinance as initially introduced relative to safety vs. aesthetic issues; re striction of only being
allowed to park in front of your own property; concern with curb painting of the 30 ft. intersection
distance only in the Avenues and not other locations in the City; City Engineer’s authority to restrict
parking areas under other provisions of City Code, and the ordinance providing an improvement to
address RV on-street parking concerns.  During discussion, Mr. Beauheim advised curb painting of the
30 ft . distance in the A venues is to assist with the amount of traf fic/parking in the r esidential
neighborhood during Frontier Days and other areas are not planned to be painted at this time due to the
lack of funding resources.  Motion to amend by substitute dated 9-24-12, as amended, carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Brown voting “no”.  Upon query,
Chief Kozak provided information on enforcement procedures involving different potential violation
scenarios, and clarified fines would be set through Municipal Court.  It was noted the ordinance, if
approved, will not be in effect until Feb. 1, 2013.  Main motion as amended by substitute, as amended,
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Brown and Mr.
Valdez voting “no”. (#3957) 
ORDINANCE - Vacating a Portion of the Right-of-Way of Bradley Avenue at the Intersection
of Bradley Avenue and East 14th Street.  Ms. Ash moved to approve on second reading, seconded by
Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. 
ORDINANCE - Pursua nt to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map A mendment, Section 5.1.2 Z oning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Of ficial Z oning Ma p of the Unifie d Deve lopment C ode,
changing the zoning classification from MR-1 Medium-Density Residential Established District to
MUB Mixed-Use Bus iness Emphasis District for B lock 16, Park Addition, Chey enne, Wyoming
(located between Richardson and Cosgriff Courts and between Ames Court and Dey Avenue).  Ms. Ash
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon inquiry, Casey Palma, Steil
Surveying Services, agent for the  applicant, e xplained the zone change request is to enable g reater
flexibility of uses for the property and stated potentially the building may be subdivided for use as office
or educational spaces.  He commented that certain uses may require administrative approval and some
would be allowable under the MUB classification.  I n response to inquiry regarding concern with
protection of the neighborhood involving certain types of allowable business uses, Jim Flesher, Planning
Dept., a dvised c ertain u ses ma y in volve o ther r estrictions a nd a pprovals p ursuant t o th e U nified
Development Code, and Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, advised staff would provide additional
information prior to the next meeting, if the ordinance moves forward, on scenarios involving potential
types of uses that would be allowed vs. those requiring administrative or Board of Adjustment approval.
In response to inquiry regarding parking relative to development of town homes on the northern portion
of the site, Mr. Palma provided information on parking standards that would be met and referenced the
conceptual site plan and parking options being considered.  Comments followed in support of the “in-
fill” redevelopment project which will transform a vacant lot and building into a viable use, and that
proposed business use(s) should compliment the neighborhood.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”-- all
members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - Pursua nt to Section 2.2.1 Zoning  Map Amendme nt, Section 5.1.2 Z oning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Of ficial Z oning Ma p of the Unif ied Development Code,
changing the  Z oning Cla ssification f rom MUB M ixed-Use B usiness E mphasis Di strict to  CB
Community Busines s District for L ots 12-22, Bloc k 656, Orig inal City  (AKA  South C heyenne
Addition), Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at 504 Central Avenue).  Ms. Ash moved to approve on second
reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon inquiry, Jim Flesher, Planning Dept., provided information
on the zone change notification process to those in the area required by the Unified Development Code,
and clarified the property owner applied for the zone change and any communications involving use
of the property is between the property owner and owner of the current business on the site.  He further
advised a MUB zone does allow for a retail store but would not allow the same building design that is
being proposed due to the setback distance necessary due to the floodplain and confirmed, upon inquiry,
that the retail business proposed to be located on the site is a Family Dollar Store.  Brandon Cammarata,
Planning and Deve lopment Direc tor, explained the reque sted zone chang e is consistent with the
comprehensive plan; advised the area is located “at the tail end” of a MUB district and that surrounding
blocks have changed over time to single use  development, and noted a MUB  zone has a maximum
setback distance (generally 10 ft.).  Discussion followed on the  slope of the lot and setback distance
relative to the floodplain under a CB zone.  Dillon Tidwell, applicant representative, advised there are
issues involving location of the building on the site relative to the setback and floodplain, and it i s
intended to have the site design compatible with the area and have the building more visible by facing
Central Avenue.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - Pursua nt to Section 2.2.1 Zoning  Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2 Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Of ficial Z oning Ma p of the Unifi ed Development Code,
changing the  Z oning Cla ssification f rom MU B Mixe d-Use B usiness E mphasis District to  CB
Community Business District for a Portion of Lot 2, Block 3, Plaza Del Range, Cheyenne, Wyoming
(located at 3308 Dell Range Boulevard).  Ms. Ash moved to approve on second reading, seconded by
Ms. Broyles.  Upon inquiry, Jim Flesher, Planning Dept., stated there are similar issues as discussed for
the previous ordinance on the agenda (i.e. setback distances; floodplain) regarding the business site
design p roposed f or the  pr operty a nd the  request t o r e-zone.  M otion c arried.  Vo ting “ yes”-- a ll
members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - Creating Section 6.08.180  Feeding Geese and Ducks Prohibited, of Chapter
6.08, Animal Care and Control Regulations Generally, of Title 6, Animals, of the Code of the City of
Cheyenne, Wyoming -- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsors -- Ms. Ash and Mr. Collins).

Professional Service s Ag reement betwee n the City  of Chey enne, Par ks and Recr eation
Department, and Ayres Associates, Inc. for engineering and bid documents for Lake Minnehaha in
Holliday Park, Remediation Phase I (2013 - 2014 State Land & Investment Board funding) -- referred
to Finance Committee.  Mr. Brown reported the item was reviewed under other items for the agenda
at the Sept. 17, 2012 Finance Committee meeting and provisions of Title 2 of City Code had been met.
Mr. Brown moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $83,580.00, seconded by Mr. Spiker. Rick
Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, explained the reason for requesting review of the item under
other items for the agenda at Finance Committee involves a timing issue with the schedule originally
submitted for design engineering which would have taken them out of the winter months for dredging
of the lake , and stated dredging needs to be a ccomplished during the cooler winter weather. Upon
inquiry, he provided information on the anticipated depth of the lake (8-12 ft.) when the dredging is
completed, proposed solar or electric water recirculating system, and the three phases to the project,
noting they are working on partnerships to provide some lake shore enhancements.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5911)

snow plows for the Street and Alley Division (2011- 2014 1% Sales Tax) - - referred t o Fi nance
Committee.
[CA] Consideration o f B id # R-3-13 fo r f urnishing a nd ins talling miscellaneous Po lice Ve hicle
Equipment for the Cheyenne Police Department (State Land & Investment Board funding) -- referred

Transfer of Ownership and Transfer of Location for a retail liquor license application filed with
the City Clerk's Office for Safeway Stores 46, Inc. d/b/a Safeway Store #2975, 305 Cole Shopping
Center, Cheyenne, Wyoming (License currently held by  Lambousis Revocable Trust, 112 W. 17 th
Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming).  Carol I ntlekofer, City Clerk, advised written notification had bee n
received by the applicant that the application is being withdrawn.  U pon inquiry with no objection
raised by members of City Council, Mayor Kaysen announced the item was being withdrawn from the
agenda at the request of the applicant.
Appointment of Dave Ohde to the Dow ntown Development Authority Board.  Mr. Pierson
moved to approve , seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Motion car ried.  Vot ing “yes” -- all members of the
governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointment).
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held October 8, 2012 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers, 2101 O’N eil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming for a tra nsfer of ownership for a
restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for Judi’s Cajun Kitchen, LLC
d/b/a Judi’s Cajun Kitchen, 620 Central Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming (License currently held by Real
Mexican Food, Inc., 620 Central Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming).
Under other business:
Eric Miller questioned the outer diameter of the traffic circle proposed for the roundabout to
be constructed at the Pershing/Converse intersection pursuant to his inquiry of same at the Sept. 10th
City Council meeting .  Nathan B eauheim, Assist ant City  Eng ineer, offe red his apolog y for not
providing a re sponse since the last meeting and advise d he would fur nish Mr. Miller with the
information.  Mr. Miller submitted a handout depicting a picture of the Little Ferry (New Jersey) traffic
circle and expressed safety concerns for pedestrians crossing traffic/roundabout circles, commenting
on his belief that the Pershing/Converse intersection could be better controlled by traffic lights.  Mr.
Miller also questioned the City’s adoption of  the International safety codes and stated that the word
“international” to him means affiliation with the U.N. (i.e. United Nations).  Mayor Kaysen advised
that City representatives attend code conferences and provide recommendations on safety codes to be
used, and that the International codes are utilized by several municipalities across the country.
 
Barbie Harrington commented on the following topics: Downtown Development Authority’s
proposed plans to relocate their downtown office and remodeling costs; meaning of eminent domain
and the judicial process relative to the City’s responsibility; repayment to Cheyenne Light, Fuel and
Power (CLFP) customers due to recent awareness of the company’s accounting error involving billing
of the City’s franchise fee and use of taxpayers’ money, and recent street construction project near the
Pershing/Snyder intersection relative to project pre-notification to businesses in the vicinity, reduced
on-street parking  availability , and alleged substandar d asphalt plac ed by  the contra ctor.  B rad
Harrington posed a question to Mayor Kaysen regarding the timing of his knowledge of the CLF&P
franchise billing error.  Following verification of the content of the question, Mayor Kaysen stated he
did not have knowledg e of the  matter until a fe w days prior to an ar ticle being published in the
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle newspaper by way of a c ounty resident presenting it t o him in an email
format.  Upon inquiry  by a Council member r elative to the Per shing/Snyder street project, Nathan
Beauheim, Assistant City Engineer, advised that WYDOT administered the construction project and
provided information on WYDOT involvement regarding procedures for resolving any dispute with
the contractor that may involve faulty work and/or warranty provisions.  He noted the City, to date, has
not yet accepted the project as complete and the asphalt issue is being reviewed.
City Attorney Dan White requested the governing body meet in executive session to discuss
two litigation matters involving the City.  Mr. Collins moved to meet in executive session, seconded
by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Mayor Kaysen
announced members would meet in executive session in Council Chambers, not open to the public;
requested the City Attorney, City Clerk, and other staff as requested by the City Attorney, be present
at the executive session, and stated there would be no action taken by the governing body as a result
of the executive session, and that the regular City Council meeting would officially conclude at the
close of the executive session.  (Clerk’s Note: Executive session convened at 8:15 p.m. following a
brief recess.)
There being no further business to come bef ore the governing body prior to the executive
session, the meeting was officially adjourned at 8:48 p.m. following closure of the executive session.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 October 4, 2012

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2012-10-08 | CLIP_ID: 335 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 335

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 October 8, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne (arriving at 6:01 pm), Mr. Spiker
and Mr. Valdez.  Absent: Mr. Pierson.  Also present: Kristy Anderson, Deputy City Clerk; Lois Huff,
City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney. 
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Collins moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Allen.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 24, 2012.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez moved to approve pa yment of vouche rs as pre sented, seconded by Mr. Allen.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
Public Hearing for a transfer of ownership for a restaurant liquor license application filed with
the City Clerk's Office for Judi’s Cajun Kitchen, LLC d/b/a Judi’s Cajun Kitchen, 620 Central Avenue,
Cheyenne, Wy oming ( License c urrently h eld b y Re al M exican F ood, I nc., 6 20 Ce ntral A venue,
Cheyenne, Wyoming).  Kristy Anderson, Deputy City Clerk, stated wr itten notification had bee n
received by the applicant advising that the application is being withdrawn at this time and, therefore,
there is no longer a purpose to conduct the public hearing.  Upon inquiry with no objection raised by
members of City Council, Mayor Kaysen announced the public hearing was being cancelled.
ORDINANCE - Vacating a Portion of the Right-of-Way of Bradley Avenue at the Intersection
of Bradley Avenue and East 14th Street.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash moved
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present. (#3958)
ORDINANCE - Pursua nt to Section 2.2.1 Zoning  Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2 Zoning
Districts Esta blished, and Section 5.1.3 Officia l Z oning Ma p of the Unifie d Deve lopment Code,
changing the zoning classification from MR-1 Medium-Density Residential Established District to
MUB Mix ed-Use Business Emphasis District for Bloc k 16, Park Addition, Chey enne, Wyoming
(located between Richardson and Cosgriff Courts and between Ames Court and Dey Avenue).  Ms. Ash
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Ms. Ash moved to amend by
substitute, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  It was noted the purpose for the substitute is to include standard
language indicating the zone change will not take effect until the final plat is approved.  Motion to
amend carried.  Voting  “yes” -- all member s of the g overning body present.  Comm ents followed
regarding concerns with approving spot zoning wherein most of the area surrounding the property is
zoned residential; support for the  zone chang e, and ty pes of use s for the c urrently vacant lot and
building that aren’t allowed under the current zone classification.  Motion, as amended, carried.  Voting
“yes”-- all members of the governing body present. (#3959)
ORDINANCE - Pursua nt to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2 Z oning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Of ficial Z oning Ma p of the Unified De velopment Code,
changing the  Z oning Cla ssification f rom MU B Mixe d-Use B usiness Emphasis Di strict to  CB
Community Business District f or L ots 12-22 , Block 656, Or iginal Cit y (AKA  South C heyenne
Addition), Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at 504 Central Avenue).  Ms. Ash moved to approve on third
and final reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carr ied.  Voting  “yes”-- all member s of the
governing body present. (#3960)
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to S ection 2.2.1 Z oning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2 Z oning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Of ficial Z oning Ma p of the Unifie d De velopment Code,
changing the  Z oning Cla ssification f rom MU B Mixe d-Use Business Emphasis Di strict to  CB
Community Business District for a Portion of Lot 2, Block 3, Plaza Del Range, Cheyenne, Wyoming
(located at 3308 Dell Range Boulevard).  M s. Ash moved to a pprove on third and final reading,
seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body present.
(#3961)
ORDINANCE - Creating Section 6.08.180  Feeding Geese and Ducks Prohibited, of Chapter
6.08, Animal Care and Control Regulations Generally, of Title 6, Animals, of the Code of the City of
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Shirley Stamm, spoke in opposition to the proposed ordinance noting concerns
with the proposed penalty provision for violations.  She provided a handout of pictures (on file with
City Clerk’s office) depicting a park in North Platte, NE that contains resident geese and commented
that officials there have placed swans in the park to assist with cutting down the geese population.  Ms.
Stamm voiced her suggestions on dealing with the number of geese in the community.  Marilee Manalo
also expressed her opposition t o the proposed or dinance; noted community benefits geese provide,
offered alternative suggestions to dealing with geese, and recommended additional research be done on
the topic prior to acting on the ordinance.  Jan Nelson-Schroll also stated her belief that more research
should be conducted on alternatives specific to parks, encouraged the park area be cleaned on a regular
basis, and spoke in opposition to the proposed $750 fine for violations.  Ms. Ash moved to approve on
second reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Ms. Ash moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Ms.
Broyles.  Discussion followed on the proposed fine amount and jail time contained in the proposed
substitute, and having those provisions in place to “mainstream” all fines and fees within City Code.
Motion to amend failed.  Voting  “yes”-- Mr. Allen a nd Ms. Broyles.  Voting “no” -- Ms. Ash, Mr.
Brown, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Upon inquiry, Todd Feezer,
Assistant Director, Parks and Recreation Department, provided information on alternative methods to
control the geese population such as e gg addling, landscape changes, hazing, repellent and ca nine
control.  He explained that most geese problems take place in manicured areas of parks and advised they
are planning to chang e some of the  landscaping elements to make it less attrac tive for g eese.  He
advised r epellent h as b een u sed w ith li ttle n oticeable d ifference; h azing me thods a re e xpensive
alternatives, and that egg addling has been the most successful method previously used.  Mr. Feezer
stated that 80% of geese that are relocated will find their way back to their native park; that curtailing
the population is the goal, not harming the existing population, and that the first step is to quit feeding
the geese.  Discussion continued on alternative methods to controlling the geese population including
down sides to applying repellent and adding swans that can be aggressive and dangerous.  Upon inquiry,
Dan White, City Attorney, advised he was not aware if an ordinance such as the proposed one has to
have a fine attached to it.  Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
present with the exception of Ms. Broyles and Mr. Valdez voting “no”.   Under other business, Ms.
Nelson-Schroll asked for  clarification on the purpose  of halting the feeding of g eese.  Mr. Fe ezer
explained that the program would be designed to control the future population of geese and the existing
number is tolerable if they would migrate when and where they should. Ms. Stamm suggested relocating
existing signage (re: Holliday Park), which states not to feed the ducks and geese, to a lower level where
it can be seen by more people.

Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held October 22, 2012 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to determine whether compliance of
annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land being situated in a

of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located approximately 150 feet west of the intersection
of Converse Avenue and Ogden Road).

adjourned at 6:56 p.m.

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2012-10-22 | CLIP_ID: 337 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 337

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 October 22, 2012
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne,
Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer,
and Dan White, City Attorney.  Members of Boy Scout Troop 116 led the recitation of the pledge
of allegiance.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)   Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Allen and Mr. Collins agenda item #21(b) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Collins
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 8, 2012.  M otion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez each declared a conflict of interest (business interest in vouchers
payable to Amer i-Tech HVAC Service s and Radio Station KYOY F M respectively) and va cated
Council Chambers.  Mr. Pierson moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by
Mr. Spiker.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council
Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez returned to Council
Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing  to determine w hether compliance of anne xation
conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land being situated in a portion of
the East Half of the East Half (E½E½) of Section 20, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th
P.M., L aramie County , Wy oming ( located a pproximately 150 fee t west of the inter section of
Converse Avenue and Ogden Road).  Jim Flesher, Urban Planning Office, reviewed requirements and
compliance of conditions contained in Wy o. Stat. §15-1-402, including spe cific infor mation
indicating compliance with Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 (a)(i) through (vi).  Ed Murray, Murray Properties,
adjacent l and owner and c oncerned c itizen a dvised h e ha d n o p roblem w ith the  a nnexation in
principle or the requirements of state statute for compliance, however, he did have concerns with the
proposed road configuration depicted in Exhibit D, Road Network for Section 20, submitted with the
annexation documents.  He state d the proposed configuration contemplates significant deviations
from the master transportation plan and is not in compliance with the long range transportation plans
as outlined in the Unified Development Code (UDC) under Section 4.3.2 (City Clerk’s note: copies
of the UDC section were submitted by Mr. Murray and is on file with the City Clerk’s Office).  Mr.
Murray expressed his concerns with the road configuration and questioned the timing of the submittal
of the transportation network plan, commenting on his belief it should be submitted as part of the
preliminary and/or final plat documents and not with the annexation paperwork.  Kim Rindlisbacher,
Scenic Development, Inc., provided brief comments on the project Scenic is planning on developing
on the property, if annexed.  He advised the project proposed is an upscale apartment community and
stated the project will provide an amenity for the community, noting their development area will be
conducive to the street plans outlined in PlanCheyenne.  Comments followed with regard to having
a ma ster plan for development growth, including roads, of the area in place for review prior to
approval of the annexation request.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the
hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.
ORDINANCE - Creating Section 6.08.180  Feeding Geese and Ducks Prohibited, of Chapter
6.08, Animal Care and Control Regulations Generally, of Title 6, Animals, of the Code of the City
of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash moved to approve on
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the
governing body with the exception of Mr. Pierson and Mr. Valdez voting “no”. (#3962)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne is in support
of the Town of  Albin’s projec ts to be submitt ed to the State L oan and I nvestment Board for the
December 2012 County wide Consensus Bloc k Gra nt funding  in the total amount of $115,000.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5436)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne is in support
of the Town of Pine Bluffs project to be submitted to the State Loan and Investment Board for the
December 2012 Countywide Consensus Block Grant funding in the amount of $26,000.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried. (#5437) 
[CA] RESOLUTION - Re- appropriating the Fiscal Year 2013 budg et of the City  of C heyenne
according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 and 16-4-113.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
(#5438)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Designating the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority (“DDA”) as
the local Main Street Organization for the implementation of the Wyoming Main Street Program;
authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to join with the DDA in the submission of a Wyoming Main
Street-Certified Track Application to the Wyoming Business Council; and designating the Executive
Director of the DDA as the Program Director for the Wyoming Main Street Program in the City of
Cheyenne.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5439)
RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to enter into an agreement to purchase
537 square feet of land l ocated at the southeast corner of the interse ction of East 19th Street a nd
Converse Avenue, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsor
-- Mr. Brown).  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown advised the item had been discussed
under other items for the agenda at Finance Committee and provisions of Title 2 of City Code had
been met.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City
Attorney, advised the agreement is the result of discussions with the Cole family and would provide
the City  with acquisition of land to be af filiated with the City ’s rounda bout project
(Converse/Pershing/19th  intersection).  He noted it is desired that the transaction proceed at this time
as the Cole family will be closing on their property sale soon involving the Safeway Store site.  Eric
Miller ques tioned the size of the proposed roundabout, noting he had received an email per his
previous request but would like a response “on the record.”  Mayor Kaysen read a portion of the email
that was sent to Mr. Mi ller from Nathan Beauheim, Assistant City  Engineer, that stated “ ... the
outside diameter of the proposed roundabout is about 230 feet, though this varies a little bit as the
roundabout isn’t a pe rfect circ le...”.  Upon f urther inquiry, Mr. W hite stated the la nd is being
purchased from the Cole Corporation, and Mayor Kaysen and Frank Cole noted that a property
appraisal was done with the land valued at $5,350.00 and that the agreed upon amount for purchase
of the land is $5,000.00.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5440)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne has agreed on
the Town of Burns substitution project to be submitted to the State Loan and Investment Board for
the 2011-2012 Count ywide Consensus B lock Gra nt funding  -- r eferred to Financ e Committ ee
(sponsor -- Mr. Collins).
[CA] RESOLUTION - Supporting the City of Cheyenne’s application for a Land an d W ater
Conservation Fund Grant for the Ma rtin Luther King, Jr. Park Quickstart Tennis Court Project --
referred to Finance Committee (sponsors -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Valdez).
[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to approve and accept the FY2012 Grants
to encourage arrest policies and enforcement of Protection Orders Program from the U.S. Department
of Justice Office on Violence Against Women in the amount of $300,000.00 (Award No. 2012-WE-
AX-0058) -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsor -- Mr. Brown).
[CA] RESOLUTION - Determining conditions for annexation required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402
exist for a pa rcel of land being  situated in a portion of the Ea st Half of the  East Half (E½E½) of
Section 20, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located
approximately 150 feet west of the intersection of Converse Avenue and Ogden Road) -- referred to
Public Services Committee (sponsor -- Ms. Ash).
[CA] RESOLUTION - A uthorizing the Gover ning Body of the City of Chey enne to a dopt the
revised statement of investment policy -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsors -- Mr. Collins and
Mr. Brown).
[CA] Contract between the City  of Chey enne, Transit Division, and Wy oming Department of
Health, Aging Division, to provide supportive service s to persons 60 y ears or older ( Title III-B
Program Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5919)    
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Parks and Recreation Department, and Cheyenne
Capitals Youth Hockey Association, for 2012 - 2013 youth hockey season at the City owned Ice and
Events Center.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5920) 
Ratification of e xisting Contract No. 5801 betwee n the City of C heyenne and A ssociated
Legal Group, LLC, to provide funding for additional legal services as needed for the acquisition of
property for the East Pershing Boulevard Reconstruction Phase IV Project, as provided in Section 6.E.
of said contr act.  Mr. B rown reported the re was no recommendation on the item from F inance
Committee.  Mr. Brown  moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City
Attorney, explained, pursuant to initiation of the City’s eminent domain action, the existing contract
provides for outside legal counsel who has extensive knowledge and experience in the area of eminent
domain.  He stated there is a clause in the agreement requiring governing body approval if the amount
for services exceeded $35,000.00.  Mr. White advised they  are continuing negotiations with t he
owners of the Hollywood Video property site and need to continue with the legal services being
provided due to an upcoming hearing and conclusion of the matter, which will exceed the $35,000.00
amount.  Comm ents followed in support of c ontinuing with the outside leg al services, as well as
comment in opposition to taking property generally.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- Ms. Ash, Mr.
Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr.  Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr.  Allen, Mr .
Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez. (#5801) 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and RBC Global Asset Management (U.S.) Inc. to
provide investment advisory  services (Solid W aste, 2007-2010, 2011- 2014 1% Sales Ta x & 1%
Specific Purpose Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Contract Modification No. 2 to Contract No. 5432 between the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation
Division, and A-1 Organics for resources and services at the public Composting Facility.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried. (#5432)
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to  Contract No. 5511 between the City of Cheyenne, Public
Works Department, and TriHy dro Corporation for environmental monitoring a t the Happy  Jack
Landfill.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $209,220.00). (#5511)
[CA] Second Amendment to Contract #4966 between the City of Cheyenne and Saddle Ridge, LLC,
pertaining to Saddle Ridge  Subdivis ion gr eenway sy stem improvements -- r eferred to Financ e
Committee. 
[CA] Consideration of B id #M-4-13 for  a contr act between the City of Chey enne, Engineering
Department, and Reiman Corpor ation, Chey enne, Wy oming, f or t he Ea st Pershing B oulevard
Reconstruction Phase IV Project (1% Sales Tax 2007-2010, State and Local Grants, Water and Sewer
Enterprise funds, Miscellaneous Federal Grants).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not
to exceed $3,651,937.00). (#5921)
Consideration of B id #M-6-13 for  a contr act be tween the  City  of Chey enne, Par ks and
Recreation Depa rtment, and Simon Cont ractors, I nc., Chey enne, Wy oming, f or the Chey enne
Greenway Walterscheid Underpass Project (1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use  Tax, Miscellaneous
Federal Grants).  Mr. Brown moved to a ccept the bid in an amount not to ex ceed $785,662.50,
seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Comments followed regarding work previously provided by the contractor
and level of confidence involving “a good product” being received for this project.  Doug Vetter, City
Engineer, explained the bid was the lowest, qualified responsive bidder and provided information on
the Pershing/Snyder project involving the contractor, WYDOT and the City, noting the project is still
unfinished as punch  list items have to be  corrected prior to finalizing the pr oject.  He  advised
WYDOT and the contractor have reached an agreement with regard to some corrections.  Discussion
included the following topics: Previous project concerns relative to quality of services provided by
the contractor; compaction problems; quality of road construction and life of streets; aggregate and
concrete products and proble ms (i.e. alk ali silica reactivity a/k/a ASR); City  specifications and
requirements, and on-site inspections during street projects.  During discussion Mr. Vetter provided
information on compaction problems that need to be watched during paving projects; Engineering
Department’s approach of providing provisions for mix designs to best suit project needs; nature of
the underpass project that did not require special provisions being specified; regional problems with
ASR involving aggregate, and concrete mix elements.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Brown,
Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” --  Mr. Allen, Ms.
Ash, Mr. Collins and Dr. Rinne.  Mayor Kaysen requested inspectors monitor the job closely due to
concerns raised during discussion. (#5922)
Authorization to purchase Utility Rocket 5GHZ quadband communication equipment in the
amount of $167,999.72 fr om CDW - G for  th e Cheyenne Police De partment (SLIB Funding) --
referred to Finance Committee.  Mr. Brown advised the item was discussed under other items for the
agenda at Finance Committee and provisions of Title 2 had been met.  Mr. Brown moved to approve
the purchase from CDW-G of Vernon Hills, IL in an amount not to exceed $167,999.72, seconded
by Mr. Valdez. Captain Mitch Maxwell, Cheyenne Police Department, advised they had recently
received information that a similar product may exist and requested a 2 week postponement to allow
for further evaluation.  Upon inqui ry, Captain Maxwell ex plained the c ommunication equipment
consists of  a box that goes in the trunk of the vehicle and connects to and interfaces with the in-
vehicle computer and camera system, which allows officers to run drivers licenses, etc. in the field.
He explained it will increase the download speed for video transference, boost the cell signal, help
eliminate communication dead zones, and has GPS tracking capability.  Victor Sicone, Buffalo Ridge,
questioned how many “spares” would be provided and how long the equipment would last bef ore
becoming obsolete and has to be replaced.  Mr. Maxwell advised they have allocated funding to equip
all patrol vehicles and spare vehicles and that the average life of the utility rockets is 5-7 years.  Mr.
Brown moved to postpone to Nov. 13, 2012, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Concern was expressed that
Finance Committee members were requested to review the item under other items for the agenda due
to t imeliness i ssues in volving ins tallation o f v ehicle e quipment, a nd sta ff’s resulting request t o
postponement.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. 
Authorization to purchase a used Ice Surface Groomer, 2004 Olympian Model 2000, in the
amount of $55,000.00 from Big Hill Services Ltd., Cochrane, AB, Canada, for the City owned  Ice
and Events Center -- referred to Finance Committee.  Mr. B rown advised the item was discusse d
under other items for the agenda at Finance Committee and provisions of Title 2 had been met.  Mr.
Brown moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $55,000.00, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Rick
Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, provided information on recent equipment problems with the
current 1996 ice groomer; problems in acquiring a temporary unit; need for a backup unit to fulfill
contractual obligations to users of the Ice and Events Center for hockey activities; number of daily
times the ice is g roomed; other  ice centers having  2 to 3 gr oomers, cost of a  new g roomer
($100,000.00) and advised purchase of the 2004 model would include a 2 year warranty.  He stated,
if approved, the new unit could be received prior to the next governing body meeting, noting he may
request a counter check be issued due to timing of the delivery, and indicated the current ice groomer
will be kept operational as a backup with regular inspections.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body. 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held November 13, 2012 at 6:00 p.m. in
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a  transfer of ownership for
a re tail liquor license applicat ion filed with the City  Clerk' s Offic e for  L os Abuelos Mexican
Restaurant, LLC d/b/a Los Abuelos Mexican Restaurant, 1601 Central Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.
(License currently held by One East Sixteenth LLC, 1601 Central Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming). 
Announcement was made of the Appointments of Robert Mathia and Melissa Leger to the
Downtown Development Authority Board to be confirmed November 13, 2012.
Announcement was made that due to the Veterans Day Holiday on Monday, November 12,
2012, the regularly scheduled meeting of the Governing Body will instead be held on Tuesday,
November 13, 2012 beginning at 6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers, Municipal B uilding, 2101
O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.      
Under other business:
 
Barbie H arrington e xpressed c oncerns r egarding th e q uality o f w ork d one b y Simon
Contractors and a recent street project (Pershing/Snyder Ave. area).  She also questioned the source
of funding for the office relocation of the Downtown Development Authority (DDA) and whether the
money the  DD A i s u sing fo r c osts i nvolving th e move might b e un constitutional pu rsuant t o
provisions of the Wyoming Constitution.  Mayor Kaysen advised the funding for the DDA move is
a loan through a joint powers board and not directly from the City.  It was noted that the joint powers
board involved was scheduled to meet the next day (Oct. 23 ) at 7:00 a.m. at the Align Building offrd
of Airport Parkway. 
Eric Miller commented on the quality of concrete pours for the Pershing/Snyder project, as
previously referenced by Ms. Harrington, and suggested core tests be done with inspections for these
types of projects.
Brad Harrington advised he had done research on the United Nation’s Agenda 21 document
and the City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) and had information regarding this that he would
like to present to the governing body, noting it would take him some time to review general Agenda
21 information as well as facts and details.  Upon inquiry, he explained the purpose of providing the
information is to demonstrate that codes of the City are connected with Agenda 21.  Mayor Kaysen
suggested Mr. H arrington distribute the infor mation packets he had pre pared to members of the
governing body to give them an opportunity  to review the research.  Mayor Kaysen stated if Mr .
Harrington so desired he  would make himsel f available for f urther discussion on the topic.  Mr.
Harrington submitted copies of the information packets (titled “On Agenda 21: The United Nations’
Blueprint for Collectivization and Enslavement, And on How  It’s Being Implemented on a Local
Level Right Here in Cheyenne, Wyoming”) to governing body members (document on file with City
Clerk’s Office).  Comment was  made that perhaps the topic could be r eviewed at a public wor k
session.  Mayor Kaysen noted that the UDC is undergoing its first annual review for presentation to
the governing body for any changes and, as part of the review process, there will be the opportunity
for public involvement.
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 7:17 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2012-11-13 | CLIP_ID: 340 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 340

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 November 13, 2012
Due to the V eterans Day holiday, the Gover ning Body of the City  of Chey enne instead
convened in regular session on this date beginning at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:
MAYOR  -  Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS - M r. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms.
Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. P ierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker a nd Mr. Valde z.  Also present: Carol
Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.

RESOLUTION - Determining conditions for annexation required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402
exist for a parc el of land being situated in a portion of the Ea st Half of the  East Half (E½E½) of
Section 20, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located
approximately 150 feet west of the intersection of Converse Avenue and Ogden Road).  Ed Murray,
speaking on behalf of Murray Properties, property owner in the vicinity, referenced comments of
concern he had voiced at the Oct. 22 City Council meeting, and submitted and discussed 3 hand-outs
(on file with City Clerk’s office) depicting property involving the proposed annexed area and near
vicinity (1. Enlargement of PlanCheyenne Land Use Map; 2.  Map of area including parcel owned
by Murray Properties; 3. Exhibit D (map) as submitted with the Petition for Annexation).  Mr. Murray
expressed concerns with inconsistencies in the road network proposed in the maps submitted with the
Petition for Annexation relative to the land use map adopted with PlanCheyenne, and explained that
the proposed road network submitted with the annexation request would de prive his company of
being able to utilize their property for future use as identified in PlanCheyenne and pursuant to zoning
classification.  He indicated an orderly and coordinated transportation plan must be fac ilitated as
contemplated in PlanCheyenne, and in conjunction with easements acquired during the Storey Blvd.
extension project and including collector/connecting streets to allow access.  He further commented
that the issue involving the road network plan, as proposed as part of the annexation packet, should
be a ddressed th rough th e pla tting process and no t be  c onsidered a s a n a pproved r oad p lan in
conjunction with determining that annexation conditions had been met.  Frank Cole, property owner
of the pa rcel proposed for  annexation as well as land in the vicinity , referenced and discussed a
written agreement of record the Cole family has with the City that laid out accesses off Storey and
Converse and water “stub-outs”, and stated that, officially, the PlanCheyenne map has no bearing.
Reporting fo r P ublic Services Co mmittee, M s. As h mo ved to  a dopt, se conded b y Ms.  Broyles.
Comment was made concerning discussion on the resolution at the last Public Services Committee
meeting relative to communication between the property owner, developer, staff and those currently
working on an update  to the PlanChey enne do cument.  Upon inquiry  regarding the proc ess for
updating the Master  Transpor tation Plan, Matt Ashby , Planning Ser vices Dire ctor, a dvised an
amendment to the tra nsportation plan would involve a public he aring befor e the Planning
Commission and require a resolution adopted by the governing body, noting public input is allowed
throughout the process.  Upon further inquiry, Mr. Ashby stated that, at this point, Exhibit D is just
a “figure” submitted with the Petition for Annexation and that during the platting process discussions
would be held regarding the road network plan.  Brad Emmons, AVI, agent for Scenic Development,
Inc. who is planning a development project on the proposed annexed parcel, advised they are trying
to ensure they conduct the right traffic study to accompany the platting process relative to a collector
vs. local road, and expressed they feel they can separate the long range master plan from the proposed
development action.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Emmons stated that depictions in Exhibit D matches existing
curb cuts and stub-outs, and commented that Exhibit D labels the road in question as E. Carlson Street
but that st reet name is f or reference only and not necessarily the final street name.  Comme nts
followed regarding request for postponement of the item, as previously received by AVI, which would
have allowed time for further information to be acquired vs. adoption of the resolution; frustration
and concerns with incr emental annexations/additions relative to the road network system; recent
detention pond construction near East Carlson Street and land in the area being located in the county
and not within city  limits, and ar ea street network addressing future growth for the community’s
health, safety and welfare.  Mr. Emmons explained the purpose of the request to postpone was to
provide for the annexation ordinance to coincide with timing of re-zoning and preliminary and final
plat processes, and advised of the options available regarding postponement of the resolution vs.
delaying  the introduction of the annexation ordinance.  He verified preference for adoption of the
resolution at the pre sent meeting ; stated timing is critica l f or Scenic De velopment, re garding
resolution approval, to enable them to move forward with project financing, and noted delaying
ordinance introduction would provide time to work on the road network issue.  Mr. Cole provided
a brief history on certain road alignments and configurations in the area, including accesses on Storey
and Con verse a nd distance be tween a ccess p oints, a nd c ommented o n to pography pr oblems,
infrastructure, Cole family working with the City for numerous years on many developments, and the
recent detention area.  Discussion topics included: Location of the detention area (within Section 21);
road network system and effect on future area development as well as Murray Properties parcel;
whether adoption of the resolution legally mandates approval of the future annexation ordinance that
will be sub mitted, and effect of any changes in conditions that may occur following resolution
adoption.  Upon inquiry regarding whether adoption of the resolution compels a favorable vote on
the ensuing annexation ordinance, Dan White, City Attorney, stated that pursuant to comments that
have been made a situation is developing whereby he needs to advise the governing body that he may
have a conflict of interest (family related) involving a concerned party to the discussions and that the
question should be refer red to another staff a ttorney or outside counse l, and indicated he  was
declining to render any legal advice on the question posed at this time.  Upon a hypothetically phrased
general question with regard to whether the governing body adopts a resolution determining that an
area meets all six conditions for annexation, is the governing body then compelled to vote for the
related annexation ordinance, Mayor Kaysen requested Mr. White reply to the inquiry on a process
basis only.  Mr. White advised it would be helpful to conduct legal research on the topic, and stated
his initial impression is if the governing body adopts a resolution that finds that the Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-
402(a) annexation criteria have been met, the governing body could not later make a different finding
in the absence of a significant change of circumstances that would provide a substantive basis for the
change.  Comments followed and included: State statute wording regarding action on anne xation
ordinances; past actions on annexation ordinances following resolution adoptions determining that
conditions had been met; further legal review, and reason for postponing action at some point in the
annexation process to coincide with re-zoning and platting.  Mr. Allen moved to amend, seconded
by Mr. Collins, by adding the following language within the proposed resolution:  “The approval of
this resolution is not intended to and shall not be construed as modifying any of the components of
the Cheyenne Area Master Transportation Plan contained within PlanCheyenne, adopted in November
2006”.   M r. Cole spoke in o pposition to t he amendment, commenting that if the amendment is
approved the same wording should be included in other future annexation related resolutions.  Mr.
Murray spoke in support of the amendment, commenting that any transportation network plan should
comply with long range transportation plans adopted by the City and that the area is a very important
part of the  community and its g rowth.  Discussion followed on the per centage of the  area being
discussed vs. percentage of the proposed annexation property; use of land by property owners relative
to City and County boundaries; support for planning efforts, and whether resolution adoption would
be a commitment to the road plan depicted in Exhibit D.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Ashby expressed his
belief that definitive action on a road plan takes place during the platting process and that the master
(transportation) pla n is  int ended to  pr ovide fo r h aving a  ro ad n etwork in  pla ce a nd se rves a s a
guideline for open discussion to meet transportation needs.  Mr. Murray commented that the
amendment would pr ovide a history  to those new membe rs of the g overning body who will be
addressing the annexation or dinance next y ear.  Further comments included the topics of ro ad
network configuration; property owners’ competing interests, and potential changes to PlanCheyenne
due to the current review being conducted.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr.
Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “ no” -- Ms.
Ash, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Spiker.  Discussion on the main motion, as amended, followed and included
the following topics:  Condit ions/criteria being met for annexation vs. Exhibit D as a stree t issue,
overall area development for best interests of the community, and school district student capacity and
communication with the school district with regard to the development (apartment complex) planned
for t he parcel proposed to be  annexed.  Mr. Collins noted, for the re cord, that he hoped if the
governing body changes its mind in the future (City Clerk note: Regarding resolution adoption and
future annexation ordinance action) that it will not be considered to be arbitrary or capricious as the
discussion record reflects governing body’s concerns about the roads in the area and that an update
to the Master Transportation Plan may be made to r eflect area arterial and collector roads.  A lso
during discussion, Mr. Emmons again reviewed  postponement options to align with the platting time
frame, advising the goal is to start development proje ct construction next spring.  Main motion as
amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne
voting “no”. (#5444)  Following announcement of the vote, Mayor Kaysen requested all interested
parties continue to look at the road networking, safety and topography issues to resolve.

(#5448)
 RESOLUTION - Directing the Solid Waste Division to provide curbside collection services
for automated containers for all residential addresses in the city, except for certain addresses where
curbside collection is not feasible.  Speaking in opposition to the proposed resolution were:  Matthew
Potter, Frank Cole, Mike Luna, Trish Becklinger, Randy Byers, Paula Gordinier, John Rogers, Mike
Hand (submitted petition - on file with City Clerk’s office), Dennis Auker, Kenneth Barrow, Brad
Emmons (submitted photos - on file with City Clerk’s office), Don Erickson and Jan Nelson-Schroll.
The following conce rns wer e ra ised:  hardship on re sidents involving lack of  acc ess to move
containers from bac k to front of re sidence; lack of stora ge areas in fr ont of reside nces; need for
implementation of rules re garding placement of garbage cans; refuse debris due to wind;  nar row
street widths in some areas and sanitation truck operators maneuvering around parked cars and motor
homes, and traffic congestion; addressing pick-up concerns relative to overgrown trees in alley ways
obstructing pathways of trucks; home owners responsibility for trimming trees in alleys; definition
of “feasibility” in relationship to exemptions; containers left in the street if resident is out of town;
containers in streets detra cting from community  bea utification; z oning a nd UDC (Unifie d
Development Code) re quirements which ma y conflict with the re solution, and issues with heavy
traffic and highly dense areas involving parked cars on streets.  Upon inquiry, Jim Elias, Public Works
Director, advised the Solid Waste Director is still reviewing properties eligible for exemptions so the
list is not all inclusive at this time.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash stated there
was no recommendation on the item from the committee.  Ms. Ash moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.
Pierson.  Discussion foll owed on the list of exempted properties, stre ets that should be on the
exempted list, and concerns with the impact on traffic patterns on busy streets such as Dell Range and
Pershing.  Upon inquiry, Dennis Pino, Director of Solid Waste, reviewed houses located on Dell
Range and Pershing Blvd. and advised they are still reviewing areas for exemptions, noting there will
still be some areas that will continue to have alley pick-ups.  In response to query on what benefits
the resolution will provide, Mr. Elias noted there would be an estimated 25% savings in fuel, future
equipment would be significantly less expensive as they could purchase single-arm vehicles instead
of dual-arm vehicles, alley maintenance would decrease, route efficiency would be realized, and a
decrease in insurance costs would be anticipated as trucks won’t have to maneuver in alleys, which
is where the majority of sanitation-related accidents occur.  Mr. Elias further added the department
is looking at getting GPS software packages for the trucks, which will assist with providing better
pick-up routes.  Mr. Pino explained incidents resulting in damages occur fairly regularly involving
alley trees and tree  branches.  He  explained depar tment efforts involving container pick-ups for
elderly or handicapped residents, who will remain exempted under the resolution.  Further discussion
continued on issues with Sanitation t rucks driving down alleys, including the cost inefficiency of
trucks running  both alley  and stree t routes, and the ne cessity to identify  additional  areas for
exemption.  Upon inquiry regarding a procedural question, Dan White, City Attorney, advised the
motion on the floor to adopt the resolution is now the “property” of the governing body and can only
be withdrawn with unanimous approval of the council.  Mr. Brown moved to call for the question (to
limit debate), seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mr. White clarified the motion was not debatable.  Motion
to call for the question to cease debate carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
with the exception of Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  Main motion to adopt
failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Ash voting “yes”.
Under other City business, it was suggested Public Works/Sanitation staff bring the resolution back
to the governing body after compiling a complete list of all exempt properties.

Announcement was made  of the a ppointments of Ty ler B edwell, Sav annah Brown, J on
Chenchar, Alex Cole, Kadi Davis, I an Dome, Juliette Givhan, Alex Far kas, Addie Fleming, John
Matsen, Margarita Medrano, Rachel Moseley and Jacob Peterson to the Mayor’s Youth Council, to
be confirmed December 10, 2012.
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, December 10, 2012 at 6:00
p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a Bar and Grill liquor
license application filed with the City Clerk's office for The Suite, LLC d/b/a The Suite, 1901 Central
Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 7:12 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Kristy B. Anderson, CMC
 Deputy City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

 December 6, 2012
